# Canon Events Timeline

- The drow overseers of Velkynvelve divided the prisoners into labor groups before the day began, dragging them from the slave pen and past three sneering drow at the guard post. [ch 002 / 002.01]
- Zalthir was assigned to clean and operate the outpost's lift, a creaking zurkhwood and spider-silk contraption suspended high above the cavern floor; two quaggoth guards amused themselves by dropping the basket suddenly and yanking it back up. [ch 002 / 002.01]
- Thorin, Ront, and Prince Derendil were marched to the waterfall to haul small barrels of water; Thorin assured the others, “Any feats of strength you need, I'm your guy.” [ch 002 / 002.01]
- Gyrgum was put to work in the drow dining hall preparing meals for sneering warriors lounging at circular zurkhwood tables; he joked, “I'll make sure to plate it nicely.” [ch 002 / 002.01]
- Daz, along with Stool and one other drow prisoner, was assigned to clean chamber pots, which gave him an opportunity to observe the lower levels of the outpost and the underground river flowing from the pool below. [ch 002 / 002.01]
- While being marched through the outpost, the prisoners took in the confusing vertical layout of Velkynvelve—hanging chambers, webbing, rope bridges, the lower pool, the river tunnel, and ominous creatures below—and checked their temptation to attack the guards as a total-party-kill. [ch 002 / 002.01]
- Zalthir saw the lift consisted of a swinging arm, a hand-cranked horizontal spool, several pulleys, and a heavy woven basket supported by spider-silk cord, appearing to be the only visible way to descend safely from the barracks level. [ch 002 / 002.02]
- Zalthir discovered a mysterious list of people belonging to a nearby settlement among the rubbish removed from the lift basket. [ch 002 / 002.02]
- Jimjar enlivened the lift work by proposing wagers on passengers, stops, and who would emerge first, extending credit since the prisoners had no money: “As long as you're willing to trust me as the bookkeeper, I'm willing to go on credit.” [ch 002 / 002.02]
- Eldeth occasionally accepted Jimjar's bets, and he displayed an almost freakish ability to remember exactly how much had been wagered, won, and lost. [ch 002 / 002.02]
- Zalthir studied the lift as a target for sabotage: the spider-silk cord was extremely hard to cut, the hand-cranked zurkhwood winch could not be broken without tools, though its operation could be impeded or slowed; Daz suggested freezing part of the mechanism with Shape Water. [ch 002 / 002.02]
- Zalthir realized his fire breath changed the picture: the webbing burned, and the rope would give way to flame much more readily than to a blade; the same fire could potentially damage the wooden mechanism and the extensive webs surrounding the outpost. [ch 002 / 002.02]
- Eldeth found a comb, potentially valuable in the filthy conditions of the slave pen. [ch 002 / 002.02]
- Gyrgum attempted to talk to the drow but could not understand their Undercommon; Shuushar announced he would omit the “colorful adjectives” for the sake of his spiritual and mental balance, then enumerated the insults anyway. [ch 002 / 002.03]
- Gyrgum used performance to manipulate, combine, and dramatically sizzle the ingredients; the meal's presentation and preparation impressed the drow, who offered rare praise. [ch 002 / 002.02]
- Topsy advised that stealing should be done “carefully, quietly, and not make a big production of it,” pointedly looking at Gyrgum's massive body. [ch 002 / 002.02]
- Gyrgum's first attempt to steal a knife went badly: a drow caught him holding the knife up, but Gyrgum claimed, “I'm going to sharpen this knife,” and his performance and the goodwill from his meal persuaded the drow not to punish him. [ch 002 / 002.04]
- The drow warned through Shuushar, “don't do that. Or next time, you may find yourself missing various body parts.” [ch 002 / 002.04]
- On the following day, with six or seven drow paying close attention to Gyrgum's celebrated cooking, he asked Topsy and Turvy to steal the knives; the twins successfully brought two knives back to the prisoners, which Gyrgum sharpened. [ch 002 / 002.04]
- Thorin's water-hauling group was sent to Mistress Ilvara, the cruel high priestess who commanded Velkynvelve; they crossed a steep rope bridge into the upper level of the largest hanging tower, passing through a shrine to Lolth with a lifelike ten-foot-tall sculpted spider with eight polished-jet eyes. [ch 002 / 002.03]
- A rope ladder led down from the shrine into Ilvara's private quarters, where she sat in smug comfort alongside her favored lieutenant, Shoor Vandree, and ordered the prisoners to bring the water to her bathtub. [ch 002 / 002.03]
- When Prince Derendil declared that he should be treated with dignity, Ilvara drew a knife and made it clear that “if he wants to be dealt with, he can be dealt with,” prompting him to back down. [ch 002 / 002.03]
- Thorin noticed a treasure chest in Ilvara's chamber and another descending passage that led toward Shoor's Quarters, though the work detail was not permitted to explore it; he also sensed that something about the drow at Velkynvelve seemed “crazily off.” [ch 002 / 002.03]
- Thorin realized the waterfall would allow someone to slide safely into the pool below without suffering damage; the pool was twenty feet deep and drained into an underground river that travels for miles before spilling into unknown reaches of the Underdark. [ch 002 / 002.03]
- Back in the slave pen after the day's labor, the prisoners pooled their observations; Sarith rambled through insane tangents, Stool was delighted by everyone's cleaning activities, and Jimjar continued his obsessive betting. [ch 002 / 002.04]
- Buppido repeatedly invoked “the divine plan” without identifying the divinity behind it; Thorin and Gyrgum gradually concluded Buppido might believe he was a god. [ch 002 / 002.04]
- Buppido announced his first plan, “It's quite simple. All of you will distract the enemy while I escape through the waterfall”; challenged, he admitted he needed to consider the divine plan more carefully. [ch 002 / 002.04]
- Thorin flattered Buppido with the idea that the divine plan must have arranged for everyone to escape so they could protect him; Buppido eagerly accepted and declared, “since you have pledged your swords … your spells … your lives, at least, to protecting the divine plan, I will grant you insight into its mysteries later this evening.” [ch 002 / 002.04]
- On the second day of labor, Jorlan Duskryn, an unexpected visitor during the guard shift, quietly asked the nearest prisoner, “if I gave you a means to escape, would you take it?” [ch 002 / 002.05]
- The prisoners suspected a trap and asked why a drow would help them; Jorlan answered, “hate Ilvara, and I hate Shoor. And your escape, even your attempted escape, would ruin them.” [ch 002 / 002.05]
- Jorlan proposed leaving the slave-pen gate unlocked and creating a distraction during the changing of the guard; he revealed the armory was in the chamber above the guard post, inside the hanging guard tower directly in front of the slave pen, and suggested reaching the webs below and jumping into the pool. [ch 002 / 002.05]
- When asked what they should do after reaching the water, Jorlan replied, “that is not my problem.” [ch 002 / 002.05]
- The group debated destinations: Shuushar knew how to travel toward Darklake to the west, Menzoberranzan lay to the north and was emphatically ruled out, and Gracklstugh, a duergar city where Buppido's derro kin also live, offered a possible route to the south. [ch 002 / 002.05]
- The party debated whether to flee immediately or risk retrieving weapons first; Gyrgum and Daz reasoned that the highest-ranking drow would traditionally claim captured treasure, and since none of the guards appeared to be carrying the prisoners' belongings, they concluded their gear was probably in the chest Thorin had seen in Ilvara's private chambers. [ch 002 / 002.05]
- Zalthir raised the threat of the drow's large spools of spider silk that allowed rapid descent; the group planned to scout the spools and exploit Zalthir's fire breath to destroy the drow's mobility assets. [ch 002 / 002.05]
- Gyrgum summarized the sabotage idea as “light them on fire, and fling them,” and the GM summed up the goal: “Disable. Deny the enemy the weapon.” [ch 002 / 002.06]
- Several major choices remained unresolved as they prepared for the desperate flight ahead: whether to raid the armory, whether to risk entering Ilvara's quarters for their own gear, which route to take after reaching the pool, and how much of the outpost to burn during the breakout. [ch 002 / 002.06]
- The prisoners awake to find their cell door left open in the dark and miserable Underdark morning of Velkynvelve, recognizing a brief window to act [ch 003 / 003.01]
- Stool scatters rapport spores, establishing a telepathic link among the prisoners that lasts an hour and can be directed selectively [ch 003 / 003.01]
- The group debates dashing for freedom, raiding the guard tower armory, or recovering their confiscated belongings from Ilvara's quarters [ch 003 / 003.01]
- The group concludes that obtaining weapons first will give them a chance to fight their way toward their property and then out of the outpost [ch 003 / 003.01]
- Thorin expresses a personal need to recover a family memento: "I have a pennant made from a giant's tooth reminding me of family" [ch 003 / 003.01]
- The prisoners note that ordinary footsteps might pass unnoticed, but screams, conspicuous combat, or loud spellcasting would bring the outpost down upon them; the guards at the lookout watch outward rather than back toward the prison [ch 003 / 003.01]
- The group rejects sending Prince Derendil ahead because quaggoths work in the outpost but likely produce havoc and noise [ch 003 / 003.01]
- Zalthir, with the best chance of moving quietly, volunteers to approach the guard post, with Daz accompanying him; Zalthir's fallback plan is "Maybe I'll try going on my own first. If that fails, we all charge in and have fun" [ch 003 / 003.01]
- Zalthir trips as he enters the guard post and Daz's attempt to catch him kicks a can across the floor, alerting two drow guards playing cards [ch 003 / 003.01]
- The party's plan changes instantly from stealth to a charge [ch 003 / 003.01]
- Thorin rushes into the lower guard room and attempts an unarmed strike and shove against Imbros, the Drow Elite Warrior, but misses [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Imbros strikes Thorin with his short sword twice for seven points of damage each, using nonlethal force to keep the valuable prisoner alive [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Thorin invokes Second Wind retroactively as a bonus action, restoring four hit points [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Eldeth, Turvy, Jimjar, Sarith, Buppido, Topsy, Shuushar, and others disengage, squeeze past the combatants, and climb to the upper armory to arm themselves [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Prince Derendil remains in the lower room, exposes himself to an attack of opportunity, suffers five damage, and answers with two claw attacks that wound one drow [ch 003 / 003.02]
- A second drow targets the weakened Thorin, hits him for five points, and knocks him unconscious [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Thorin succeeds on his first death save but fails the next [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Zalthir attacks repeatedly with unarmed techniques — rolling two ones and a two — and refuses an opportunity to disengage ("Not my style"), failing to land a blow [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Daz targets a wounded guard with Mind Sliver, but the drow resists the Intelligence save and takes no damage [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Once armed, Sarith attacks one of his former captors, curses the guard, hits twice for fourteen damage, and kills him [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Eldeth's hand crossbow shot misses; Topsy or Turvy strikes the remaining guard for five damage; Jimjar adds another five; Buppido loudly insists he is an expert but misses [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Ront squeezes into the lower room and kills the second ordinary guard with a short sword [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Shuushar the Awakened uses a hand crossbow and strikes Imbros for five points [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Gyrgum reaches the fallen Thorin and casts Cure Wounds, restoring sixteen hit points and bringing him back to full strength, then passes Thorin a short sword [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Thorin disengages and climbs upstairs to secure armor [ch 003 / 003.02]
- Daz abandons Mind Sliver for Magic Missile, launching three glowing darts for twelve force damage, and then a second volley leaving Imbros at six hit points [ch 003 / 003.03]
- Imbros curses the revolting slaves in Undercommon and concentrates his poisoned blade on Prince Derendil, killing him [ch 003 / 003.03]
- Zalthir finally lands a hit for four points and pushes Imbros into the doorway [ch 003 / 003.03]
- Sarith is tempted to shout curses at Imbros loudly enough to alert the outpost, but Gyrgum calms him with an appeal to spirituality — "Think of your spirituality and how much better it would be if all this came out" — and Sarith replies "Never thought of that" [ch 003 / 003.03]
- Sarith Kzekarit, fueled by vengeance and newfound spiritual focus, delivers the final blow to the Drow Elite Warrior [ch 003 / 003.03]
- The survivors strip the tower, collecting short swords, hand crossbows, armor, one hundred feet of rope, and six bags of caltrops [ch 003 / 003.04]
- Thorin claims a short sword, studded leather armor, and a shield [ch 003 / 003.04]
- Gyrgum keeps the rope ("I like rope") and takes a bag of caltrops "for fun," giving up the hand crossbow he had considered [ch 003 / 003.04]
- Daz briefly takes leather armor but abandons it upon learning he has no armor proficiency [ch 003 / 003.04]
- Zalthir declines the available weapons because they would make him less effective [ch 003 / 003.04]
- The party leaves the tower better equipped but still without its original belongings, including Thorin's giant's-tooth pendant [ch 003 / 003.04]
- The party looted the three fallen drow guards at Velkynvelve, with Thorin and Gyrgum claiming the two chain shirts, Sarith taking studded leather armor, and Gyrgum taking a hand crossbow plus three poisoned bolts and a vial of poison from the elite warrior Imbros. [ch 004 / 004.01]
- Daz declined armor after learning that wearing armor without proficiency would hinder his physical abilities and prevent spellcasting. [ch 004 / 004.01]
- Gyrgum used divine magic (Cure Wounds) to restore Zalthir to full health, exhausting his spellcasting resources. [ch 004 / 004.01; ch 004 / spells]
- Three vrocks erupted over Velkynvelve, fighting among themselves and drawing the drow and quaggoths to defensive positions, leaving the prisoners unguarded. [ch 004 / 004.01]
- The party abandoned retrieving the rest of its confiscated gear, and Thorin confirmed his giant's tooth pendant had not been confiscated. [ch 004 / 004.01]
- The party declined to burn Velkynvelve at Sarith's urging, fearing it would provoke more determined drow pursuit. [ch 004 / 004.02]
- Topsy and Turvy revealed themselves as wererats, transformed into rats, and leapt down from the outpost, saying "we'll meet you downstairs." [ch 004 / 004.02; ch 004 / moments]
- The party descended through the waterfall into the pool, avoiding the giant spider-infested webs. [ch 004 / 004.02]
- A gray ooze emerged from the water and struck Thorin for twelve damage, but the party outran it after Eldeth warned its acid could consume armor. [ch 004 / 004.03]
- A wounded vrock landed beside the fleeing party; Ront refused to flee, struck it for nine damage with his great axe, and was killed by the demon's talons. [ch 004 / 004.03; ch 004 / moments]
- The vrock menaced Daz with its beak but flew off; the party fled into the Underdark tunnels. [ch 004 / 004.03]
- The party took a long rest, then learned Underdark travel paces: eight miles per day fast, six normal, four slow. [ch 004 / 004.04]
- Sarith stepped forward as navigator and appeared truthful about knowing the passages. [ch 004 / 004.04]
- The party learned of the Faerzress, a magical energy suffusing parts of the Underdark that gives dim light, resists divination, and disrupts teleportation. [ch 004 / 004.04]
- The party learned of the Darklake, a network of underground waterways over a hundred miles long, and chose Sloobludop, about eight days away, as its first destination. [ch 004 / 004.04]
- Stool attached himself to Gyrgum as his "bestest friend" and protector, and Gyrgum accepted the responsibility. [ch 004 / 004.04; ch 004 / npcs]
- The party found and ate Tongue of Madness fungus; Thorin resisted, but Daz and the others compulsively spoke their thoughts aloud for an hour. [ch 004 / 004.05]
- Daz retained some Tongue of Madness fungus in his sack for future use in potions or tricks. [ch 004 / 004.05; ch 004 / items]
- A sudden rockfall collapsed the tunnel ceiling, knocking Daz unconscious, dropping Gyrgum to zero hit points (kept conscious by Relentless Endurance), and trapping Buppido and others. [ch 004 / 004.06; ch 005 / 005.01]
- Zalthir and Thorin avoided the collapse and organized the rescue; Zalthir freed Daz first, Thorin freed Gyrgum, and Stool pulled Shuushar and Buppido to safety. [ch 004 / 004.06; ch 005 / 005.01]
- A carrion crawler erupted from the earth during the rescue; Zalthir blasted it with fire breath, the companions volleyed crossbow bolts dealing 20 damage, and the party killed it. [ch 005 / 005.02]
- Eldeth and Jimjar were dug out mid-fight at Thorin's call, "dwarves stick together." [ch 005 / 005.02; ch 005 / moments]
- The party harvested the carrion crawler carcass, yielding nine rations (about two days' food) that tasted like chicken. [ch 005 / 005.02; ch 005 / items]
- The party took a long rest, and everyone reached 2nd level; Daz took expertise in Arcana. [ch 005 / 005.03]
- About an hour into the march, Daz and Zalthir detected drow scouts from Velkynvelve trailing the party; Zalthir recalled that killing scouts would not stop the main force. [ch 005 / 005.04]
- The party fled; Thorin carried Stool Yoda-style, Stool released rapport spores for silent telepathic communication, and Zalthir found a hiding alcove. [ch 005 / 005.05]
- The party's stealth check at advantage held against DC 12, the drow scouts lost the scent and doubled back down a different corridor. [ch 005 / 005.05]
- The party traveled at fast pace for days; Sarith nearly led them astray in the Faerzress tunnels, and Gyrgum corrected the course. [ch 005 / 005.06]
- By the end of the third day, the party had a day's lead at pursuit level four; level five means the drow catch up. [ch 005 / 005.06]
- The party pressed deeper into the Underdark and on the third day entered a narrow tunnel whose walls shimmered with a soft, eerie green glow from a moss Jimjar identified as Ormu [ch 006 / 006.01].
- The group realized their food and water were dangerously low and spread out to forage, gathering ten gallons of water, enough for all ten travelers (four adventurers and six allies) for one day [ch 006 / 006.01].
- Daz used Shape Water to freeze the collected water into one-gallon blocks since the party had no containers, and refroze them every hour; the ice would melt overnight, requiring a fresh water search each morning [ch 006 / 006.01].
- Zalthir raised a rules question from last session about missing stealth checks during a chase escape; the GM confirmed "that was a mistake" and thanked him for catching it [ch 006 / 006.01].
- Food foraging gathered about seventeen pounds of food, covering the ten pounds the group needed for the day with roughly eight pounds to spare; only two allies turned up anything [ch 006 / 006.02].
- Eldeth took charge of covering the group's tracks with party help, and her roll of 21 dropped the pursuit level from four to three [ch 006 / 006.02].
- The GM clarified the chase rules: a true chase begins only when pursuit reaches five, and the hunt ends when it reaches zero [ch 006 / 006.02].
- Jimjar identified Ormu as a moss that grows in warm, damp places, sheds dim light in a five-foot radius, and can be harvested and dried into glowing powder or pigment; when asked if Ormu paint would help them hide, he said no, "but it would mean that we looked better" [ch 006 / 006.01; ch 006 / 006.02].
- The party harvested some Ormu [ch 006 / 006.02].
- The party discussed blocking the narrow passage with a boulder or collapsing it to delay pursuit, and the GM noted a blockage would buy time but wouldn't stop someone very determined [ch 006 / 006.02].
- The party decided to block the tunnel behind them and disguise the blockage so pursuers would not realize it was sealed on purpose, which raised the question of who would do the deceiving [ch 006 / 006.03].
- Jimjar refused to help without a wager: he asked for five gold pieces, Zalthir countered with a pound of food which Jimjar valued at a single copper, and since Zalthir had vowed never to gamble again due to a past gambling problem (as Daz explained), Gyrgum took the bet instead [ch 006 / 006.03].
- Jimjar, who bet against himself, disguised the blockage successfully, which meant he lost the bet and now owes Gyrgum five gold pieces he does not have; Gyrgum admitted afterward he didn't even know which side of the bet he was on [ch 006 / 006.03].
- The party realized Jimjar did not bet to win but because "For him, if there's no bet, what's the point of doing it?", and Zalthir tested this by betting Jimjar couldn't fly to the roof of the cave, which Jimjar declined, explaining that anyone can decline to do something stupid [ch 006 / 006.03].
- That night, Daz noticed the deep gnome twins Topsy and Turvy huddled together talking quietly, but his insight check revealed nothing about their intentions [ch 006 / 006.03].
- After two more days of travel, on the fifth day Thorin led a search finding ten gallons of water; with Zalthir's help and a strong second roll from Gyrgum, the group gathered twenty pounds of food, with ten extra for tomorrow [ch 006 / 006.04].
- Sarith, who had been guiding them, broke into barely contained excitement before an enormous chasm five hundred feet deep and two thousand feet wide strung with a shifting network of enormous spider webs, saying "Yes, I'm very excited. This means we're in the right direction. All we have to do is cross this cavern." [ch 006 / 006.04].
- Sarith explained the place was known as the Silken Paths: the major highways were easy to travel, but old webs disintegrate and giant spiders are always spinning new ones, so the paths are always changing, and his advice amounted to "cross your fingers" [ch 006 / 006.04].
- Just as Sarith was about to step onto the web, two goblins — Yuk Yuk and Spiderbait — popped up and introduced themselves, offering their services as guides through the Silken Paths for two gold pieces per day [ch 006 / 006.04].
- Daz told the goblins the party was "destitute and without funds," and Gyrgum mentioned that Jimjar owed him five gold, at which point Jimjar began listing all of his debts, "both public and private" [ch 006 / 006.04].
- Thorin proposed the goblins prove they could cross, and Gyrgum suggested Jimjar bet them; Jimjar did, with a persuasion roll of 15, and a very annoyed Yuk Yuk countered with a bet of twenty gold pieces that he could get "from here to over there" [ch 006 / 006.05].
- Gyrgum's insight (14) told him Jimjar would not be paying, but he played along, told the goblins Jimjar was honest and would pay when he had the money, and offered to sign over Jimjar's five-gold IOU as well [ch 006 / 006.05].
- Yuk Yuk and Spiderbait put something strange on their feet and proceeded to slide and surf across the strands, dodging spiders with impressive agility, and won the bet [ch 006 / 006.05].
- Jimjar cheerfully conceded "yep, you won the bet," then admitted he had no money to pay; Yuk Yuk was very annoyed [ch 006 / 006.06].
- Spiderbait proposed a new deal: the goblins would guide the party in exchange for a share of future gold to cover their fee plus the twenty gold Jimjar now owed them, reasoning "the bet was entertaining and fun. Plus, we need to follow you guys to get the money." [ch 006 / 006.06].
- The party agreed, gaining Yuk Yuk and Spiderbait as guides through the Silken Paths, and the GM described them as "two goblin surfer dudes," with the goblins keeping an informal score of "who does the coolest thing" [ch 006 / 006.05; ch 006 / 006.06].
- The goblins explained the party would not be surfing ("that's advanced techniques. We'll just walk") and that the webs could not be burned down: a crazy wizard had tried, but there were simply too many spiders rebuilding faster than he could burn [ch 006 / 006.06].
- Thorin set the terms plainly: if the spiders attacked, "the deal's off because you haven't delivered what you promised" [ch 006 / 006.06].
- The party tied themselves together with rope (advantage on the save to avoid falling), went without light to avoid attracting predators, and set off along the five-foot-wide strands over the void [ch 006 / 006.06].
- Along the way the goblins sang about web surfing and brought up the time their friend Bobby was eaten by a spider, a detail quickly shouted down: "Dude, why are you bringing us down, man?" [ch 006 / 006.06].
- The party spotted a moving cocoon in the webbing, and Stool used his rapport spores to establish a connection, revealing a voice: "My name is Fargas Rumblefoot. Can you please get me out of here?" [ch 006 / 006.07].
- Fargas said his adventuring band had been searching for a long-lost tomb when a pack of mad gnolls attacked; he described gnolls as demon-possessed creatures that implant their spawn in living bodies where it later erupts; he had escaped and fled deeper into the Underdark, only to be caught by spiders and "placed here as a snack," insisting he was not infected [ch 006 / 006.07].
- Thorin answered, "That's what I used to say in the zombie apocalypse," and the party debated whether Fargas could be infected, with Daz and Zalthir floating the idea of leaving the cocoon behind [ch 006 / 006.07].
- Yuk Yuk warned "dudes, I have a really bad feeling about this, but I think there are some spiders that are coming here," and Fargas played his last card: "I know the location of the tomb, and more importantly, I know the location of the treasure." [ch 006 / 006.07].
- The GM ruled the cocoon was AC 10 with five points of slashing damage to cut; Thorin drew his short sword and cut through it in one stroke, "the first good roll I've gotten since we started playing," freeing Fargas [ch 006 / 006.07].
- Initiative was rolled with Thorin at 17 and Zalthir at 5 as the spiders closed in [ch 006 / 006.07].
- The GM drew the major web strands on the map: each strand was five feet wide, gaps were empty space, and cutting a strand dropped the segment between two junctions [ch 006 / 006.08].
- Zalthir stepped to a junction and cut one segment, forcing the pursuing spiders to spin a new bridge before they could follow, then struck a second strand which frayed but held by a single hit point [ch 006 / 006.08].
- Daz floated an Indiana Jones-style plan to cut everything and swing to safety, and Thorin suggested crossing one bridge and severing all the rest; the GM realized aloud he had "created a graph problem for a bunch of computers" [ch 006 / 006.08].
- The session paused mid-combat with Zalthir's bonus action still waiting for the next round and the far edge of the chasm still very far away; the group agreed to resume after New Year's [ch 006 / 006.08].
- The party fled southward through the Silken Paths from giant spiders; Thorin severed a web strand for nine damage, dropping one spider, and Daz blasted an intersection with all three magic missile darts to cut off pursuit [ch 007 / 007.01].
- After reaching safety, the party confronted the rescued halfling Fargas with his own written words; he revealed the history of Brysis of Khaem and her tomb, admitted he did not know its precise location, and Sarith drew a rough map marking the tomb's likely position on the northeastern edge of the Darklake [ch 007 / 007.02].
- A spectator appeared and warned the party telepathically that "the demons are coming," but its paranoia turned hostile; Gyrgum bet Jimjar a gold piece he could not talk it down, Jimjar's persuasion failed, and the party slew the spectator after a chaotic battle [ch 007 / 007.03].
- Near the tunnel's end the party found a web-bound chest and attempted to communicate with it via Stool's spores; the attempt produced nothing, and they bypassed the chest entirely [ch 007 / 007.04].
- At the end of the Silken Paths, Yuk Yuk and Spiderbait demanded their twenty gold from Jimjar; Jimjar talked them into staying with the party until he could pay, and Buppido cryptically invited the party to join his divine plan [ch 007 / 007.05].
- The party was escorted to the Altar of the Deep Father and presented by Archpriest Ploopploopeen as offerings to the rival faction; Bloppblippodd accepted them, and preparations began to sacrifice the Duergar arms dealer Hemeth [ch 008 / 008.01].
- Just as Bloppblippodd cried "Bring me the knife!", Ploopploopeen struck his own daughter with his scepters, triggering a full-scale Kuo-toan civil war between Sea Mother and Deep Father loyalists; the party withdrew from the center with the other prisoners [ch 008 / 008.01].
- The party looted Ploopploopeen's hut for a hoard of coins, a pearl strand, potions, and a scroll, then walked confidently toward the northeastern boat; Thorin observed pools of blood and churning water where Kuo-toa had been dragged under the surface [ch 008 / 008.03].
- Ploopploopeen struck down his daughter Bloppblippodd before the altar, ending the civil war; her dying cry was "Leemooggoogoon!" and Demogorgon immediately rose from the Darklake, a 30-foot-tall two-headed demon lord, ending the session on a cliffhanger [ch 008 / 008.04].
- Demogorgon, the Prince of Demons, erupted from the Darklake and waded toward the kuo-toa village of Sloobludop, his presence forcing every party member to make a DC 13 Charisma save against madness [ch 009 / 009.01; ch 009 / npcs].
- Daz, Eldeth, Topsy, Turvy, and Shuushar lost their grip on sanity; Daz collapsed into three minutes of hysterics and was carried by Jimjar, while Thorin carried the twins Topsy and Turvy [ch 009 / 009.01; ch 009 / 009.02; ch 009 / npcs].
- Thorin used his Second Wind to augment his failed save and held on; Buppido (rolled a 16), Jimjar, Sarith, and Stool were unaffected [ch 009 / 009.02; ch 009 / npcs].
- Sarith urged the group not to abandon Eldeth, saying "She's the only one keeping us hidden" [ch 009 / 009.02; ch 009 / npcs].
- The insane kuo-toa around the boat were incapacitated for four rounds, letting the party reach it unopposed; at Thorin's suggestion, Gyrgum persuaded the kuo-toa to act heroically and distract the demonic forces while the party escaped [ch 009 / 009.02; ch 009 / npcs].
- The party crammed into a small, barely-floating boat with two paddles and fled as Sloobludop was torn apart behind them [ch 009 / 009.02; ch 009 / npcs].
- Shuushar insisted his people summoned Leemooggoogoon, but an escapee slapped him and named the truth: Demogorgon [ch 009 / 009.03; ch 009 / npcs].
- The party connected the rumors of demons loose in the Underdark, the demons at Velkynvelve, and the demonic encounters since; they concluded only an avatar came through, not the whole being [ch 009 / 009.03; ch 009 / npcs].
- The group decided to head for Gracklstugh, suggested by Buppido, with Hemeth insisting "I know people"; the lost tomb of Khaem was set aside [ch 009 / 009.03; ch 009 / npcs].
- Daz spotted a field of submerged stone teeth with Shape Water, and the party threaded through them on a DC 12 group Dexterity check, with Thorin and Gyrgum succeeding [ch 009 / 009.03; ch 009 / spells].
- A cavern ceiling dropped to three feet above water; Gyrgum, Thorin, and Daz failed their saves and were knocked into the water, with only Zalthir keeping his seat [ch 009 / 009.03; ch 009 / 009.04].
- Three demonic manta-ray-like ixitxachitl surged up from the depths; Daz hit one with Magic Missile, Gyrgum cast Tasha's Caustic Brew through two, and Zalthir conjured Darkness over them, punching one dead with two blows [ch 009 / 009.04; ch 009 / npcs].
- Thorin spent his last Tactical Mind to haul himself back aboard, failed to pull Gyrgum up, then successfully pulled Daz back aboard while dodging a barbed tail [ch 009 / 009.04].
- Daz's magic missiles finished off a second creature, and the last one lurked unseen inside Zalthir's darkness; Gyrgum scrambled aboard and the party had "a big hug fest" [ch 009 / 009.04].
- Days later the party met kuo-toa refugees about ten days out from Gracklstugh; some prisoners argued "two boats is better than one boat. We could take them," but Gyrgum chose diplomacy, hailing "Where go you, folks?" [ch 009 / 009.05].
- Zalthir readied Darkness to envelop the refugees' boat as a contingency, with a roll of 16 meaning he pulled it off unnoticed; Hemeth advised against talking, since the last time he ended up wrapped in ropes and about to be hanged [ch 009 / 009.05; ch 009 / moments].
- The kuo-toa recognized the party's boat as one from Sloobludop, called the party "wise" for fleeing, revealed they were survivors seeking a new home, and declined trade [ch 009 / 009.05; ch 009 / 009.06].
- Shuushar announced he had found his calling and departed with the kuo-toa refugees to build a new, enlightened kuo-toa society, bidding an emotional farewell and offering a final mindfulness lesson to Buppido, which made Gyrgum spit [ch 009 / 009.06; ch 009 / moments].
- The party inspected the refugees' cargo, finding barnacle-encrusted statues of forgotten, alien deities that Thorin described as having a "Cthulhu vibe" [ch 009 / 009.06; ch 009 / items].
- Continuing across the Darklake, the party relied on survival checks since they had no rangers or rogues; the GM ruled no food or water rolls were needed "if people start dying off" could change [ch 010 / 010.01].
- The party's boat grounded on a submerged sandbar, and Daz finally used Shape Water for something practical, creating a wave that pushed the boat back into open water to cheers [ch 010 / 010.01; ch 010 / moments].
- Thorin succeeded on the survival check and navigated the group without getting lost, sparing them the next day's check; when Sarith asked if he knew where he was going, Thorin said he was following the vibes [ch 010 / 010.01; ch 010 / moments].
- The party spotted an abandoned duergar keelboat about 50 feet away, still laden with cargo crates and fitted with oars; Buppido claimed never to have seen such a boat, Hemeth identified it as a duergar keelboat and warned that duergar can turn invisible, then did so himself [ch 010 / 010.02].
- At Zalthir's suggestion, Daz sprayed water around the keelboat, revealing an invisible figure who plunged deeper into the lake; Buppido flopped flat on the deck pretending to be invisible, insisting it was all part of the divine plan [ch 010 / 010.02; ch 010 / moments].
- The invisible duergar slavers ambushed the party; Jimjar explained they kidnap travelers and sell them as slaves in Gracklstugh [ch 010 / 010.03; ch 010 / moments].
- Thorin spent a superiority die on initiative through his Ambush maneuver, rolling +4 to bring himself to 12 [ch 010 / 010.03].
- Zalthir conjured a 15-foot-radius sphere of magical darkness, moving it up to 60 feet at the start of each turn and striking down duergar with flurries of blows, since his disadvantage against invisible targets was canceled by the advantage from his darkness [ch 010 / 010.03; ch 010 / spells].
- Daz learned Magic Missile needs a target he can see and fell back on Scorching Ray, landing one of three rays for 7 fire damage; Gyrgum repeatedly coated slavers in Tasha's Caustic Brew, finishing one off and leaving fleeing ones burning [ch 010 / 010.03; ch 010 / spells].
- The duergar grappled three members of the crew including the goblin Spiderbait, then abandoned their prisoners to save themselves [ch 010 / 010.03; ch 010 / npcs].
- Thorin waded into the water using feints and Hill Strikes to batter the gray dwarves, gaining Vex for advantage on a next attack [ch 010 / 010.03].
- One duergar disengaged and fled; the remaining duergar enlarged themselves to giant proportions with innate magic, one staying invisible while enlarged [ch 010 / 010.03; ch 010 / spells].
- Buppido corrected the party that he is "Derro, not a duergar," as he lay on the ground pretending to be invisible during the battle [ch 010 / 010.03; ch 010 / npcs].
- Zalthir pummeled a duergar until it fell and Daz finished off another with Magic Missile; the slain duergar shrank to normal size and fell into the water [ch 010 / 010.03].
- Gyrgum became frustrated by the darkness, shouting "Leave one of them not in darkness," and repeatedly coated fleeing duergar in caustic brew, leaving one "doubly acidified" for 8 acid damage [ch 010 / 010.03; ch 010 / moments].
- One duergar attempted to flee with Spiderbait but dropped the prisoner to prioritize his own escape [ch 010 / 010.03].
- Thorin delivered a final non-lethal Feinting Attack of 12 damage to the last remaining duergar, knocking him unconscious so they could "capture him and we want to make him talk"; Zalthir dispelled the darkness as combat ended [ch 010 / 010.03; ch 010 / 010.04].
- Buppido and Sarith both offered their "expert" interrogation services; Sarith declared "as a member of Lolth's own guards, I too am an expert in questioning techniques," adding that Daz's techniques had been most impressive of late [ch 010 / 010.04; ch 010 / npcs].
- Spiderbait, rescued but shaken, made clear he would leave at the next opportunity, saying "next place I can get out of here, I'm getting out of here," noting his best friend was dead and he had been attacked by a demon lord [ch 010 / 010.04; ch 010 / npcs].
- The party captured the duergar keelboat and opened a chest aboard yielding 1,100 gold pieces and an alchemy jug that produces a chosen liquid until dawn [ch 010 / 010.04; ch 010 / items].
- The party celebrated solving their water supply problem and debated producing mayonnaise instead of acid or poison, making their weapons "more delicious," and secured two zurkhwood casks of harvested fungi for food [ch 010 / 010.04; ch 010 / items].
- The GM reminded the party that the captive duergar was "more interested in profit than … being a hero," and that they could arrive in Gracklstugh as visitors or as merchandise [ch 010 / 010.04; ch 010 / moments].
- The party interrogated their duergar prisoner, Brannum Redmarch, on the Darklake after securing a larger boat; Thorin made clear his survival depended on guiding them faithfully to Gracklstugh [ch 011 / 011.01].
- The party decided Brannum would ride in their own boat as a hostage, tied up; Gyrgum warned he would be "the first to go" if he tried to betray them [ch 011 / 011.01].
- Sarith admired Thorin's direct interrogation technique but expressed disappointment that torture wasn't used [ch 011 / 011.01].
- Thorin floated selling Brannum into slavery; Buppido called it an excellent idea and Hemeth said he "knew a guy" [ch 011 / 011.01].
- An insight check of 18 confirmed Brannum was telling the truth about the directions, because he knew staying alive depended on it [ch 011 / 011.01].
- On the fifteenth day of travel, the party encountered a diamond-shaped limestone islet with an eight-foot pyramid of bones topped by a horned ram skull; Brannum panicked, saying "this was a mistake. I thought we were going the other way" [ch 011 / 011.02].
- A violent beam of lightning erupted from behind a stalactite and struck at the party's boat; a group check kept the boat seaworthy with some damage [ch 011 / 011.02].
- A ten-foot beholder zombie rose from behind a stalactite; Gyrgum's Command spell failed because the undead doesn't understand his language [ch 011 / 011.02; ch 011 / 011.03].
- Zalthir conjured a 15-foot sphere of magical darkness around the beholder zombie, keeping part visible for targeting, and struck it with unarmed blows [ch 011 / 011.03].
- Thorin fought from inside the darkness with advantage, used a feinting attack, and tried Hill Strike, but learned the zombie is immune to being knocked prone [ch 011 / 011.03].
- Daz cast Magic Missiles for 15 damage; Gyrgum used Divine Spark. The zombie hit Gyrgum for 10 damage and poisoned him, and paralyzed Daz with a ray [ch 011 / 011.03].
- Stool, Hemeth, and the companion crew rowed to the fight and fired crossbow volleys; the second volley, combined with Zalthir's assault, destroyed the beholder zombie, which exploded into ash and freed Daz from paralysis [ch 011 / 011.03].
- Gyrgum cast Cure Wounds on Zalthir, restoring 15 of the 16 hit points lost to the zombie's bite [ch 011 / 011.03; ch 011 / 011.04].
- Brannum confessed this route was a shortcut his crew used, where two of his boats had vanished; a survivor had spoken of "some kind of evil altar" [ch 011 / 011.04].
- Daz peered into the water and discovered the sunken wrecks of Brannum's lost boats on the lakebed, with bodies moving among them, possibly zombies [ch 011 / 011.04].
- Gyrgum cast Mending to repair the boat hull and Find Traps on the islet, which found no traps; the party never set foot on the islet [ch 011 / 011.04; ch 011 / 011.05].
- Daz spotted a small chest on the lakebed surrounded by zombies; after debate, Thorin volunteered to dive for it [ch 011 / 011.05].
- The party planned the dive: Gyrgum cast Shield of Faith on Thorin, Daz shaped an air bubble with Shape Water, and Zalthir created magical darkness to blind the zombies [ch 011 / 011.05].
- Thorin retrieved the chest without alerting a single undead; it contained 120 silver pieces, 30 gold pieces, four spell scrolls, and a driftglobe [ch 011 / 011.05].
- Brannum claimed a share of the treasure since it belonged to his crew, but Daz rebuffed him, offering that he could argue it out with the zombies below [ch 011 / 011.05].
- The DM explained scrolls can be copied into a spellbook or kept for one use outside spell slots, and explained the "Magic action" [ch 011 / 011.05].
- Two more days later, within a day of Gracklstugh, an enormous aquatic troll speaking Giant demanded one of the party for a meal; Gyrgum refused to hand over Brannum on neutral-good grounds, and Thorin called for them to run [ch 011 / 011.06].
- Jimjar shouted a warning that the troll would regenerate unless burned with fire or acid [ch 011 / 011.06].
- Daz's Scorching Ray missed, but his Magic Missiles hit for 9; the troll pulled the boat down and clawed Daz, while Zalthir struck it, making it release the hull, and pushed it five feet back [ch 011 / 011.06].
- Gyrgum cast Tasha's Caustic Brew, vomiting a wave of acid, but the troll dodged the worst of it; Brannum offered to grow giant-size and row if untied, but Thorin refused [ch 011 / 011.06].
- The troll retreated into the depths, grumbling it was only trying to be helpful by reducing the party's mouths to feed [ch 011 / 011.06].
- The party arrived at Gracklstugh; Buppido and Hemeth explained the Duergar's strict laws and dislike of Drow make it a safe haven from Drow pursuit [ch 011 / 011.07].
- Hemeth advised entering quietly through the docks, saying "The guards guard the doors, not the docks," and warned "this is not the time or place for heroics" [ch 011 / 011.07].
- The party discussed ransoming Brannum; Thorin pointed out they never promised to let him go, and he had tried to sell them first [ch 011 / 011.07].
- The party counted their funds: 27 platinum pieces and 1,100 gold pieces, after a spreadsheet error had listed the gold as platinum [ch 011 / 011.07].
- The party arrived at the duergar city of Gracklstugh by boat across the Darklake, tying up at the Darklake district where non-duergar are permitted, and were met by heat, smog, forge-light and din [ch 012 / 012.01].
- Hemeth warned the party that non-duergar are restricted to the Darklake district, that duergar guards can turn invisible, that theft is punishable by immediate death without trial, and that escaped slaves risk being repossessed or sold back to the drow; Thorin objected they were only "temporarily detained" [ch 012 / 012.01].
- The party set their goals in Gracklstugh: resupply with better weapons and armor and find information leading out of the Underdark, with the known route running Gracklstugh to the lost tomb on the dwarf's map, then to Blingdenstone as a possible exit; the party is level 3 [ch 012 / 012.01].
- At the Blade Bazaar, Gyrgum approached merchant Dulgir, who insulted him until learning he was buying, then adopted a posh accent and gave a ten percent discount on a mace in exchange for Gyrgum's short sword; Gyrgum also bought a hand axe and picked up a light crossbow [ch 012 / 012.02; ch 012 / npcs].
- Zalthir recalled a mysterious list of names found while cleaning, learning one name belongs to someone in Gracklstugh and that the people on the list may have connections to the drow slave trade, whether as suppliers or worried family members being unclear [ch 012 / 012.02].
- The party offered their captive Brannum Redmarch his freedom in exchange for using his underworld connections to procure uncommon magical goods from restricted parts of the city; Brannum eagerly agreed, insisting the deals be "done within the context of a free exchange of goods and services and not under duress" [ch 012 / 012.02].
- Hemeth, offended at being passed over, offered his own high-quality weapons at a discount boasting his handaxes would be easily reused by whoever killed their owner; he can source a +1 weapon himself but uncommon magic items must be named specifically [ch 012 / 012.02; ch 012 / npcs].
- Zalthir expressed interest in an Eldritch Claw tattoo, and Brannum claimed he may know a contact who can procure it, requesting two days; the party can shake its pursuers in the city for now [ch 012 / 012.02].
- Gyrgum purchased scrolls of Detect Magic and Identify from a merchant who punctuated his sales pitch with sudden, violent threats before continuing as if nothing happened; a first-level spell scroll costs about 60 gold pieces [ch 012 / 012.02].
- Daz sought a magical light crossbow imbued with True Strike for accuracy but was told nothing like that is sold in the bazaar; he decided against buying anything, saying "I'm happy with what I have and I need to level up before anything makes sense" [ch 012 / 012.02].
- Thorin asked for heavy armor, confirmed available in the Darklake district, and settled on darts as his ranged weapon after confirming the game isn't using pistols [ch 012 / 012.02].
- The party debated Buppido's fate—release, kill, or turn him over—with Gyrgum as a neutral good cleric unwilling to slay him in cold blood and Zalthir noting Buppido knows the tomb's location and could point the drow pursuers at it [ch 012 / 012.03].
- At Zalthir's and Gyrgum's suggestion, the party had Buppido eat a Tongue of Madness mushroom, after which he unleashed a five-day backlog of petty insults against every party member in one long, unbroken stream of consciousness [ch 012 / 012.03; ch 012 / items].
- Jimjar theorized the kuo-toa either created a god through mass psychosis or actually summoned Demogorgon, dismissed the idea that Buppido could summon a demon lord, and lamented he should have bet on Buppido screaming at him to stop betting [ch 012 / 012.03; ch 012 / npcs].
- Zalthir asked whether Buppido might summon another horror like the kraken the kuo-toa raised; the party considered marooning Buppido in the Underdark when they leave, and Jimjar and the companions were asked to guard Buppido while the party shops [ch 012 / 012.03].
- After the tirade, Zalthir concluded they could simply cut Buppido loose once they are done with the city [ch 012 / 012.03].
- Gyrgum noticed unsettling behavior in the Blade Bazaar: a duergar merchant consulting a non-existent twin brother, merchants quoting wildly different prices for the same goods, and one merchant turning completely invisible mid-transaction while continuing to speak; Gyrgum rolled Insight (15) and sensed "there's something off" [ch 012 / 012.04].
- Gyrgum stepped outside the bazaar and looked around just as a two-headed stone giant burst through one of the district's gates, howling with madness and lashing out, sending a duergar soldier's broken body crashing near the party and barreling straight at Gyrgum [ch 012 / 012.04; ch 012 / 012.05].
- Thorin recognized the giant's chaotic violence as extremely abnormal, since stone giants are normally deeply taciturn [ch 012 / 012.05].
- Gyrgum rolled Investigation (15) to test for illusion after a pebble struck his foot, concluding it was either real or an illusion so convincing the difference is immaterial [ch 012 / 012.05].
- Gyrgum brandished his holy symbol at the giant as it barreled toward the party; the GM confirmed they can fight it and that duergar troops will arrive, and helping the duergar might earn goodwill while fleeing might get them blamed [ch 012 / 012.05].
- Daz opened the engagement with Magic Missile dealing 15 damage, and Zalthir rushed into melee range, conjuring a sphere of magical darkness that swallowed the giant and blinded it [ch 012 / 012.05].
- Thorin exploited the giant's blindness, striking with his short sword with advantage for 6 damage and holding his Action Surge in reserve; Gyrgum channeled radiant energy dealing 11 damage after the giant failed a DC 13 Constitution save [ch 012 / 012.05].
- The giant lashed out blindly at Thorin with disadvantage, rolling a 25 (+9) for 10 bludgeoning damage, but Zalthir deflected the blow, a reaction reducing damage by 1d10 plus a modifier, so no damage was taken [ch 012 / 012.05].
- A squad of duergar city guards arrived, two acting at initiative 10, ready to join the fray [ch 012 / 012.05].
- The party continued assaulting the giant with Magic Missile for 9, Zalthir hitting for 11 and again with a focus point, Thorin using a feinting attack for 9 and then 7, and Gyrgum hitting with a crossbow bolt for 9; the session stopped with the duergar guards up next [ch 012 / 012.05].
- Hemeth and Brannum went off on their errand together, marked by an arrow on the map [ch 012 / 012.04].
- The party told its six traveling companions to wait in the district, with Hemeth willing to keep quiet and Buppido eager to leave [ch 012 / 012.02].
- The party fought alongside a squad of duergar militia against a rampaging, two-headed stone giant in the streets of Gracklstugh; two duergar guards magically enlarged themselves, two others unleashed fire sprays dealing 23 total damage, and the giant swung his great club at the party and guards [ch 013 / 013.01].
- Thorin made a check (16) recognizing the giant had likely gone mad, noting stone giants are particularly unforgiving of those who kill their kind [ch 013 / 013.01].
- Daz struck the giant with Magic Missile for 6 damage; the GM ruled dropping the giant to zero with a weapon would knock him out but a spell like Magic Missile would kill him [ch 013 / 013.01].
- Gyrgum channeled divine energy through his holy symbol against the giant; Thorin scored a critical hit with his short sword then used Action Surge for another hit; Zalthir declared non-lethal damage and attacked three times, landing one hit for 5 damage [ch 013 / 013.01; ch 013 / 013.02].
- The party switched to non-lethal weapon attacks and wore the giant down from 34 hit points to 8; Gyrgum hit him with a mace for 1 damage and missed with a hand axe swing [ch 013 / 013.02].
- Thorin failed to persuade or intimidate the duergar militia to stay their hand, and the militia delivered the killing blow; Thorin spoke funeral words over the body and the darkness (cast by Zalthir) lifted [ch 013 / 013.02].
- The duergar militia declared the operation a success and said any future support for the local militia and police would be "greatly appreciated and rewarded" [ch 013 / 013.03].
- Gyrgum, after Thorin suggested questioning the militia, asked about the strange behavior; the militia, once questions were phrased literally, confirmed a significant increase in chaotic and antisocial behavior across the city with no known cause, and suggested asking a clan leader [ch 013 / 013.03].
- Dorhun, a stone giant and fellow apprentice to Stonespeaker Hgraam, arrived, identified the fallen giant as Rihuud (who went mad and sprouted a second head while communing with stone in Cairngorm Cavern), and confirmed via the militia that the party tried to spare Rihuud's life [ch 013 / 013.03].
- Dorhun invited the party to visit Cairngorm Cavern before they leave the city and ordered the reluctant Stone Guard to escort them there [ch 013 / 013.03].
- Gartokkar Xundorn of the Keepers of the Flame materialized from invisibility, flashed a badge that scattered onlookers, explained his order tends to the red dragon Themberchaud, and invited the party to his quarters by the following day for an unnamed task [ch 013 / 013.04].
- Gartokkar promised city passes and, as Thorin summarized, "a limited get-out of jail-free card" for helping with a problem he was not yet ready to name; the only commitment was attending the meeting [ch 013 / 013.04].
- The party went to the Darklake Brewery's outside kiosk (the brewery being duergar-only); Gyrgum bought drinks for a talkative halfling who revealed the Deepking Horgar Steelshadow had reportedly gone mad and that the factions were at each other's throats [ch 013 / 013.05].
- The halfling warned that attending an official meeting with a faction leader would turn the party from civilians into soldiers in the eyes of rival groups, confirmed the invitation came from the Keepers of the Flame, and explained the Keepers periodically replace their red dragon with one raised from a purchased or stolen egg [ch 013 / 013.05].
- A drow patron mocks duergar conspiracies as blunt, graceless violence compared to Menzoberranzan's political intrigues [ch 013 / 013.05].
- Captain Errde Blackskull of the Stone Guard arrived from the prison direction and brought the party to her front office in Overlake Hold, where she commended their initiative and "brutal efficiency" and proposed a job [ch 013 / 013.06].
- Errde tasked the party with tracking down the derro Droki, who lives in the West Cleft District and evades patrols "as if he somehow knows in advance where the duergar patrols will be invisible or otherwise"; she revealed a suspected conspiracy involving the Council of Savants and clan lairds [ch 013 / 013.06].
- Errde offered each party member 350 gold pieces' worth of equipment, safe passage out of the city, and protection from drow hunting them; after Daz's persuasion roll of 18, she gave each a down payment of 175 gp of equipment from her armory [ch 013 / 013.06].
- Thorin drew on his armory credit for chain mail (75 gp) and twenty darts; the others deferred their scrolls and potions to next time, and the session ended just before an encounter was to be run [ch 013 / 013.06].
- The party noted Errde never forbade them from also working for the Keepers of the Flame, and suspected she might be mad [ch 013 / 013.06].
- The party reviewed their recovered gear (bedroll, rope, tinderbox, torches, waterskin) and used their 175 gold-piece store credit, good only at that store for common potions and scrolls priced from "Sane Magical Prices," to buy a week's rations and healing potions [ch 014 / 014.01].
- Gyrgum wanted scrolls of Thunderwave and Heroism but learned he "can only cast spells that are on your spell scroll on your class spell list," so he bought a scroll of Guiding Bolt and reserved the rest of his funds; the shopkeeper was disappointed she got "the two who don't need any magic, who don't need any weapons" [ch 014 / 014.01].
- The party ended with nine healing potions (two already on hand, two each from Daz and Thorin, three added by the GM), held jointly; Daz was left with 105 gold [ch 014 / 014.01].
- Captain Errde Blackskull of the Stone Guard pulled the party aside and asked them to investigate rumors of the "Empty-Scabbard Killers" (also Forak-Erach-Naek), a supposed order of psionic assassins; she introduced her subordinate Grimholl Forgebrand (nicknamed Hulg), who described finding bodies with no visible wounds that clerics couldn't revive [ch 014 / 014.02].
- Errde said the deaths of two of her own clan members made the matter personal; she had found one of the bodies herself, and snapped when Gyrgum asked who found it; she believed corrupt forces were using the myth to cover their tracks and pointed the party toward the Whorlstone Tunnels beneath Gracklstugh [ch 014 / 014.02].
- The party took a long rest in a shared bunk-bed room costing five copper pieces per person per night at the lodging where their crew had been put up, with Daz down to five hit points [ch 014 / 014.03].
- In the morning, Jimjar urged heading to Blingdenstone, noting "We could just get up and leave today," but Daz insisted on visiting the Keepers of the Flame first, arguing "I think gathering intelligence is just as important as stocking up," and the party agreed [ch 014 / 014.03].
- The party noticed the Topsy-Turvy twins had slipped away during the night; Zalthir asked "Were they the wererats?" and the GM noted they "haven't" necessarily left for good; Buppido still looked cranky but everyone's health seemed fine [ch 014 / 014.03].
- At the end of a pier on the Darklake Docks, two masked, hooded, previously invisible assassins shimmered into view and stabbed a Duergar merchant named Werz with glowing blades; the party charged to intervene, and Gyrgum dubbed the group "Firefly Tactics" [ch 014 / 014.04].
- Daz dealt nine damage with magical bolts; Zalthir conjured a 15-foot sphere of magical darkness, missed on a natural 1, then spent a focus point to attack again and killed one assassin outright [ch 014 / 014.04].
- Within the darkness, Thorin used a Precision Attack superiority die (advantage from blindsight) for six damage, Gyrgum struck with radiant energy for two damage via True Strike, and Daz finished the last assassin with Scorching Ray at disadvantage from four and six damage [ch 014 / 014.04].
- The assassins' glowing blades vanished upon their deaths, revealing them to be psionic weapons; the party was disappointed, Thorin declaring "God damn it. Come on. It's a psychic blade. This is bullshit, man." [ch 014 / 014.04; ch 014 / moment].
- Thorin cautioned before helping Werz that "He might be a bad guy, and the guys we just killed might be the good guys"; Gyrgum healed Werz with a prayer, and Werz thanked them and invited the party to meet him at the Shattered Spire for a reward, claiming he had "no idea why I was targeted by assassins" [ch 014 / 014.04].
- The party searched the assassin bodies and found eighteen silver pieces, a trinket, bloodstained gloves (not magical), and a box of scented candles, plus a fish-skin parchment with a charcoal likeness of Werz, confirming he was a deliberate target [ch 014 / 014.05].
- Gyrgum pushed the corpses into the water and the party slipped away before the Stone Guards arrived; they took a short rest, during which Daz used Arcane Recovery to regain a second-level spell slot [ch 014 / 014.05].
- The party, escorted by Duergar priests on the strength of their permit, traveled through the Flowstone District, territory "nobody but Duergar," to meet Themberchaud, the Wyrmsmith of Gracklstugh, who announced himself with "You didn't say I was having surfacers for dinner tonight" [ch 014 / 014.06].
- In his lair, Themberchaud breathed fire in all directions ("Anyone who's invisible will be turned to ash") to root out invisible spies, then settled onto a mountain of gold and interrogated each party member, dismissing Gyrgum's devotion to Bahamut with "Next!", mildly disdaining Zalthir's brass dragonborn heritage, approving of Daz's Drow origins from Menzoberranzan, and delighting in Thorin's rootless backstory ("Nobody who can black, nobody to be blackmailed. That's wonderful.") [ch 014 / 014.06; ch 014 / moment].
- Themberchaud, without waiting for acceptance, declared the party his personal agents, summoned his liaison Gartokkar, and gave each a golden badge bearing his profile; their first mission was to do whatever the Keepers ask but report back to Themberchaud first [ch 014 / 014.06].
- Gartokkar revealed the Keepers' power "derive in equal measure from our psionic abilities, the leadership of the Deepking, and our influence over Themberchaud," and that the dragon did not know he was being groomed for replacement via a stolen red dragon egg taken by the Gray Ghosts [ch 014 / 014.07].
- Gartokkar insisted the old dragon would be "set free" rather than disposed of, described a great psionic disturbance in the Underdark he believed the Gray Ghosts caused (the party's lore checks failed, Daz rolling a natural 1), and tasked the party with tracking down the derro messenger Droki, who carries messages and supplies for the Gray Ghosts [ch 014 / 014.07].
- Gartokkar gave the party a holy symbol of Laduguer and gold pins bearing Themberchaud's profile (brought by an acolyte), granting passage beyond the Darklake District, and threatened that the Stone Guards would "invent an appropriate crime" if they refused [ch 014 / 014.07].
- The party realized they now operated as agents of the dragon, servants of the faction replacing him, and investigators for a Stone Guard captain hunting the same derro; Thorin floated escaping "in a blaze of glory on the back of a dragon" [ch 014 / 014.07; ch 014 / moment].
- The party decided to visit the Stone Giants of Clan Cairngorm, the one group they had not yet spoken with, who had an open invitation for them, weighing their open leads before acting [ch 015 / 015.01].
- Gyrgum's player said "we should get all the offers or assignments before we really do anything." [ch 015 / 015.01]
- The DM confirmed as lore that Daz's previous character "was a famous writer, a hero of the realms, involved in a significant battle, and a well-known publisher of fiction and art," and Daz is a fan of that character [ch 015 / 015.01].
- The party arrived at the entrance of Cairngorm Cavern in the Southfurrow District, a long tunnel running several hundred feet into the home of Clan Cairngorm, and were met by Hgraam, the Stone Giant clan leader, sitting thoughtfully at the entrance [ch 015 / 015.01].
- Thorin asked "What, like the clan leaders just chilling by the entrance?" when they met Hgraam [ch 015 / 015.01].
- Gyrgum was nervous about the meeting because he "didn't have a super relationship with them before." [ch 015 / 015.01]
- The party prepared to initiate conversation with Hgraam, knowing stone giants "can wait a very long time before they initiate a conversation because they don't want to rush anything." [ch 015 / 015.01]
- In the audience, Hgraam spoke gravely of his apprentice, the Stone Giant the party had earlier tried to stop without killing, who had been driven to madness not by an enemy blade but by the very stones themselves [ch 015 / 015.02].
- Daz offered condolences to Hgraam: "Sorry for what happened to your training master. Hope you're doing okay." [ch 015 / 015.02]
- Hgraam expressed grief: "Yes, it was a tragedy." and acknowledged the party tried to stop the apprentice rather than kill him [ch 015 / 015.02].
- Hgraam warned of an evil stirring in the blackest depths of the Underdark and recited omens: "a cave with two faces, rock devoured, land overgrown," and "The pebble believes itself flesh. The earth rejects its wards, and the tunnels shake in fury" [ch 015 / 015.02; ch 015 / moment].
- Hgraam explained the surface world, which he called the "Dreamlands," can be reached through the town of the deep gnomes; he has never been there, does not know the path himself, and has only heard through trade that the deep gnomes know a way out, echoing what Jimjar told the party [ch 015 / 015.02].
- Hgraam warned the Whorlstone Tunnels beneath the city are filled with evil, saying "There is much evil in there. Many have told the Deepking that what goes underneath his city affects what goes on in his city. And what goes underneath is great evil" and "The more he enforces control, the more it escapes outside the city to seize control of the city." [ch 015 / 015.02]
- Zalthir's Arcana check led him to suspect the apprentice's madness was tied to a growing presence of demons, which was far more widespread than the party's scattered sightings of Demogorgon, the kuo-toa's worshipped creation, the demonic manta rays, and the brawling demons at Velkynvelve had suggested [ch 015 / 015.02].
- Daz found the giant's speech comical and senseless ("This makes absolutely no sense"), while Zalthir and Gyrgum deduced that various factions are hiding underground to escape the Deepking's rigid control, away from the invisible duergar patrolling the city above [ch 015 / 015.02].
- Zalthir asked why the apprentice in particular went mad, and his Arcana result suggested the increasing presence of demons; since the apprentice never left the city's caves, the madness could strike anyone and "not a localized phenomenon" [ch 015 / 015.02].
- The DM noted: "For what you normally expect outside of the abyss, it is crawling with demons," and the fact that a demon lord of Demogorgon's power is walking the earth implies an extraordinarily powerful magical force behind it [ch 015 / 015.02].
- Hgraam knows nothing about the Gray Ghosts, though the Deepking has asked him about them, and he has advised the Deepking to be patient and "watch the stones." [ch 015 / 015.02]
- Hgraam revealed the Deepking has become increasingly paranoid and erratic since taking on a new advisor named Shal, who has never come to visit and with whom the king no longer discusses Gracklstugh's problems with Hgraam [ch 015 / 015.02].
- Gyrgum summed up Hgraam's point about the Deepking: "The tighter you squeeze your fist, the more it will slip through your fingers." [ch 015 / moment]
- After a long rant about everyone being in a rush, Hgraam ended the audience: "it is time for you to leave there are rocks that need to be tended." [ch 015 / 015.02]
- Hgraam presented the party with a polished crystal the length of a human forearm, imbued with the power to commune with animals, plants, and the dead, and to sharpen the mind's eye when searching for hidden truths; his apprentice brought it out [ch 015 / 015.02; ch 015 / 015.03].
- The party examined the gifted Stonespeaker Crystal, learning it has 10 charges, requires attunement (possible only after a long rest), grants advantage on Investigation, with speak with dead costing four charges and speak with animals costing two [ch 015 / 015.03].
- The DM suggested the character with the highest Intelligence carry the crystal; Daz has INT 17 and Investigation +5, Thorin has INT 14 [ch 015 / 015.03].
- Daz was fine with someone else taking the crystal; Thorin thought speak with dead is the most useful power and that animals and plants could say whether someone came this way [ch 015 / 015.03].
- Gyrgum was keen to meet the Deepking's advisor, but the DM warned this "might not have quite the effect you want," and it went on the checklist for later [ch 015 / 015.03].
- The party reviewed what they know of Droki, a derro courier linked to various factions, unclear whether he belongs to a faction or just carries messages, pointed toward by both Errde Blackskull and Gartokkar [ch 015 / 015.03].
- The party planned to head to the West Cleft District to track down Droki, deducing that a derro living in a slum where "any duergar that tried to get in there would probably get slaughtered" would be the ideal messenger [ch 015 / 015.03].
- The DM described the West Cleft District as the original home of the city's derro slaves, remaining a dark and dangerous ghetto, with the East Cleft District settled after the derro earned their freedom [ch 015 / 015.03].
- Upon reaching the entrance of the squalid district, the party decided to pivot and consult Buppido for local intelligence [ch 015 / 015.03].
- The party returned to Ghohlbrorn's Lair and found Jimjar gambling with the local residents, who said "ah, yes, the crazy derro" and led them upstairs to a forlorn Buppido [ch 015 / 015.04].
- Thorin asked "is it insulting to assume all derro know each other?" when questioning Buppido about Droki [ch 015 / 015.04].
- Buppido laughed heartily and revealed that the entrance to the Whorlstone Tunnels is hidden within the Derro slums, offering: "I'll even show you where it is if you let me go." [ch 015 / 015.04]
- The party had already planned to free him eventually; Thorin, the most paranoid about it, noted "he did kill one of our party members." [ch 015 / 015.04]
- Buppido explained the Council of Savants, the derro governing body in Gracklstugh made up of more powerful derro with special abilities [ch 015 / 015.04].
- Buppido delivered a manic rant about the corrupt Council of Savants, which he says has always promised to lead the derro to freedom and always failed, claiming he has a divine mission to free his people: "You must let me go. If you don't let me go, the Savants will do something stupid, as they've always done something stupid. I know. I have the divine mission in my hands." [ch 015 / 015.04; ch 015 / moment]
- The party concluded Buppido is mad, with Gyrgum suspecting the demons may have affected him [ch 015 / 015.04].
- The party saw the key insight: the derro control the entrance to the Whorlstone Tunnels, possibly why the Deepking cannot crush the factions there, recalling Errde Blackskull said marching in would cause a riot and a war [ch 015 / 015.04].
- The party debated Buppido's fate: Zalthir wanted to wait and free him when they left the city, Thorin suggested taking him along to vouch for them with Droki [ch 015 / 015.04].
- The party agreed to give Buppido a spare Stone Guard pass so he could accompany them and guide them into the caverns; an Insight check left them convinced he will gladly help, and Buppido said "don't you worry, I know what to do." [ch 015 / 015.04]
- Thorin warned Buppido that a double-cross would end with him sliced up; Buppido replied "but you are now allowing me to proceed onto my grand vision." [ch 015 / 015.04; ch 015 / moment]
- The party used their Stone Guard passes to enter the West Cleft District, accompanied by Buppido, who gleefully showed his pass to gate guards that met him with "the usual set of contempt." [ch 015 / 015.05]
- Inside the district, the stench turned from metallic to repugnant with "hundreds of derro muttering, screaming at each other," and those who spotted the party looked at them "with a burning hatred." [ch 015 / 015.05]
- Thorin proposed yelling for Droki, but Buppido made clear they should stay on the down low; Thorin said "It's like yelling Maria in West Side Story." [ch 015 / 015.05; ch 015 / moment]
- The party chose to find Droki before the cave entrance, reasoning Droki may know what is in the caves; Thorin: "once you go in the dungeon, you're in the dungeon." [ch 015 / 015.05]
- They spotted Droki, a Derro courier in a floppy hat with two tentacle-like protrusions, muttering to himself and moving with unnatural, almost magical speed: "Droki is late. They will be so angry. Delays, blocks, procrastinations, obstructions, pfah!" [ch 015 / 015.05; ch 015 / moment]
- Droki vanished down a narrow fissure hidden behind scraps of canvas and moldy boxes; Buppido said "he's going into stone tunnels. That's one of the entrances" and immediately chased after him [ch 015 / 015.05].
- The party let Buppido go, with Thorin reasoning "At this point, we can't coerce him without a fuss, and that could go bad." and then followed into the Whorlstone Tunnels, squeezing through a gap leaking eerie fog, Thorin entering "Warily, ready for traps or the unexpected." [ch 015 / 015.05]
- The Whorlstone Tunnels had ceilings about 50 feet high dropping to 25 in the narrow tunnels, an air that tasted faintly of metal, all sound strangely muted, dripping water making no echo, and swirling patterns of faerzress casting dancing spirals of soft light across jagged stone pillars [ch 015 / 015.05; ch 015 / moment].
- The party discovered unique fungi, including bigwigs and pygmyworts, with size-altering properties, and took one of each; Gyrgum worried about eating too much bigwig since only a medium creature fits through the fissure [ch 015 / 015.05].
- Droki headed north, oblivious to the party, and the party followed openly, reasoning "doesn't seem alert to being followed" and that following him might reveal all the people he works for [ch 015 / 015.05].
- At an intersection, the party encountered Buppido standing before a makeshift altar constructed of skeletons and bones, wiping his hands on his vest and smiling viciously [ch 015 / 015.06].
- Buppido claimed a divine mission and demanded the party worship him as leader, declaring them his 'faithful': "I didn't expect you to find me here with my shrine nearly finished and power." ... "Yes, I sense you are ready to receive my truth into your hearts." [ch 015 / 015.06; ch 015 / moment]
- Zalthir noted the absurdity: "Finds you here? We were with you five seconds ago." [ch 015 / moment]
- Buppido flew into a psychotic rage and attacked with a hooked spear, with a group of skeletons rising to defend his shrine and surrounding the party [ch 015 / 015.06].
- Thorin asked "Just to be clear, we can take him if we need to, right?" before the fight, and noted "I'll tank it and take some hits." [ch 015 / 015.06]
- Daz cast a spell outlining three skeletons in glowing light (Faerie Fire, affecting a 20-foot cube, Dexterity save), and Zalthir conjured a sphere of absolute darkness to disorient the attackers [ch 015 / 015.06; ch 015 / 015.07].
- In the battle, Thorin engaged the skeletons in melee, hitting one with advantage for 4 damage [ch 015 / 015.06].
- Zalthir hit a skeleton for 5 damage and spent a focus point on a second attack which missed [ch 015 / 015.07].
- Buppido lunged at Thorin with a hooked spear but failed to land a blow [ch 015 / 015.07].
- Skeletons retaliated with short swords and crossbows but struggled to see through the magical dark and glowing outlines; a short bow missed Gyrgum, a short sword hit for 5 damage, and a crossbow skeleton hit Gyrgum for 5 damage [ch 015 / 015.07].
- Gyrgum tried Turn Undead, but only three skeletons could see the symbol, so Zalthir offered to move his darkness so all could see it; Gyrgum readied the turning as a prepared action, his player calling it "Well, working together." [ch 015 / 015.07]
- Daz unleashed unerring darts of pure magical energy (Magic Missile) striking Buppido for 15 damage against his 13 hit points, ending his delusions and his life; the DM said "Buppido's long quest of greatness and his belief in his divine mission has now ended. He's dead." [ch 015 / 015.07; ch 015 / moment]
- Gyrgum brandished his chessboard holy symbol, channeling the divine power of Bahamut, forcing the remaining skeletons to make a DC 13 Wisdom save at -1, with three fleeing in blind panic; Thorin killed one with an 8-damage opportunity attack [ch 015 / 015.07].
- Gyrgum said "The power of Bahamut compels you to get to the point of the matter." [ch 015 / moment]
- The party coordinated to eliminate the retreating undead: Gyrgum's crossbow hit a faerie-fired skeleton with advantage for 8 damage, Daz split a second Magic Missile for 8 and 1 damage, Zalthir killed the last skeleton next to Thorin [ch 015 / 015.07].
- A skeleton hit Zalthir for 5 piercing damage, and Zalthir deflected it completely, saying "Great. No damage." [ch 015 / 015.07]
- Zalthir missed twice, prompting the DM to say "The universe does not like you," but he deflected another blow later [ch 015 / 015.07].
- The DM declared the skeletons dead: "you are now fourth level." [ch 015 / 015.07]
- The party stood amid the wreckage of Buppido's mad shrine; Buppido had killed Yuk Yuk, the party's goblin guide, and Thorin reflected "He was crazy, but he was our crazy." [ch 015 / 015.07; ch 015 / moment]
- The party took a moment to collect themselves, knowing Droki was still somewhere ahead and that deeper mysteries of the Whorlstone Tunnels and the evil Hgraam warned about still lay waiting [ch 015 / 015.07].
- The party defeated the skeletons in the Whorlstone Tunnels and advanced to 4th level before play resumed; Daz's Intelligence reached 19 and Gyrgum bumped a score to 19 [ch 016 / 016.01].
- A ghost with a cleaved head emerged from the ground; he introduced himself as Pelek, a deep gnome traveler from Blingdenstone, and expressed relief that the party had killed the derro nearby [ch 016 / 016.01].
- Pelek explained he was lured by business deals and murdered and dismembered by his former friend, the derro Buppido, who had grown convinced he was the avatar of a major deity; Pelek had been trapped for about a year [ch 016 / 016.01; ch 016 / npcs].
- Pelek requested the party collect his specific bones and return them to his ancestral home in Blingdenstone; Gyrgum wrapped the bones and stored them in his pack, binding Pelek's spirit to travel alongside them [ch 016 / 016.01].
- Thorin and Daz attempted to intimidate Pelek, Therin rolled a 1 and the attempts failed; Pelek offered that he "could try to possess one of you" [ch 016 / 016.01].
- Pelek advised the party on Blingdenstone: the drow Daz must be declared from the Overbright and unallied with Menzoberranzan, nothing associated with eight or divisible by eight, and no spiders; he said the deep gnomes know ways to the "Overbright" [ch 016 / 016.01].
- Pelek revealed the strange magic of the tunnels had animated his severed hand, which scuttled away through narrow crevices still wearing his obsidian ring [ch 016 / 016.01; ch 016 / items].
- The party followed Droki's messy trail of food scraps southeast to a massive cavern with a deep black pool spanning nearly a hundred feet across [ch 016 / 016.02].
- The party traced Droki's path to a solid rock wall riddled with narrow cracks too small to squeeze through; Pelek slipped through the cracks to scout ahead and reported the tunnel continued with nobody there [ch 016 / 016.02; ch 016 / 016.03].
- Gyrgum ate Pygmywort to shrink, but it only reduced him to Small while the cracks required Tiny; he took Bigwig to restore his size and stocked an ample supply of both mushrooms [ch 016 / 016.03].
- Daz froze successive sections of the dark pool with Shape Water, creating a temporary ice bridge the whole party walked across to the far shore [ch 016 / 016.03].
- The party entered a dense fungi forest of giant mushrooms and began foraging, gathering barrelstalks and bluecaps; Nilhogg's Nose was also mentioned [ch 016 / 016.04; ch 016 / items].
- Swarms of centipedes erupted from the fungi and ambushed the party; Zalthir was covered in biting insects [ch 016 / 016.04].
- The party fought the centipede swarms through the rest of the session, with Gyrgum using Tasha's Caustic Brew, Daz casting Toll the Dead, and Zalthir punching; the session ended mid-combat surrounded by swarms [ch 016 / 016.04].
- The next session, the party finished off the centipede swarms; Thorin used his new Sentinel feat, Gyrgum channeled radiant energy through his hand axe with True Strike, Daz fired magic missiles, and Thorin delivered the killing blow to the last cluster [ch 017 / 017.01].
- Gyrgum cast Prayer of Healing on Thorin, Daz, Zalthir, himself, and the ghost, granting each a short rest's benefit plus an additional 2d8 healing, allowing the party to keep moving [ch 017 / 017.02].
