- The party peered into a natural cave and saw three myconid adults and several sprouts dancing frantically to a silent tune, accompanied by two quaggoth spore servants covered in mushroom growths and yellow mold; they chose to leave it undisturbed [ch 017 / 017.03].
- A flumph appeared and spoke into the party's minds; it said it feeds on the psionic energy of evil creatures such as aboleths, gith, and mind flayers [ch 017 / 017.04].
- The flumph "trauma dumped" vivid psychic visions on the party: a shrine to Demogorgon, a broken magical shard being worked upon near a dark obelisk, and a stolen red dragon egg being subjected to cruel experiments [ch 017 / 017.04; ch 017 / npcs].
- The flumph revealed a derro savant named Plinki was torturing the stolen red dragon egg, trying to mutate the embryo into a two-headed pet and eventual grand mount for Demogorgon [ch 017 / 017.04; ch 017 / npcs].
- The flumph shared locations: the Assassin's Guild with a mind flayer it feeds on, the Council of Savants, and a leader-type figure named Zubriska always surrounded by four bodyguards [ch 017 / 017.04].
- The flumph lamented that its once "hippy-dippy" home had been corrupted since the faerzress blinked out and back some six or seven months ago; the flumph declared it would accompany the party [ch 017 / 017.04; ch 017 / npcs].
- The party left the Whorlstone Tunnels and returned to the Darklake District of Gracklstugh; the ghost stayed invisible to avoid panicking locals [ch 017 / 017.05].
- The party went to the Keepers of the Flame and met Gartokkar Xundorn, who pulled them aside into a separate chamber; the flumph projected an image of the egg into his mind [ch 017 / 017.05].
- Gartokkar tasked the party with retrieving the egg, a small statuette of Demogorgon, and any incriminating paperwork, which combined with the egg's inscriptions could persuade the Deepking to "wage unholy war" on the Gray Ghosts [ch 017 / 017.05].
- The party negotiated payment: Gartokkar offered 2,000 gold, rejected Gyrgum's eager acceptance of it as a full advance, and settled terms at 30% up front and 70% on delivery [ch 017 / 017.05].
- The party raised a confidentiality rider to keep the egg secret from Themberchaud; after a flash of murderous madness, Gartokkar agreed, muttering "What Themberchaud doesn't know doesn't hurt Themberchaud" [ch 017 / 017.05].
- Thorn demanded double for the discretion, Daz hinted at other bidders, and Gartokkar conceded an extra 1,000 gold; the upfront terms settled at 1,100 gold split evenly at 275 apiece, plus official badges for the party's companions [ch 017 / 017.05].
- Gartokkar delivered a fierce threat, enumerating every cave, rock, tunnel, and pool in the Underdark where the party might think to hide should they fail to deliver [ch 017 / 017.05].
- At session start the GM posted a corrected map revealing that the area previously marked as Mind Flayer territory is actually where the cult of Demogorgon and the assassins keep fighting each other [ch 018 / 018.01].
- The magic items the party had ordered would not arrive until the next morning, but Gyrgum judged himself "not in bad shape," so the party went to the Stone Guard straight away [ch 018 / 018.01].
- The party went to the Stone Guard headquarters with the invisible ghost and the flumph, the latter "very impressed with all the things that it's seeing and very concerned, but very excited" [ch 018 / 018.01].
- Errde Blackskull received them with thinly veiled charm barely concealing her seething intensity and demanded "do you have any useful information for me?" [ch 018 / 018.01].
- The party learned Errde wanted documented proof of Gray Ghosts' and Council of Savants' conspiracies, evidence of the Assassin's Guild targeting her clan, and evidence for the Deepking's sign-off on a "pacification mission" against the Derro — the more violent kind [ch 018 / 018.01].
- The party learned both the Keepers of the Flame and the Stone Guard knew Droki would lead them to the answers in the Whorlstone Caverns, and that neither faction could send people in because the Derro's paranoia and traps catch invisible intruders [ch 018 / 018.01].
- The party discussed the Deepking's erratic behavior and speculated about an "evil vizier," noting his grant of all weapons contracts to Clan Steelshadow bred resentment [ch 018 / 018.02].
- The party discussed Themberchaud, who has "started to grow up and noticed that maybe things aren't as they seem," which is why Gartokkar is frantic about the dragon egg [ch 018 / 018.02].
- The party deliberated whether to gather evidence, play factions against one another, or arm themselves and flee, with the GM noting the Keepers of the Flame had formally threatened permanent hostility if betrayed [ch 018 / 018.02].
- Thorin proposed a personal philosophy of 'radical candor', saying "I think just information wants to be free," and Gyrgum replied "That seems like a great way to get killed" [ch 018 / 018.02; ch 018 / moment].
- Daz conceded he has 19 Intelligence but only 10 Charisma (and 14 Wisdom), and the GM pointed out "Intelligence and being able to manipulate people are not necessarily the same thing" [ch 018 / 018.02].
- The party reviewed that their only paid job is to figure out what's going on with the egg, retrieve it, and figure out what's going on with the Council of Savants [ch 018 / 018.02].
- The ghost recalled that pre-asylum Buppido felt the Council of Savants "weren't committed to independence and freedom of the derro Society and were simply a mechanism of control by the duergar," and came to see himself on a "mission from God," summed up as "I am the force of history" [ch 018 / 018.02].
- Thorin delivered their report with radical candor while the flumph shared its psychic impressions; the flumph complained repeated trauma dumping had become "no longer as satisfying" [ch 018 / 018.03].
- Errde concluded the Gray Ghosts and Council of Savants were working in concert; Daz pushed back "I'm standing near you right now," and Errde conceded "you present a conundrum. Daz. Well played" [ch 018 / 018.03; ch 018 / moment].
- Errde demanded the party return to the Whorlstone Caverns with documented proof, agreeing to arm Jimjar, Spiderbait, and Eldeth with medium armor, short swords, shortbows, and shields [ch 018 / 018.03].
- The party kept the red dragon egg secret from Errde; she noticed the egg in the flumph's visions but could not imagine it was the lost egg, and Thorin rolled a 20 on his blurt save, holding his tongue [ch 018 / 018.03; ch 018 / moment].
- Errde approved Daz's cover story of the party visiting Clan Ironhead and Clan Xardelvar so it would appear they worked against the Deepking rather than for the Stone Guard [ch 018 / 018.03].
- The party visited Werz Saltbaron, who rewarded each member with an obsidian gemstone worth ten gold pieces and offered to cover their lodging expenses [ch 018 / 018.04].
- Daz perceived (insight 18) that Werz "knows way too much about working with assassins," and Werz admitted he might have helped "some clans sell some goods on the down low" [ch 018 / 018.04].
- Werz shared that the Deepking grew more paranoid because of his friend Shal, that people competing with his trade had been disappearing, and that Stone Guard members had recently begun taking bribes [ch 018 / 018.04].
- Werz attempted to hire the party to eliminate the assassins targeting him in exchange for future trade favors in Blingdenstone, with the condition "But with me still alive" [ch 018 / 018.04].
- Gyrgum offered an optional NDA which Werz heard as a "Non-Demonic Agreement"; Gyrgum stared at the ghost, who said "boo" [ch 018 / 018.05; ch 018 / moment].
- Daz set a list price of 1,250 gold, then offered a flat thousand; when Werz called it "highway robbery" and tried to walk it back, the price was raised to 1,100, which Werz paid [ch 018 / 018.05; ch 018 / moment].
- Werz gave the party five special gems as a sign of good faith, hinting they are of particular interest to gnomes and valued at 100 GP each [ch 018 / 018.05].
- Back at Ghohlbrorn's Lair, Jimjar identified the five gems as empty spell gems used in the magical defenses of Blingdenstone, noting the alchemist Kazook Pickshine would pay handsomely, and questioning how Werz obtained them [ch 018 / 018.06].
- Zalthir recalled an unhappy sprout near a group of strangely dancing myconids; Stool recognised it as his friend Rumpadump and pleaded with the party to drop everything and save him [ch 018 / 018.06].
- The party debated the nature of quaggoths and the ethics of myconid breeding, with Stool arguing "It's in their nature to be violent. They are bred to be violent," prompting Thorin's "That's eugenics" [ch 018 / 018.06; ch 018 / moment].
- Stool asked "Can we drop everything and go find out right now?" and the party agreed to look for Rumpadump on their way to deal with the assassins, since his location is along their route [ch 018 / 018.06].
- The party collected their ordered magical items: two enchanted tattoos for Zalthir and a +1 longsword for Thorin, at a 10% discount from pooled party funds [ch 018 / 018.06].
- The party visited Clan Ironhead as part of their cover; Thangus Ironhead publicly dismissed the Whorlstone Tunnels as "rumors for children," and Thorin replied "Okay, we'll go play there" [ch 018 / 018.07].
- Grinta Ironhead, Thangus's granddaughter, privately told the party that if the Deepking were unseated, Clan Ironhead — which would become the premier clan — would offer favorable trade terms to those who helped [ch 018 / 018.07].
- Jimjar noted such a deal would be enormously beneficial to the people of Blingdenstone [ch 018 / 018.07].
- The DM laid out the party's quest log: the Keepers of the Flame want only the egg, with none learning it was lost; Errde wants proof of derro involvement and knowledge of the assassins; Werz wants the assassins off his back in exchange for better trade deals with the gnomes; Errde's reward is support, return of the party's weapons, and "a pat on the back" [ch 019 / 019.01].
- Thorin proposed lying to everybody and escaping on the dragon, and the DM stressed the real challenge would be deciding who gets the information once the party has it [ch 019 / 019.01].
- The party returned to the Whorlstone Caverns, moving north toward the obelisk chamber where the egg was rumored to be held, with allies including Flumph, Pelek's ghost, Jimjar and Spiderbait [ch 019 / 019.01].
- Two duergar sentries, Grimgrim (nicknamed "Bob") and Brobrek (nicknamed "Rust"), intercepted the party in a hallway, declaring it the exclusive territory of the Empty-Scabbard Killers [ch 019 / 019.01].
- When the party mentioned their shared hatred of the Demogorgon cultists, Rust's eyes lit up with interest [ch 019 / 019.01].
- Daz, with a natural 20 on Arcana, recognized that the dragon egg in the obelisk chamber will become a two-headed dragon and has probably been possessed by demonic power [ch 019 / 019.01].
- The party learned the assassins share a common enemy: the derro who have allied with Demogorgon [ch 019 / 019.01].
- Rust escorted the group to meet the assassins' boss, a formidable duergar soulblade named Eldgrim, whose chamber was guarded by several more soulblades [ch 019 / 019.02].
- Eldgrim's first offer was only free passage through his territory to reach the derro; after negotiation, a deal was struck: if the party eliminated the Demogorgon worshippers, Eldgrim would remove Werz from the assassins' list of targets free of charge [ch 019 / 019.02].
- Daz negotiated with Eldgrim to have Werz removed from the assassins' hit list; Eldgrim initially quoted 200 gold pieces to "whitelist" Werz, or 20 gold pieces if the party dealt with his Demogorgon problem [ch 019 / 019.02].
- Eldgrim scoffed at the idea of proof, saying the assassins carry no talismans and even a signed paper would be dismissed as a forgery [ch 019 / 019.02].
- Daz flattered Eldgrim with talk of writing a book and persuaded the assassin leader to walk to his desk and sign his name on parchment [ch 019 / 019.03].
- Thorin, genuinely baffled by the logic of killers who carried empty scabbards, blurted out his confusion aloud, prompting one of the soulblades to manifest a shimmering psionic weapon in wordless explanation [ch 019 / 019.03].
- While the room's attention was pulled away, Daz rifled through the papers on Eldgrim's desk and slipped away a document bearing the royal seal of Gracklstugh and the signature of the Deepking himself, proving the crown was secretly in league with the assassins [ch 019 / 019.03].
- The DM warned that "there's a clock," though the assassins would not work it out "like tomorrow" [ch 019 / 019.03].
- Daz staged a violent coughing fit, and when Zalthir came over to help him he thumped the dragonborn hard in the chest, Zalthir's cue to breathe a gout of fire directly onto the desk, igniting the remaining papers [ch 019 / 019.03].
- Eldgrim erupted in fury, cursing the party and ordering them out of his territory under threat of death; the party exited, passing a pair of invisible guards who fist-bumped them [ch 019 / 019.03].
- Thorin proposed "Plausible deniability" as the group's name [ch 019 / 019.03].
- At an intersection, the party chose to bypass the Council of Savants entirely, slipping past the mad derro scholars without incident [ch 019 / 019.04].
- Zalthir conjured a sphere of impenetrable magical darkness to trail behind the group, cloaking their approach in shadow as they crept northward through the tunnels [ch 019 / 019.04].
- Flumph, who had probed their minds after the assassins' lair and found "no trauma," admitted he was disappointed: "I was really expecting to see at least some of you have your brains devoured" [ch 019 / 019.04].
- The party arrived at the entrance to the obelisk chamber, a vast cavern with faerzress energy, bioluminescent fungi, a central rocky mesa with ramps and a bridge, atop which sat the dragon egg and a fifty-foot-tall obelisk of smooth black metal [ch 019 / 019.05].
- A female derro named Plinki stroked the obelisk with reverent intensity, squealing with delight each time it flashed, scribbling notes and paying no attention to the party; she had a crowd of minions, some possibly invisible or hidden [ch 019 / 019.05].
- Gyrgum, drawn irresistibly toward the egg by his devotion to Bahamut, strode forward and announced himself as a cleric of the dragon god come to worship at the egg [ch 019 / 019.05].
- Plinki declared that Demogorgon himself had sent this holy man to acknowledge the greatness of her achievement and scrambled down from the mesa to greet him [ch 019 / 019.05].
- Plinki revealed the egg had been stolen from the Keepers of the Flame and she had spent untold hours using the obelisk's power to infuse it with demonic energy in honor of Demogorgon, showing him the intricate incantations inscribed across the egg's surface [ch 019 / 019.05].
- Plinki revealed that the final ingredient to complete her ritual was the beating heart of a cleric of Bahamut, instantly turning the encounter hostile [ch 019 / 019.06].
- The table debated what a cleric of Bahamut would do about a demon-corrupted egg; the DM consulted Gemini, which named four camps: destroy it, cleanse it, study it, or seek guidance while holding it [ch 019 / 019.06].
- Gyrgum grandstanded loudly, warning Plinki and her followers that Daz would not hesitate to smash the egg if they did not stand down, since a drow could never be trusted [ch 019 / 019.06].
- The cultists hesitated, but Plinki's madness won out, and she screamed for her followers to attack [ch 019 / 019.06].
- Zalthir moved first, seizing Plinki in an iron grip, pinning her in place before unleashing a rapid and brutal series of strikes; his first attack totalled 27 against her AC 13, and he spent a focus point for another strike, dealing 11 damage [ch 019 / 019.07].
- Gyrgum channeled a surge of devastating necrotic energy directly into Plinki, a 2nd-level Inflict Wounds; she succeeded on a Constitution save and still took 12 damage [ch 019 / 019.07].
- The session paused mid-combat [ch 019 / 019.07].
- The party found themselves in a dark cavern beneath the Underdark, protecting a mysterious egg from Derro cultists; Plinki, grappled by Zalthir from chapter 19, had the crazed derro fixed on killing Gyrgum and dropping his heart over the egg [ch 020 / 020.01].
- The GM read derro lore aloud: they cooperate "out of necessity and when riled up by a charismatic leader," and warned "expecting to have a rational conversation may be too much" [ch 020 / 020.01].
- Gyrgum stated he wanted to protect the egg but was willing to steal it, and the GM noted Daz already had a plan to steal it [ch 020 / 020.01].
- A Derro spy emerged from the shadows and severely wounded Eldeth with a crossbow bolt, dealing 12 damage and dropping her to four hit points [ch 020 / 020.01].
- A second spy attempted to shoot Daz but missed [ch 020 / 020.01].
- Thorin engaged a Derro warrior with his longsword, learning his new Sentinel feat; blindsight in the darkness gave him advantage and his Sap mastery imposed disadvantage on the target's next attack [ch 020 / 020.01].
- A spectator, a multi-eyed beholder-like creature, suddenly appeared and joined the fray, spending all its movement to arrive [ch 020 / 020.01].
- Daz conjured a giant earthen hand that restrained the spectator, dealing 16 damage, then retreated into the darkness [ch 020 / 020.01].
- Gyrgum called upon a ghostly ally to protect the egg, and the ghost acted on his turn "because you have the bones" [ch 020 / 020.01].
- Zalthir unleashed a 15-foot cone breath attack, killing Plinki outright with a DC 11 Dexterity save; the damage die rolled low, but it was enough to finish her, and the blinded condition hit survivors, others taking one or two points [ch 020 / 020.01].
- With Plinki dead, a derro Master Thief proved tenacious, shrugging off blows as Zalthir repeatedly grappled and wrestled him; Thorin's sentinel instincts rooted the thief in place when he tried to slip away [ch 020 / 020.02].
- Daz used a burst of teleportation magic to vanish from the fray, secretly concealing the egg beneath his robes as the Derro cultists scrambled blindly in the darkness [ch 020 / 020.02].
- Zalthir grappled the Master Thief and maneuvered him toward Thorin, dropping him off a ledge for an additional 16 damage and prone [ch 020 / 020.02].
- The ghost's first possession attempt on the Master Thief failed when he made his saving throw [ch 020 / 020.02].
- Gyrgum unleashed Tasha's Caustic Brew, coating several enemies in corrosive fluid; DC 14 Dex save, one of three failed and took 7 [ch 020 / 020.02].
- A spy climbed up, sensed the ghost, "freaked out," attacked it and missed; a derro attacked Thorin and missed [ch 020 / 020.02].
- Thorin killed derro #6 for 7 damage [ch 020 / 020.02].
- The spectator broke free from the earthen grasp on a lucky Strength roll of 17, then the party helpers hit it twice, leaving it at one hit point [ch 020 / 020.02].
- Daz fired a second-level Magic Missile, killing one Derro and wounding another for 9 damage [ch 020 / 020.02].
- Daz climbed the stairs and picked up the mysterious egg; the Derro scrambled in the darkness realizing it was taken, one screaming "where is the egg?" [ch 020 / 020.02].
- The crazed Derro shrieked that whoever held the egg held absolute power, one cultist howling for the sacrifice of Gyrgum, demanding his heart be cut out and fed to a dark entity [ch 020 / 020.03].
- The ghost responded to the sacrifice threat with a terrifying voice and the cultists faltered; Gyrgum unleashed streams of corrosive acid coating the fumbling Derro [ch 020 / 020.03].
- An enemy assailed Gyrgum, knocking the cleric to the ground with six damage; he failed a DC 9 Strength save and was knocked prone [ch 020 / 020.03].
- Zalthir relentlessly attacked and grappled the Master Thief, dropping him off the ledge again for 6 damage; GM (as the thief): "what the?" [ch 020 / 020.03].
- Gyrgum recovered his footing and unleashed Caustic Brew on #2 and #4, both failing and taking five each [ch 020 / 020.03].
- A hidden spy became terrified by the spectral presence and attempted to flee the cavern; Gyrgum's player noted "he's been revealed as a spy, and that ruins his whole business" [ch 020 / 020.03].
- Thorin hit the Master Thief for 13 after being told the thief still had 30 hit points [ch 020 / 020.03].
- The party helpers all fired short crossbows "and they all spectacularly" miss; Eldeth hung back: "I don't want to get killed" [ch 020 / 020.03].
- Daz utilized teleportation magic to escape, safely hiding the mysterious egg beneath his robes [ch 020 / 020.03].
- Enemies rallied against Gyrgum, shouting to eliminate the cleric and knocking him prone once more, only one hit landing for a single point [ch 020 / 020.03].
- Zalthir delivered the killing blow to the Master Thief, ending the rogue's stubborn resistance [ch 020 / 020.04].
- Zalthir struck a Derro warrior and pushed him off a ten-foot ledge, dealing 10 damage plus the fall's d6 rolling a 6, killing him instantly in a moment compared to the film 300 [ch 020 / 020.04].
- The Master Thief attempted to strike the ghost but was stopped by Thorin's sentinel ability, which halved his movement after an opportunity attack dealt 11 damage [ch 020 / 020.04].
- Gyrgum scrambled to his feet and used Healing Word (2d4) on himself, which worked "in a creature you can see" [ch 020 / 020.04].
- Thorin cut down another Derro with his longsword for 12 damage and moved to block the path of the remaining enemies [ch 020 / 020.04].
- The party helpers finally hit the spy once for four; the GM called them Keystone Cops [ch 020 / 020.04].
- The ghost recharged possession [ch 020 / 020.04].
- Daz used a magical earthen hand to restrain the spy, who took 8 damage but broke free and maneuvered to retaliate [ch 020 / 020.04].
- Three Derro swung at Thorin screaming "the egg, the egg" — "Amazingly, all three of them missed" [ch 020 / 020.04].
- Zalthir grabbed and killed two more Derro, saving his last focus point [ch 020 / 020.04].
- Daz cried out for assistance as he was cornered by the spy — "somebody help" [ch 020 / 020.04].
- The ghost ally flew across the battlefield with 40 feet of fly speed and successfully possessed the spy, incapacitating him and taking control of his body with a DC 13 Charisma save [ch 020 / 020.04].
- The possessed spy did not fight — "He's just got possession of a new body. He doesn't want to let go of it" [ch 020 / 020.04].
- Gyrgum navigated the darkness to strike a remaining enemy with his mace for three, and Thorin then killed the last Derro for nine [ch 020 / 020.04].
- The session ended the moment the last Derro fell, with play paused until after the 13th while the GM travels to Greece [ch 020 / 020.04].
- The party, still holding the dragon's egg and with the ghost Pelek inhabiting the derro spy's body while they kept his bones, turned to investigating the corrupted chamber and gathering the notes and documents there [ch 021 / 021.01].
- The party discovered numerous notes linking the Council of Savants to nefarious activities and corruption [ch 021 / 021.01].
- The party deliberated on which faction to deliver their gathered intelligence to, weighing the interests of Themberchaud, the Keepers of the Flame, and the Stone Guard; the DM confirmed "everybody's paying you to find it out" [ch 021 / 021.01].
- Gyrgum used his religious knowledge (Religion +5, rolling an 18) after a failed Nature check to determine the dragon's egg would hatch on its own in forty-four weeks [ch 021 / 021.02].
- Gyrgum learned lesser restoration would not lift the demonic corruption from the egg, which would require research, and resolved to consult his superiors as a follower of Bahamut [ch 021 / 021.02].
- Thorin wished aloud for a baby dragon party member, unintentionally slighting Zalthir the dragonborn, and apologised [ch 021 / 021.02].
- The party decided to return the egg to Gartokkar and the Keepers of the Flame, telling them they found no evidence of who was behind the theft, to secure safe passage and trade rights for the gnomes [ch 021 / 021.02; ch 021 / 021.03].
- The party chose to withhold the incriminating list connecting the Deepking to the Assassin's Guild from Errde Blackskull [ch 021 / 021.03].
- The party looted the chamber, finding 170 gold pieces and a heavy baby rattle inscribed with the name of Clan Thrazgad, crafted from materials too durable for an infant's toy, which they recognised was made for a dragon hatchling to gnaw on [ch 021 / 021.03].
- The party encountered four Derro guards at a heavy double door with an alarm mechanism; Thorin's die roll decided between killing and intimidation, landing on intimidation [ch 021 / 021.04].
- Daz conjured floating lights and Gyrgum used thaumaturgy to create ground tremors; Daz told the guards "You do not know who we are," and Thorin told them to tell everybody what happened [ch 021 / 021.04].
- The party voted on a group name and declared itself the Ember Vanguard [ch 021 / 021.04].
- Daz rolled a 20 on group intimidation (Thorin failed); the Derro mistook the ember-like lights for a meteor swarm and became convinced they faced a 20th-level drow archmage and his elite retinue [ch 021 / 021.04].
- The party let the four Derro guards go alive to spread the legend of the Ember Vanguard [ch 021 / 021.04].
- The party chose to skirt the assassins' territory entirely and headed west through the Underdark passages, setting aside Stool's plea to help his Myconid friend [ch 021 / 021.05].
- A Trapper, a manta-ray-shaped predator, ambushed the party from the rear and wrapped its wings around Gyrgum, smothering him in darkness and acid [ch 021 / 021.06].
- Gyrgum was driven to a single hit point, using his orcish ability to drop to 1 instead of 0, and failed his escape roll with a 9 [ch 021 / 021.06].
- Daz fired magic missiles into the Trapper and cast Silvery Barbs and Toll the Dead; the Trapper resisted [ch 021 / 021.06].
- Thorin landed heavy longsword blows, including a Hill Strike for 4 extra damage and a Sap mastery giving the creature disadvantage [ch 021 / 021.06].
- Zalthir seized the Trapper with his bare hands in a counter-grapple, landing repeated strikes, and beat the creature to death, releasing a battered and acid-covered Gyrgum [ch 021 / 021.06].
- The Ember Vanguard stopped in the dim tunnels beneath Gracklstugh to tend their wounds; Gyrgum used Channel Divinity to heal himself for 14 hit points and gave the remaining 6 to the badly wounded dwarf companion Eldeth Feldrun, bringing both to about 20 hit points [ch 022 / 022.01].
- The GM relabelled the group in Roll20 from "the party and allies" to "the Ember Vanguard and Groupies" [ch 022 / 022.01].
- The party advanced cautiously through the cavern system beneath Gracklstugh; Daz insisted that "anytime there's a turn cavern, I think it's a good idea to be careful" [ch 022 / 022.01].
- The party entered a vast chamber reeking of death and decomposed flesh, with faerzress glow drifting like fog and a deep pit full of zombies; a lone derro cultist named Skiit stood on a ledge tossing body parts into the pit, complaining he always got "the low end of the stick" [ch 022 / 022.02].
- Daz rolled a 20 on a religion check and recognized Skiit as a low-ranking, true-believing member of the Cult of Demogorgon who needs someone else to think for him, insane from mind flayer experiments and demonic influence [ch 022 / 022.02].
- Gyrgum posed as a visiting supervisor, asking about the "big project" and the work of Ulnara and pressing Skiit to keep his zombies "on plan"; the ruse worked for a while, and Skiit even asked permission to feed the party's goblin companion to the zombies [ch 022 / 022.02].
- The bluff collapsed when Gyrgum rolled a natural one; Skiit boasted of Demogorgon and Plinki and accused the party of spying for the duergar or the mind flayers [ch 022 / 022.02].
- Gyrgum shoved Skiit off the ledge into the pit while Daz used minor illusion to dim the area and fill the air with floating icons of Demogorgon, giving the party an aura of dark authority [ch 022 / 022.02].
- Skiit's screaming drew a derro savant named Ulnara and a hulking zombie out of the back of the chamber from about 80 feet away; she demanded to know who the strangers were [ch 022 / 022.02].
- The party answered with baffling confidence, invoking the name "Ember Vanguard" and offering to look up their employers in a "database," leading to a debate about inner and outer joins; Ulnara was left momentarily speechless [ch 022 / 022.02; ch 022 / moment].
- Daz cast Maximilian's Earthen Grasp on Skiit at the bottom of the pit and crushed him for 8 damage; Ulnara changed tone, called Skiit a disappointment to "your lordship," and promised to raise him as a zombie [ch 022 / 022.02].
- When Skiit broke free and ran, Daz finished him with Toll the Dead; Ulnara thanked the party for the "excellent supplies" and asked them to tell Plinki what a wonderful ally she had been, then let them pass [ch 022 / 022.02].
- The GM noted that if the party had entered from the other side, past a marked line on the map, the zombies would have attacked on sight [ch 022 / 022.02].
- The party took a short rest, which let Zalthir reset his focus points, then pushed through a set of large double doors they had listened at and found cool to the touch, coming to a branching passage [ch 022 / 022.03].
- To the east, the party looked into a chamber with three enormous cave bears resting in cages of scrap iron, with a spiral path marked by small stone pylons winding toward a tent and a large cauldron at the center; two insane derro stood at the cauldron talking while ignoring each other [ch 022 / 022.03].
- One derro grumbled about endless contributions for a "prince" who was "always hungry," and the other recited a cryptic poem about a scale "smooth as polished obsidian" that "came from the deep, deep, deep," from a place where a voice sang of two heads, then corrected himself and started again [ch 022 / 022.04; ch 022 / moment].
- The treasure in the tent was unknown and the bears a real risk, so the Ember Vanguard quietly withdrew and took the western path, with Gyrgum suggesting they could "come back here another time" [ch 022 / 022.04].
- About 100 feet down the western path, a derro patrol of four spotted the party; the party tried a casual stroll past, helped by a deception roll of 21, but it ended when a guard asked whether they worshipped the dual-headed master and Gyrgum declared his devotion to Bahamut, causing the derro to attack at once [ch 022 / 022.05; ch 022 / moment].
- In the fight, Thorin won initiative with a 19 and hit hard with his longsword, his Sap mastery leaving his target at disadvantage; Zalthir moved magical darkness over two enemies and scored a critical unarmed strike from inside it, leaving one at a single hit point; Daz split magic missiles across the patrol; Gyrgum hit them with Tasha's Caustic Brew [ch 022 / 022.05].
- Thorin took up a Guardian stance and cut down a derro that tried to go after Zalthir; the GM commented "he chose poorly" [ch 022 / 022.05].
- Gyrgum parried a hooked spear while debating theology with his attacker, cast True Strike, and killed the last standing derro with a bone-crushing blow of his mace, remarking with quiet arrogance that one head was indeed better than two [ch 022 / 022.05; ch 022 / moment].
- The party pressed on to an intersection and chose to explore a large chamber smelling of brimstone and chemicals; Gyrgum, with a passive perception of 14, spotted a hidden pit trap near the entrance and warned the others, so everyone got past it safely [ch 022 / 022.06].
- The cavern was lit by the hellish red glow of faerzress swirling in spiral patterns on the walls, dripping water made no echo, and thick fungi covered the floors and walls; the party avoided detection by a nearby death dog and a group of derro [ch 022 / 022.06].
- At the center stood a five-foot natural platform with a glowing ritual circle and books with pages made from trillimac mushroom caps, filled with mad scribblings, laid out almost but not quite in a line; the party recognized these as possible physical evidence for Errde Blackskull to expose a demonic conspiracy within the Council of Savants [ch 022 / 022.06].
- The cult leader Narrak picked up one of the books and declared their experiments served the elevation of the derro, the triumph of Demogorgon, and the destruction of the duergar, ordered his crowding followers to stand back since none could read it, and announced he had found "the spell we need," that "All we need is parts" [ch 022 / 022.06; ch 022 / moment].
- The session ended with the Ember Vanguard watching from the shadows, knowing the most dangerous part of their mission was only just beginning [ch 022 / 022.06].
- The party crept into a vast, foul-smelling cavern reeking of brimstone and fungi, finding five Derro cultists chanting around a pulsing green ritual circle, a two-foot-tall stone giant statue sprouting a second head, a caged death dog, and a massive female ettin named Grula-Munga lurking in a fungal alcove [ch 023 / 023.01].
- Zalthir confirmed this was the same cult behind the demonic infusion of the dragon egg; the party pieced together the ritual likely caused the two-headed stone giant that rampaged through the market four days earlier [ch 023 / 023.01].
- Daz's arcana check (14 vs 16) and Gyrgum's first religion check (10) fell short, but on a second religion roll of 21, Gyrgum was inspired by Bahamut and surmised the statue is a focal point for the curse and that bringing one to Stonespeaker Hgraam could allow him to reverse it [ch 023 / 023.01].
- The party retreated to Buppido's former resting place to take a long rest, carrying Stool, who fell asleep after pressing them to help Rumpadump [ch 023 / 023.01; ch 023 / 023.02].
- Daz recalled from his textbook *The Undead Horde* that the possession was stable unless the host was knocked unconscious, but withheld this, reasoning that keeping the untrustworthy newcomer bound was good policy [ch 023 / 023.02].
- Jimjar bet five gold pieces that Pelek would not escape his bonds by morning; Gyrgum bet he wouldn't, and Jimjar paid up with "well done, well done" [ch 023 / 023.02].
- Returning to the ritual cavern refreshed, the party found Narrak had completed a second head on one statue and was preparing the third, announcing that converting another of Hgraam's giants "by golly, that will destroy Gracklstugh" [ch 023 / 023.03].
- Gyrgum quietly cast Bless on his three companions (not himself), and the party beat the cultists' passive perception of 7 on a group stealth check, earning a surprise round [ch 023 / 023.03; ch 023 / 023.04].
- The ambush began with Zalthir's sphere of magical darkness and dragonborn fire breath; flames scored four enemies, and Gyrgum unleashed Tasha's Caustic Brew onto four cultists [ch 023 / 023.04].
- Zalthir briefly felt madness from the ritual circle but steadied himself with monastic focus; Thorin charged a cultist instead of the circle [ch 023 / 023.04].
- Daz's scorching ray killed one of the tougher Derro and seared Grula-Munga for 8 damage [ch 023 / 023.04].
- Narrak retaliated with a Fear spell (DC 14), sending Thorin, Gyrgum, and Zalthir fleeing; Gyrgum had blessed everyone but himself and failed his save [ch 023 / 023.04; ch 024 / 024.02].
- Grula-Munga's battle axe missed Thorin but her morning star hit him for 14 damage [ch 023 / 023.04].
- Zalthir dashed away and repositioned the darkness to block Narrak's line of sight, allowing the frightened party to recover; he activated his Eldritch Maul tattoo empowering his melee attacks to reach 15 feet [ch 023 / 023.04].
- Gyrgum's Guiding Bolt marked Grula-Munga for Thorin's follow-up longsword strike with advantage for 16 damage, leaving her at 54 hit points [ch 023 / 023.04].
- At the DM's suggestion, Daz cast Maximilian's Earthen Grasp, a massive hand of stone seizing Narrak, breaking his concentration and ending the Fear; the session paused mid-combat [ch 023 / 023.04].
- In chapter 024, a derro warrior charged out of the darkness, drove a hooked spear into Gyrgum for 2 damage, knocking him prone on a failed DC 9 Strength save, and shattering his concentration, ending his caustic brew [ch 024 / 024.01].
- Zalthir dragged his sphere of darkness over the ettin, his tattoo transforming his arms and face into inky tentacles lashing 15 feet into the darkness, grappling Grula-Munga and hitting for 8 and 5, then 12 with a focus point, bringing her to 41 hit points [ch 024 / 024.02].
- Gyrgum stood, exhaled a stream of caustic acid over all three remaining derro (two dodged, one was fully coated), while Daz stepped out of the darkness and killed one cultist with all three Magic Missile darts [ch 024 / 024.02].
- Zalthir's tentacles and Thorin's longsword wore down the ettin; Zalthir's tentacle strikes of 11 and 11 finally slew Grula-Munga [ch 024 / 024.03].
- Gyrgum's acid-coated derro died as it took 6 more acid damage from the brew; Daz crushed Narrak with the earthen hand for 5 damage after he failed his Strength save [ch 024 / 024.03].
- Thorin used blindsight to attack Narrak despite the darkness and used Second Wind as a bonus action to heal 11; Narrak's spear thrust at Thorin failed while restrained [ch 024 / 024.03].
- The last derro cultists were defeated, Narrak's silence ended, and a lone figure remained in the background "very much trying really hard at this point to not be noticed" [ch 024 / 024.03].
- The party searched the chamber, finding *The Rituals of the Two-Headed Beast* text, and Gyrgum prayed to Bahamut before retrieving a key from around Narrak's neck [ch 024 / 024.04].
- Thorin used the key to cautiously open the iron chest, finding 45 gold and 15 silver pieces and five doses of Keoghtom's Ointment, which the party entered into its shared inventory [ch 024 / 024.04].
- The party discussed the political implications of killing two Council of Savants members, reviewed its evidence (king's collusion with assassins, a red dragon egg, and passes to walk out), and Jimjar bet they'd want to hold onto some of it later [ch 024 / 024.04].
- Stool reminded the party they still needed to rescue Rumpadump, urging them to "just open the cell," and the session ended mid-conversation [ch 024 / 024.04].
- The party took stock of their haul inside the Whorlstone Caverns: a two-headed dragon egg, proof of derro demonic alignment, proof the Deepking allied with assassins, proof Themberchaud is "Themberchaud the 28th," and a ritual statue tied to the stone-giant second-head affliction [ch 025 / 025.01].
- The party discussed what to do with their evidence; Jimjar advised holding onto everything for future leverage, Zalthir proposed a magical dead man's switch, and Thorin suggested visiting the merchant they had helped but worried about betrayal [ch 025 / 025.01].
- Stool threw a tantrum, threatening to go alone to find his friend Rumpadump, and tried to win Thorin over by declaring him "much nicer than Zalthir" [ch 025 / 025.02].
- Zalthir objected to visiting Rumpadump, recalling infected creatures including "a massive ape creature that looked not particularly friendly"; Stool reassured the party that spores require death before zombification and that myconids only calm and talk [ch 025 / 025.02].
- Daz recalled myconid lore: peaceable walking mushrooms used as drow target practice, spores that stun within 5 feet, communication within 20 feet, and bludgeoning fists [ch 025 / 025.02].
- Gyrgum cast Aid on three party members, granting 5 temporary hit points for eight hours, and the party followed Stool deeper into the caverns [ch 025 / 025.02].
- The party arrived at a natural cave where several myconids danced frantically, with two large ape-like creatures covered in yellow mold and mushroom growths hunched nearby [ch 025 / 025.03].
- Sarith became agitated upon seeing the dancing myconids and lurched toward them wanting to join; Zalthir grappled him and pulled him back around the corner [ch 025 / 025.03].
- Rumpadump appeared calm and studious; Stool erupted with excitement ("I found you. I found you. I found you."), and Rumpadump said "something is not right here" [ch 025 / 025.03; ch 025 / 025.04].
- Rumpadump explained that a myconid named Voosbur released strange spores back in the Neverlight Grove, after which the group walked through some fungi and materialized in the Whorlstone Caverns [ch 025 / 025.04].
- Rumpadump avoided madness by not eating "the silly mushrooms," which proved to be the same growy-shrinky mushrooms the party had gathered; Stool disputed this theory [ch 025 / 025.04].
- Rumpadump urged the party to help him return to the Neverlight Grove, noting the Myconid Sovereign might know a way out of the Underdark [ch 025 / 025.04].
- Sarith began speaking about a Dark Lady calling to him and showing visions; the party's arcana checks failed, but Gyrgum's religion check of 23 identified the Dark Lady as Zuggtmoy, the Demon Queen of Fungi, Lady of Rot and Decay [ch 025 / 025.04].
- The party theorized Sarith was infected by Zuggtmoy's influence before they met him in prison, and noted it seemed "entirely possible that Zuggtmoy is also walking around" after seeing Demogorgon's influence [ch 025 / 025.04].
- The party approached the infected myconid Voosbur, who spoke euphorically about the Lady's dream and invited them to "drop in, tune out, and dance with the spores" [ch 025 / 025.05].
- Thorin, after a die roll, took Voosbur's outstretched hand and entered the vision; Gyrgum followed as the "designated driver," while Daz declined and Zalthir stayed back holding Sarith [ch 025 / 025.05].
- Thorin and Gyrgum found themselves in a bioluminescent spore glade where glowing fungi pulsed brighter when touched [ch 025 / 025.06].
- They passed into the Vine Wood, a maze of slime-covered sentient vines that began advancing; with a group stealth roll of 7, the pair ran and barely escaped as the vines collapsed behind them [ch 025 / 025.06].
- They emerged into a grim clearing with a fungal throne and mushroom sentinels, where a spectral Zuggtmoy appeared and offered the gift of joining her "endless mycelium" forever [ch 025 / 025.07].
- Thorin felt keen temptation but refused the offer, saying he had too many responsibilities in the waking world; Zuggtmoy lamented and the vision dissolved [ch 025 / 025.07].
- Voosbur told Thorin "Hey, man, I guess you didn't want to join the party. I got to go now," and he and the infected myconids stepped through a spore cloud and vanished [ch 025 / 025.08].
- The party took a long rest and rose to fifth level; Daz gained Fireball, and Thorin's longsword became +8 to hit with an extra attack [ch 025 / 025.08].
- After three days underground, the party emerged to find Gracklstugh's gates sealed, guarded by grim Duergar; the drow on the near side were even more on edge [ch 025 / 025.08].
- A guard named Gorglak blocked the party and demanded to inspect all of their possessions for contraband, ending the session on a cliffhanger [ch 025 / 025.08].
- The party, now at 5th level, with Gyrgum gaining third-level spells, Daz gaining Fireball, and Thorin gaining a second attack and Tactical Shift, prepared to exit Gracklstugh through the West Cleft Exit, a narrow, suffocating corridor under heavy Duergar lockdown [ch 026 / 026.01; ch 026 / 026.06].
- A guard at the West Cleft Exit identified each party member's species one by one — drow, orc, dwarf, "not a red dragonborn" — and concluded they "must not be the Ember Vanguard" [ch 026 / 026.01].
- The guard passed the party to Captain Gorglak, a scarred Duergar wearing a clenched-fist pendant, which Zalthir's history reading identified as a mark of loyalty to the Deepking [ch 026 / 026.01].
- The party chose their Stone Guard passes over their Keeper of the Flame passes; their Stone Guard task had been to find proof that the derro were engaged in demonic possession [ch 026 / 026.01].
- Zalthir spotted five invisible Duergar soldiers positioned throughout the hallway, ready to strike if negotiations collapsed [ch 026 / 026.01; ch 026 / memorable], while the captain "posed as standing alone" [ch 026 / npcs].
- Daz prepared a contingency plan, seeing through Gorglak as a corrupt official angling for a bribe; he would carry the egg and other incriminating items, cast Misty Step past the guards, and claim divine favor [ch 026 / 026.01; ch 026 / spells].
- Gyrgum recalled Bahamut ritual details to gain advantage on the Stone Guard's secret salute, performed it with conviction, and Gorglak conceded, "I see that you are clearly working for the stone guard. You clearly are not the Ember Vanguard" [ch 026 / 026.01; ch 026 / 026.02].
- Gorglak demanded a 20 gp bribe (five each): "it would be a shame if we had to inspect you to find some coin to make this worth their while"; the party paid, the hidden soldiers stood down, and the gates opened [ch 026 / 026.02].
- Gorglak warned that the Deepking feared a drow raiding party while the derro were convinced the Deepking was behind the unrest, and advised the party to "do it quickly and leave" [ch 026 / 026.02].
- In Laduguer's Furrow, Jimjar bet Thorin a gold piece that Thorin would do something embarrassing; Thorin promptly "accidentally" let his belt unbuckle and his pants fall, delighting Jimjar; the DM ruled small wagers are "fake money" unless reaching hundreds [ch 026 / 026.03; ch 026 / memorable].
- The party decided to visit the stone giants first, with Thorin reasoning "the giants seem to be more even-keeled of the group of all these factions" [ch 026 / 026.03].
- Near the Clan Thrazgad forge, the party paid a gray-skinned Duergar informant one gold piece (which Thorin had Jimjar pay from his lost wager) for two warnings: the Stone Guard was paranoid about strangers, and the Ember Vanguard was blamed throughout the city for the assassination of several members of the derro High Council [ch 026 / 026.03].
- The party realized the "High Council" members were the derro they had killed: the one making the two-headed giants and the one perverting the dragon egg [ch 026 / 026.03].
- The party learned Gracklstugh's good inn is reserved for duergar, while a not-so-good inn serves everyone else [ch 026 / 026.03].
- At Cairngorm Cavern, the party found Hgraam dealing with another brother afflicted with the two-headed curse, and Thorin presented the two-headed statue recovered from the Derro [ch 026 / 026.04].
- Hgraam probed whether the derro ruling council was behind the curse; the party stayed vague about council involvement, and Thorin's persuasion roll (13, with advantage as a giant-friend) held [ch 026 / 026.04].
- Hgraam accepted the statue gravely, declaring "with this statue I shall be able to cure," pressed 500 gold pieces into the party's hands, warned that the Stone Guard was becoming agitated, and closed the cavern to outsiders while he consulted the stones [ch 026 / 026.04].
- Thorin asked Hgraam whether he knew a way to the Overbright; Hgraam said he did not know where it is or whether it exists, though "little people" have told him it does [ch 026 / 026.04].
- The party moved toward Gartokkar's residence, with Zalthir casting Darkness low over the group (which he could see through via Shadow Arts) to shroud their movements through the smog, giving them advantage on stealth [ch 026 / 026.05].
- At Gartokkar's residence, the party handed over the corrupted red dragon egg, covered in ritualistic markings, declining to clean them off; Zalthir noted "Here's the egg. We rescued it from a mysterious ritual" [ch 026 / 026.06].
- Gartokkar expressed surprise at the party's survival, having assumed they were caught in the crossfire; he remembered Daz as a mere third-level drow wizard [ch 026 / 026.06].
- Gartokkar explained the egg's purpose: to replace Themberchaud before the beast realized he was not truly in control of Gracklstugh, as red dragons turn violent once they learn they are not in charge [ch 026 / 026.06].
- Gartokkar paid the party their remaining fee of 2,400 gp, covering the original 2,000 gp deal (600 gp up front) plus a 1,000 gp confidentiality rider; the party's spreadsheet showed 1,042 gp plus one electrum each [ch 026 / 026.06; ch 026 / items].
- The party learned the derro's history: mind flayer experiments left them unstable, the Blink pushed them into madness, their High Council had been co-opted by the Deepking, and after the Blink they turned to Demogorgon rituals to "rise up and strike them down" [ch 026 / 026.06].
- The party joked about new names to escape the Ember Vanguard reputation — "The Gold Vanguard," "The Gold Ember Vanguard," "The Disciples of Bahamut" — with Thorin's suggestion falling flat [ch 026 / 026.06].
- The party prepared to sneak toward the Darklake District, with Thorin saying "I feel like the whole thing's about to blow up... And I would like to hear what happens from afar" [ch 026 / 026.06].
- The party's stealth attempt failed (the group missed DC 14, despite Zalthir rolling 24), and a Derro assassin confronted them, declaring that the Derro Council had sent its Gray Ghosts to exact revenge for the deaths of their High Council members [ch 026 / 026.07].
- The derro claimed they knew who the party was, "even if the rest of this city doesn't know who it is," maybe having told the city otherwise "so that we would get our revenge" [ch 026 / 026.07].
- Zalthir swiftly cut down the visible leader and a second hidden attacker before they could react [ch 026 / 026.07; ch 026 / memorable].
- Gyrgum cast his newly learned Spirit Guardians, creating a 15-foot emanation of spectral angelic guardians that dealt 17 and 15 radiant damage, killing two derro assassins [ch 026 / 026.07; ch 026 / spells].
- Thorin finished the last invisible attacker with his new two-attack sequence, his advantage cancelling the invisibility disadvantage, ending the skirmish with all five derro dead [ch 026 / 026.07].
- The party looted the derro, finding coins, a fuzzy lucky foot (claimed by Gyrgum), and a letter from the Council of Savants ordering the Gray Ghosts to "strike down the Duergar who brought in the assassins of the Ember Vanguard to kill us all" [ch 026 / 026.07].
- The DM noted "Your announcement of who you are had consequences," and the party concluded the Ember Vanguard was now "enemy number one" [ch 026 / 026.07; ch 026 / memorable].
- Whether to leave the bodies in the street, hide them, or stage them was left open for next session [ch 026 / 026.07].
- The party stood before the heavily barred gates of Gracklstugh near Laduguer's Furrow, debating how to bypass them to reach the Darklake; options discussed included bluffing, presenting themselves openly, or a "clever and insane" scheme, and Thorin suggested pole-vaulting the gates [ch 027 / 027.01].
- Gyrgum noted the difficulty of sneaking in a city of invisible people; the party decided to stand their ground when Themberchaud, the red dragon Wyrmsmith, dive-bombed toward them after spotting them during an earlier fight [ch 027 / 027.01; ch 027 / 027.02].
- Themberchaud landed with a graceless impact, mistook the party for his personal agents, complained about the Keepers of the Flame's inefficiency, and grandly offered his "express service," inviting the party to climb aboard [ch 027 / 027.02].
- Gyrgum agreed to the ride calling it "dangerous and stupid" but worth it for a dragon ride; the party climbed onto Themberchaud's back [ch 027 / 027.02; ch 027 / moments].
- In flight, the party persuaded Themberchaud to take them to the docks by arguing his agents should check uninvestigated areas; Gyrgum's Persuasion roll of 13 and a helped reroll both failed, but Themberchaud conceded, "Hmm, that's a good point" after admitting he had no idea where to look [ch 027 / 027.03].
- As Themberchaud banked inelegantly, several party members lost their grip and fell — Gyrgum (rolled 12), Thorin (natural 3 +4, plus a d10 boost, still failed), and Daz (rolled 8) — on DC 13 Athletics/Acrobatics checks [ch 027 / 027.03].
- Zalthir, newly level five, sprouted wings and caught the falling Thorin; Daz vanished in a silvery mist and teleported next to the flying Zalthir via Misty Step; Jimjar saved the falling Gyrgum by hurling a rope, which Gyrgum caught with a 19 on Acrobatics [ch 027 / 027.03].
- Themberchaud, oblivious to the chaos, showed off his realm with fly-bys, made a detour to the Forge of Thrazgad where he boasted and breathed fire to light it; the duergar below rolled their eyes at his ego [ch 027 / 027.03].
- The citizens of Gracklstugh saw the party on the dragon's back and, with Daz rolling a natural 20 on Insight, the party realized the crowd mistook them for the Ember Vanguard — legendary agents of the Drow Empire — throwing the city into panic; Daz is rumored to be an 18th-level mage who can cast Meteor Swarm [ch 027 / 027.03].
- Themberchaud landed heavily at the docks, roared with frightful presence to terrify the locals, and departed for his lair after Gyrgum promised to "search the docks far and wide" [ch 027 / 027.03; ch 027 / 027.04].
- A lone duergar guard stepped forward to halt the party in the name of the Deepking; Gyrgum's pass "in the name of Themberchaud" failed (roll 8, then worse), and the guard pulled out a wanted poster recognizing them as the Ember Vanguard [ch 027 / 027.04].
- The party leaned into the deception, claiming to be undercover agents impersonating the Ember Vanguard; Gyrgum "deputized" the guard with a caltrop as a secret symbol of their conspiracy and advised him to file down the sharp points; the relieved guard rushed off to inform Captain Errde [ch 027 / 027.04].
- With the guard gone, the party seized a boat and pushed off into the Darklake [ch 027 / 027.04].
- On the water, the party debated routes — Blingdenstone was the known exit, the Lost Tomb of Khaem lay four days by boat en route, and the Neverlight Grove was where Stool and Rumpadump longed to return; Zalthir proposed raiding the tomb first for a demon-slaying treasure [ch 027 / 027.05].
- Jimjar made a wager, betting an empty shell gem that the Neverlight Grove's "circle of joy" would reveal itself to be a "circle of unwilling vows" before the party leaves it; Gyrgum took the bet [ch 027 / 027.05].
- On the third day of the voyage, a swarm of stirges (AC 14, 2 HP, 1d4+3 piercing bite) descended from the cavern ceiling; Zalthir incinerated three with a 15-foot cone of fire and punched down another, Daz deflected one with a magical shield, and Thorin cut down two with his longsword [ch 027 / 027.05].
- Gyrgum was latched onto by a stirge that began draining his blood for 4 damage; he cast True Strike but missed his hand axe attack; Daz ended the encounter with unerring magical bolts that killed the remaining stirges [ch 027 / 027.05].
- The party arrived at a desolate, lonely beach that Sarith identified as the path toward the Lost Tomb of Khaem, and Sarith proposed sinking the boat so it could be recovered later with Mending [ch 027 / 027.06].
- The party scuttled their own boat, with Gyrgum punching a hole in it, planning to fix the hull with Mending when they returned; Sarith then led the group inland into the winding tunnels of the Underdark [ch 027 / 027.06].
- The party traveled deeper into the Underdark through a Faerzress-suffused region where teleportation is hampered; the GM recapped that the Faerzress "went blink and caused everybody to go mad" and the party's working hypothesis is the blink caused the demons to appear [ch 028 / 028.01].
- Sarith showed the party fist-sized quartz crystals that shed dim light in a 10-foot radius and, when hurled against a hard surface, burst into a blinding flash in a 10-foot radius; a DC 10 Constitution save or blindness for a minute repeats at the end of each turn, and he called them "excellent crystals... powerful crystals... very useful crystals" [ch 028 / 028.01].
- Gyrgum concluded "you should throw it at least 10 feet," and Sarith agreed [ch 028 / 028.01].
- Daz rolled a 19 on Arcana and recalled Zalthir's earlier explanation that magical darkness blocks all light, even magical light; the party harvested twelve of the glowing stones [ch 028 / 028.01].
- The party encountered two wretched former Drow slaves, Fuurm and Baedora; Daz rolled a 22 on Investigation and spotted their Drow prisoner markings [ch 028 / 028.01; ch 028 / 028.02].
- Fuurm raised a glowing crystal as a weapon upon seeing Daz, a drow, declaring "we could be of some help"; Daz's 24 on Insight read the pair as pure desperation, not agents [ch 028 / 028.01].
- Fuurm told the party he entered the Underdark to save his sister, stolen from the Overbright by the Drow, was captured almost immediately, and escaped during a demonic attack on his captors [ch 028 / 028.01; ch 028 / 028.03].
- The party invited Fuurm and Baedora to travel along, sharing food and water, with Zalthir urging everyone to keep an eye on them [ch 028 / 028.01].
- Eldeth warned that harvesting crystals would leave an obvious trail for Drow trackers, so Zalthir devised a plan to smash the remaining crystals under his shroud of magical darkness to make the area look like a battle site [ch 028 / 028.02].
- Gyrgum was eager to help smash the crystals ("they sound like a great prank") and pointed out smashing them would blind them, leading to the plan of doing it in Zalthir's darkness [ch 028 / 028.02].
- Eldeth used her survival skills (Survival +6) to mask the party's tracks, slowing the Drow pursuit [ch 028 / 028.02].
- The party entered a fungus cavern filled with shriekers that wailed; a crystal thrown at them from 10 feet away did nothing—"It's just shrieking"—but Eldeth made a DC 16 Survival check that covered their passage [ch 028 / 028.03].
- Around the sixth day, the party passed another deserted crystal cluster and Daz decided "Might as well search for a little bit more"; Eldeth again covered their trail [ch 028 / 028.03].
- By journey's end Eldeth was fairly confident she had almost shaken the Drow, and the party's pursuit level stood at one [ch 028 / 028.03].
- Sarith announced their arrival at the Lost Tomb of Khaem ("Aha, we have arrived") [ch 028 / 028.04].
- Just before the tomb doors, Jimjar demanded a bet; Gyrgum wagered the crystals would come in handy, a startled Jimjar accepted, then Jimjar's follow-up bet that no one would agree on whether the crystals were useful found no takers [ch 028 / 028.04].
- A soft, feminine telepathic voice reached the party near the tomb, pleading to be freed from the dark "for so long. So very long," and gave them a sense of the entrance's direction [ch 028 / 028.04].
- The party entered the tomb's double doors; a 24 on Arcana confirmed this was truly Brysis's Netherese tomb and that powerful magic lay within [ch 028 / 028.04].
- The party descended a long staircase over a hundred feet into the earth, passing a Netherese calendar stone ("just the calendar" per the GM), with Gyrgum in the lead [ch 028 / 028.04].
- At the bottom the party found a shrine with shredded tapestries, gouged friezes, and a cracked marble altar that was the target of recent violence, noted how intact everything was after thousands of years, and Gyrgum concluded the culprits were floating undead that leave no tracks in the dust [ch 028 / 028.04].
- Gyrgum recalled from his days with Bahamut that such undead don't rest but are driven to keep searching, and suggested they hate things they were associated with in life [ch 028 / 028.04].
- The party recalled Fargas Rumblefoot's tip that the tomb held a secret, false tomb, though he didn't know where it was [ch 028 / 028.04].
- The party pressed east through a side door chosen by die roll in Thorin's honour (Gyrgum rolled an 18) and entered a chamber of four stone sarcophagi [ch 028 / 028.05].
- The party noticed the northeast sarcophagus rested on hidden stone rollers; Zalthir drew his shroud of magical darkness across the room and Thorin rolled the sarcophagus aside while the hangers-on stayed outside eating [ch 028 / 028.05].
- Four specters tore free from the sarcophagi and attacked; the distant voice cried, "No, you must save me" [ch 028 / 028.05].
- Zalthir struck down one specter with a flurry of blows (fighting in his own darkness gave him advantage; he hit for 11 and 9, spent a focus point for extra attacks, then hit a second for 9) [ch 028 / 028.05].
- Daz launched magical missiles at the specters (two into one for 10 damage, one into another for 5) but was struck twice by life-draining touches that lowered his hit-point maximum by 2, then by 10 [ch 028 / 028.05].
- Gyrgum attempted Turn Undead (DC 15 Wisdom save), but every remaining specter resisted, prompting him to wonder if Bahamut was still displeased about the dragon egg [ch 028 / 028.05].
- Thorin cut down a specter with his enchanted longsword (hitting for 12, killing one, then 11 on another), and protected Daz, saying "Don't worry, Daz, I've got you covered" then "well, not that well covered. You may want to think about getting out of here" [ch 028 / 028.05].
- Zalthir finished the remaining specters, moving his darkness two squares and punching "the ghost," which indignantly corrected him—"Specter, I am a specter"—landing 13 damage to finish it [ch 028 / 028.05].
- The party looted the sarcophagi, finding gold bracelets (northeast sarcophagus), a fancy walking stick, and a gold censer with platinum filigree (southeast sarcophagus) [ch 028 / 028.05].
- Daz rolled a 27 on Arcana and determined the undead in this tomb likely reform every twenty-four hours, so the party could not safely take a long rest—"No, we can keep going" [ch 028 / 028.05].
- Beneath the rolled-aside sarcophagus the party found a four-foot square hole dropping down into darkness; Gyrgum confirmed he fits ("I'm an orc, not a giant") [ch 028 / 028.05].
- The party scrambled down into a lower chamber with richly pigmented walls and semi-precious stone inlays; a gilded sarcophagus rested atop a stone bier with an invisible stone chest at its foot [ch 028 / 028.06].
- A wraith materialized, cackling "Your deaths will free me from this prison, and how my victims will serve me even in death. Ha ha ha!" [ch 028 / 028.06].
- Daz considered casting Fireball but recalled (Arcana 17) that the wraith is resistant to fire and that "I have all fire stuff"; he briefly considered Maximilian's Earthen Grasp, then cast Magic Missile at third level—five darts for 20 damage—and the wraith reeled [ch 028 / 028.06].
- The wraith screamed about past defeats by drow and dwarves, lashed out at Thorin with a life-draining touch that missed entirely, then slipped into her gilded sarcophagus to hide, forcing the party to spend an action to open it [ch 028 / 028.06].
- Zalthir shifted his shroud of darkness into the lower chamber, knowing it would cost Daz his line of sight for Magic Missile ("Cognizant of that"), and spent his action to open the gilded sarcophagus [ch 028 / 028.06].
- As Zalthir opened the sarcophagus, the mysterious feminine voice spoke again from within: "in the sarcophagus, I can help you" [ch 028 / 028.06].
- Zalthir spotted a magical sword resting atop a withered, mummified corpse inside the gilded sarcophagus, surrounded by a whole bunch of treasure; the session ended here, mid-combat [ch 028 / 028.06].
- Zalthir pried open the stone sarcophagus of Brysis, revealing her mummified remains and a sentient sword resting atop the corpse that pleaded to be rescued: "You can't leave me here. Please." [ch 029 / 029.01]
- Gyrgum groaned at yet another talking magic sword, wishing she had been "a less squeaky talking mace"; Zalthir, a monk, said she could have been a glove; the sword was unimpressed, saying "wait, you're a monk. Get the fighter over here." [ch 029 / 029.01]
- Zalthir moved his orb of darkness over Thorin and spent focus points on a bonus-action flurry, striking twice with advantage from inside the darkness for roughly 20 magical damage. [ch 029 / 029.01]
- Gyrgum held back on turning the undead, saving his last use in case the party needed a quick getaway, and rejected Guiding Bolt as a blind ranged attack at disadvantage; instead he cast Tasha's Caustic Brew as a five-foot-wide stream past Zalthir, and Brysis failed the DC 15 Dexterity save. [ch 029 / 029.01]
- Daz cast Scorching Ray with his last 2nd-level slot, firing three rays at disadvantage against AC 13 into the darkness, landing 10, 10, and 3 for 23 damage, earning "the award for pissing off the demonic creature." [ch 029 / 029.01]
- Brysis raged that she would not be defeated after a millennium of imprisonment; Daz retreated up the tunnel to avoid being turned into a specter out of revenge. [ch 029 / 029.01]
- Thorin, rather than attacking, reached into the sarcophagus and pulled free the sentient sword; a blade of pure radiance blazed to life and she announced, "Dawnbringer here! Let me show you what I can do!" followed by "Finally, light!" [ch 029 / 029.01; ch 029 / npcs]
- Brysis, resistant to acid but down to three hit points, succumbed to the lingering 4d4 acid damage at the start of her turn, crying "Acid! I'm resisted to ah!" and ending the fight. [ch 029 / 029.01]
- The party pried loose a thin gold covering from the sarcophagus worth 250 gold pieces. [ch 029 / 029.02]
- Daz cast Faerie Fire to reveal a magically invisible chest near the sarcophagus; Zalthir volunteered ("I'll do it. I'll take one for the team.") and opened it, dumping its invisible contents onto the floor. [ch 029 / 029.02]
- The chest held 4,000 silver pieces, 1,200 gold pieces, eleven zircons worth 50 gold each, a necklace of fireballs, a philter of love, and a potion of greater healing; Gyrgum claimed the necklace and Daz moved the money into D&D Beyond's party inventory tab. [ch 029 / 029.02]
- Gyrgum, a self-described completionist, led the party into the unexplored southern chamber, with Daz hanging back ready to throw fire. [ch 029 / 029.03]
- The party found the southern chamber held only canopic jars of desiccated organs and a sarcophagus decorated with mosaics of great floating cities, with no danger because its only curse had already been destroyed along with the wraith. [ch 029 / 029.03]
- Thorin shoved the decoy sarcophagus lid off after Zalthir balked at the Strength check ("My punches are all driven by Dex."). [ch 029 / 029.03]
- The party took a long rest in the Underdark caverns; Gyrgum regained all his hit points, spells, and Channel Divinity uses. [ch 029 / 029.04]
- Over several days of travel, Eldeth obscured the party's trail with successful Wisdom checks, breaking the drow pursuit; the pursuit rating dropped to zero, though the GM warned "they'll eventually pick up your trail again." [ch 029 / 029.04]
- The party arrived at Neverlight Grove, a cavern of bioluminescent mushrooms covering every surface with a lake at its heart; Rumpadump cried "We're home! We're home! We're home!" and sprinted ahead with Stool. [ch 029 / 029.05]
- Zalthir wrapped himself in a shroud of darkness, saying "I don't trust any of these things"; Eldeth deemed Thorin "crazy" and stood beside Daz instead. [ch 029 / 029.05]
- Sovereigns Phylo and Basidia welcomed the party for returning the young myconids; Phylo spoke of a coming "day of joy" and praised the "Great Seeder" as "she" and "her," which Daz and Gyrgum found strange since myconids have no gender. [ch 029 / 029.05]
- An insight check of 18 by Gyrgum caught Basidia staring at Phylo in visible discomfort; she arranged for Phylo to stay behind and led the tour herself. [ch 029 / 029.05]
- Basidia revealed that a previous group of "softers" had accepted Phylo's hospitality, been taken to the Garden of Welcome, and never seen or mentioned again. [ch 029 / 029.06]
- Basidia noted Sarith was infected with spores she did not recognize, remarkable since myconid sovereigns can identify nearly every spore in the Underdark, and she feared Phylo "has contracted some sort of diseased spore." [ch 029 / 029.06]
- Basidia explained Phylo had restructured the grove's seven circles so members meld only within their own circle, created a new inner circle and a Circle of Masters, hoarded zurkh carcasses, and sent myconids outside the grove without telling anyone. [ch 029 / 029.06]
- Basidia gave the party a moldy scroll case holding a scroll of protection against fiends and offered potions plus treasure if they would investigate the Garden of Welcome; she could not go herself for fear of corruption. [ch 029 / 029.06]
- Gyrgum offered to cast lesser restoration on Phylo; Basidia answered, "If you could convince him, perhaps," and confirmed "Phylo is not well" when Zalthir asked if she accepts any outcome. [ch 029 / 029.06]
- Basidia promised to vouch for the party's authority everywhere in the grove except the Garden of Welcome, and began a tour of the circles. [ch 029 / 029.06]
- At the Circle of Hunters, Loobamub, a staunch Basidia ally, explained myconid hunters track already-dead creatures for reanimation and asked the party to kill a grick alpha lurking in the nearby fungal wilds and bring back its carcass intact. [ch 029 / 029.07]
- Zalthir voiced the idea that a reanimated grick could fight alongside them against the demonic force in the Garden of Welcome; Basidia called it "an excellent idea." [ch 029 / 029.07]
- The GM ruled that fire damage does not prevent reanimation, but slashing a creature to pieces or disintegrating it with acid does. [ch 029 / 029.07]
- Dawnbringer refused to be dimmed at night, keeping the party awake, saying "I don't trust the dark. I've been in the dark for a very long time." [ch 029 / 029.07]
- The session ended before the grick hunt began. [ch 029 / 029.07]
- The party ventured deeper into the vast caverns surrounding Neverlight Grove, navigating a massive mushroom-filled cavern far larger than their previous maps had implied, with a new, more detailed map introduced [ch 030 / 030.01].
- Loobamub warned the party to kill a Shambling Mound if they found it: "We don't reanimate shambling mounds," explaining that the Grick makes a fine reanimation target but the Shambling Mound is awkward [ch 030 / 030.01; ch 030 / npcs].
- Daz devised a strategy of freezing small sections of mossy ground in a checkerboard pattern with Shape Water, since the spell only freezes water with no creature in it, so any patch that failed to freeze would reveal the hidden Large creature [ch 030 / 030.01].
- The party advanced with Thorin in front and Dawnbringer glowing fiercely in the dark, since "the magical blade refuses to tolerate the absence of light" [ch 030 / 030.01; ch 030 / items].
- The Grick Alpha, disturbed by the party freezing its surroundings, woke and emerged to confront them, baring its teeth and claws [ch 030 / 030.01; ch 030 / 030.02].
- Zalthir shrouded the Grick Alpha in magical darkness and waded in, landing unarmed strikes for 29 damage, though the Grick shook off his Stunning Strike (DC 13 Constitution save succeeded with a 14) [ch 030 / 030.02].
- Daz, unable to see the creature through the darkness, angled a fireball to miss his allies; the Grick made its DC 15 Dexterity save and took only 13 damage [ch 030 / 030.02].
- Thorin charged in wielding Dawnbringer, hitting for 9 radiant damage and then 6, leaving the creature with disadvantage on its first attack [ch 030 / 030.02].
- Gyrgum, unable to use Guiding Bolt without line of sight, tried Tasha's Caustic Brew; the Grick made its DC 15 Dexterity save and took no damage [ch 030 / 030.02].
- The Grick Alpha missed all its attacks against Thorin and Zalthir due to the magical darkness, then "ponders its life for a moment" and attempted to flee [ch 030 / 030.02; ch 030 / 030.03].
- Zalthir's attack of opportunity hit for 13, and Thorin's blow killed the fleeing Grick Alpha [ch 030 / 030.03].
- The party harvested the Grick Alpha's hide, which could be fashioned in six days into a cloak granting advantage when hiding in rocky terrain [ch 030 / 030.02; ch 030 / 030.03; ch 030 / items].
- The party learned Shambling Mounds are "notorious for not having any treasure," but Daz's Arcana check revealed their vines are valued for crafting enchanted ropes [ch 030 / 030.03].
- The GM suggested carrying the vines to Blingdenstone, where Jimjar's crew was headed, motivating the party to hunt the Shambling Mound [ch 030 / 030.03].
- Daz resumed freezing the swamp in a checkerboard pattern and located the Shambling Mound near a pool of water, rolling initiative for a new engagement [ch 030 / 030.03].
- Zalthir closed in along the shore (avoiding the 10-foot-deep water that costs half speed to cross), landed a critical hit for 18, and his Stunning Strike (DC 13 Constitution) succeeded, stunning the mound [ch 030 / 030.04].
- Daz unleashed three scorching rays at the stunned creature with advantage, all hitting for 6, 10, and 7, but resistance halved the 23 damage to 11 [ch 030 / 030.04].
- Gyrgum summoned Spirit Guardians — spectral spirits resembling platinum dragons swirling in a 15-foot radius — exempting Thorin and Zalthir; the mound failed its DC 15 Wisdom save, took 13 damage, and had its speed halved [ch 030 / 030.04].
- Zalthir grappled the stunned creature ("Suddenly I have a reason to grapple for once") to keep it trapped within Gyrgum's aura, then hit for 11, and spent a focus point for two more hits of 14 and 10 [ch 030 / 030.04].
- Daz detonated a fireball, first checking the 20-foot radius and whether anyone would take steam damage from "melting the lake"; the mound failed its save and took 12 after resistance, dropping to 9 hit points [ch 030 / 030.04].
- The Shambling Mound retaliated with electrified tendrils; one missed, one struck Zalthir for 11 lightning and bludgeoning, which he deflected to take only 2 lightning damage [ch 030 / 030.04].
- The mound engulfed Thorin, who failed the DC 15 Strength save, was blinded, took 10 damage, muttering he had "smelt better cologne," then struggled free [ch 030 / 030.04].
- The Spirit Guardians' radiant energy finished the Shambling Mound, which failed its Wisdom save at the end of its turn, took 15 damage, and collapsed into a heap of lifeless vines [ch 030 / 030.04].
- The table discussed Spirit Guardians rules; the GM ruled a creature takes damage when moved into the emanation and again if it stays there on its turn, saving only once per turn, and that it is not an infinite-damage hack [ch 030 / 030.04].
- The party recovered enough Shambling Mound vine material for six ropes' worth, valued at 300 gold pieces [ch 030 / 030.04; ch 030 / 030.05; ch 030 / items].
- The party returned to Neverlight Grove and presented the Grick Alpha's remains to Basidia, who was mildly disappointed at the state of the hide but mused, "hmm, this will be an interesting transformation. I can't wait" [ch 030 / 030.05; ch 030 / npcs].
- The party checked on Stool and Rumpadump, who were happy and cheering; Gyrgum told them they were welcome to adventure, but they preferred to stay home ("That's very sweet of you") [ch 030 / 030.05].
- Daz wondered whether to head on to Blingdenstone, but Basidia reminded them she had promised magic and treasure if they explored the Circle of Masters [ch 030 / 030.05].
- During a short rest, Basidia lectured on the circles, then led the party to the Circle of Builders, a vast cavern of terraced irrigation systems and thousands of glowing fungi with a thick, sweet smell [ch 030 / 030.05; ch 030 / locations].
- There they met Gasbide, leader of the Circle of Builders and a supporter of Sovereign Phylo, who immediately interrogated the party about surface-world architecture, demanding shapes, materials, and counts of windows and doors, and was captivated by the word "window" [ch 030 / 030.05; ch 030 / npcs].
- Zalthir conjured a minor illusion of a small surface building; Gasbide was transfixed, asked whether it was for small people, and decided its creator must be a member of a surface "circle of builders" [ch 030 / 030.05].
- Gasbide revealed his dream of building a fungal tower greater than Yggmorgus to break through the Underdark to the surface, "possibly with the aid of Araumycos" [ch 030 / 030.05].
- Gasbide asked for a construction that could shield him from the sun; Gyrgum suggested "shade," and they discussed sizes and materials, with Gyrgum offering a rule of thumb of "about three zurkhwood mushrooms to the square foot" [ch 030 / 030.05].
- Gasbide mentioned a coming "Great Awakening" and a celebration involving Araumycos, saying "Phylo said that there would be a great awakening, that everything will be better after the great celebration," though he had no real understanding of what Araumycos was [ch 030 / 030.05; ch 030 / npcs; ch 030 / locations].
- Thorin's player returned and Thorin confirmed he was still alive, though the party admitted Basidia had occasionally determined his actions at random [ch 031 / 031.01].
- Thorin examined Dawnbringer, a sentient legendary sunblade with a gilded hilt that manifests a blade of pure radiance as a bonus action, and attuned to it [ch 031 / 031.01].
- The party learned Dawnbringer is a plus-two weapon dealing radiant rather than slashing damage, deals additional damage to undead, and can cast lesser restoration once per day [ch 031 / 031.01].
- The party discussed Dawnbringer's anxiety about darkness and abandonment after being trapped in the dark for millennia, and its possible reaction if Thorin favored another weapon [ch 031 / 031.01].
- The DM explained the sword is sentient with a kind feminine voice and may attempt to charm Thorin if he rushed into magical darkness, a risk given Thorin's negative charisma modifier [ch 031 / 031.01].
- Thorin accepted the arrangement, saying "I mean, no, we're bonded at this point" [ch 031 / 031.01].
- Basidia introduced the party to Hebopbe, leader of the Circle of Growers, who explained myconids are sentient herbivores cultivating specific fungi for consumption [ch 031 / 031.02].
- Thorin compared the arrangement with the analogy "You're a fungus and you eat fungus. You eat fungi. You are a — just like I'm a mammal and I eat mammals." [ch 031 / 031.02].
- Hebopbe showed off his prized zurkhwood and favored cultivated fungi, and expressed indifference to Phylo's organizational changes, viewing specialization as an advantage [ch 031 / 031.02].
- Basidia revealed Hebopbe was infected by strange evil spores; the party confirmed "It's the farmer, not the food" [ch 031 / 031.02].
- The party traveled to the central basin, passing awakened sentient zurkhwood trees, and Basidia explained most zurkhwoods are not ordinarily sentient [ch 031 / 031.02].
- At the circle mound, Basidia showed newly spored myconids only inches tall, and the party met Yrberop, its infected leader swaying to a discordant internal cacophony heard faintly through Basidia's rapport spores [ch 031 / 031.02].
- Yrberop spoke cryptically of a "great plan" by a "great cedar," praised the "great Zuggtmoy," and mentioned plans to send myconids into the Overbright [ch 031 / 031.02].
- Thorin connected the chaotic evil quality of Yrberop's internal noise to his earlier encounter with the demonic presence [ch 031 / 031.02].
- Yrberop crooned a lullaby to the newly spored myconids praising Zuggtmoy's coming wedding to the "great body" and the joy to spread throughout the world above and below [ch 031 / 031.02].
- The party recalled Phylo speaks of Zuggtmoy in specifically feminine terms and refuses to explain what she is, saying "One cannot be told what the great Zuggtmoy is. One must experience it firsthand," and suspected the Garden of Welcome visit was intended to make them sacrifices to a demon [ch 031 / 031.02].
