- Jimjar behaved strangely, hinting at a coming wedding and placing no bets; Basidia confirmed he was not infected, and Jimjar offered to bet that he had never been infected by spores [ch 031 / 031.02].
- The party met Rumpadump, Circle of Explorers leader, who distrusts Phylo, advocates nomadism, and has prepared escape routes and hidden nutrient caches in the exit tunnels [ch 031 / 031.02].
- Rumpadump dismissed moving into the Overbright as "completely insane and perverse" and offered to lead the party into the Garden of Welcome through a secret passageway, which they accepted immediately [ch 031 / 031.02].
- The party entered the Garden of Welcome via Rumpadump's secret route before Phylo's planned tour the following morning [ch 031 / 031.03].
- The party found the heads of creatures from a dozen Underdark races peeking from the soil, consumed by mold, with only a few glowing mushrooms providing light [ch 031 / 031.03].
- The party encountered Xinaya, a dying drow with half her face rotted and sprouting mushrooms, who raised a spider medallion from her bloated fungal hand and begged for death [ch 031 / 031.03].
- Xinaya revealed she was part of a patrol sent by Ilvara to search for and capture the party near the grove, having made the mistake of accepting Phylo's invitation to the garden, and laughed at begging her would-be captives for salvation [ch 031 / 031.03].
- Xinaya urged Daz, recognized as being from Menzoberranzan, to warn the other Drow that the Demon Queen of Fungi had entered the Underdark [ch 031 / 031.03].
- Yestabrod, a larval creature possibly once a myconid, rose from the ground, welcoming the party to a wedding rehearsal and promising the "great cedar" would make them one with her chosen "great body" [ch 031 / 031.03].
- Yestabrod rejected the party's audit ploy — "There will be no audit" — and summoned drow spore servants from Xinaya's former patrol and myconid adults to attack [ch 031 / 031.03].
- Thorin activated Dawnbringer, emitting genuine sunlight in a fifteen-foot radius that hampered light-sensitive myconids, though Yestabrod was immune to this weakness [ch 031 / 031.04].
- Gyrgum channeled necrotic energy into Yestabrod, severely wounding it, but Yestabrod exploded a nearby corpse and absorbed the remains to heal itself [ch 031 / 031.04].
- Daz unleashed a fireball that badly burned three clustered creatures; a player joked the blast made the spores crispy, but the GM clarified the spores had already been emitted [ch 031 / 031.04].
- Yestabrod exploded another corpse in a shower of gore, offal, and acid, and released a thick cloud of spores that afflicted Daz and Zalthir with secret madnesses communicated privately to their players [ch 031 / 031.04].
- Sarith's head exploded during the battle and he rose as a mindless drow spore servant; Eldeth and Jimjar were forced to cut him down almost immediately [ch 031 / 031.04; ch 031 / moment].
- Thorin was incapacitated by stunning spores from a myconid adult and remained so until the battle was nearly over [ch 031 / 031.04].
- Zalthir ended Yestabrod with a relentless flurry of strikes, preventing further corpse detonations [ch 031 / 031.04].
- Gyrgum withered another myconid with a second necrotic touch, Daz finished two weakened enemies with precisely divided magic missiles, and Zalthir destroyed the final myconid while using Dawnbringer's sunlight [ch 031 / 031.04].
- In the aftermath, Zalthir retrieved the spider medallion from Xinaya's fungal hand while she was still alive [ch 031 / 031.05].
- Xinaya warned the party that those infected by the spores had only days to find a cure, saying "It is too late for me" when asked if she could be cured [ch 031 / 031.05].
- Gyrgum considered lesser restoration and revivify but concluded only magic on the scale of a wish might have restored her [ch 031 / 031.05].
- Thorin consulted Dawnbringer and together they granted Xinaya the merciful death she had begged for [ch 031 / 031.05].
- Beyond the garden, the party heard a discordant, wheezing melody, and Thorin recognized the same musical quality he had sensed in Yrberop's mind [ch 031 / 031.06].
- A parade of humanoid creatures covered in luminescent lichen and tumescent growths emerged from the mist, chanting a wedding hymn celebrating the union of Zuggtmoy and Araumycos in the party's minds [ch 031 / 031.06].
- Zuggtmoy herself appeared at the head of the procession with her bridesmaids, and the party confirmed she was the same demon lord Thorin had encountered before, ending the session as she walked toward them [ch 031 / 031.06].
- The party observed a bridal procession led by the Demon Queen of Fungi, Zuggtmoy, marching through a grove near the "Majestic Mushroom," a colossal hollow fungal structure filling the cavern from floor to ceiling [ch 032 / 032.01].
- The party pieced together that Zuggtmoy intended to ritually mate with — and potentially possess — Araumycos, an immense sentient fungal growth in the Underdark, and that the intended groom was not yet present, so the wedding could not begin [ch 032 / 032.01].
- The party recognized the wider danger: Demogorgon had already appeared in the Underdark, and now a second demon lord was pursuing a powerful native entity [ch 032 / 032.01].
- The party witnessed a mock wedding ceremony in which a chamberlain and bridesmaid stood in for Araumycos and Zuggtmoy, and three spore servants carried the recently slain Yestabrod's corpse like a puppet to play the priest [ch 032 / 032.02].
- A shared vision overcame the party, showing the interior of a great fungal tower where a woman-like figure made entirely of fungi and mold floated at its center, three times the height of the fungal bridesmaids tending her, who wove delicate lichen veils and a long mycelium bridal train [ch 032 / 032.02].
- The party approached the Majestic Mushroom, where a stench of rotten decay threatened flesh and soul, and a cacophony of atonal music echoed from within; they wetted cloth strips to cover their mouths and noses against drifting spores [ch 032 / 032.02].
- Daz was overcome by short-term madness from the spores, experiencing vivid hallucinations including happy elf families, dwarves and elves living in peace, countless pictures of dogs, and a drow city without spiders; he screamed, hid, and struggled to distinguish hallucinations from reality [ch 032 / 032.02].
- Zalthir, also spore-affected, was seized by maddening greed when the party collected equipment near Xinaya's burial place — including finely crafted studded leather armor and magical scrolls — hoarding the scrolls and refusing to share them [ch 032 / 032.02].
- Thorin used Dawnbringer's Lesser Restoration on Zalthir, breaking his madness; Zalthir apologized and said he did not know what had come over him [ch 032 / 032.02].
- The party refused to venture through a passage deeper into Zuggtmoy's realm, with Thorin deciding, "Yeah, no, let's not do that" [ch 032 / 032.02].
- The party returned to Basidia and Rasharoo, reporting the full account of the wedding rehearsal and Zuggtmoy's plans; Basidia insisted Phylo had to be told while Rasharoo argued they should run [ch 032 / 032.03].
- The party returned to Phylo, hiding in plain sight and preserving the fiction they would attend the next day's celebration; Gyrgum signaled danger and Dawnbringer's light made the surrounding myconids visibly uncomfortable [ch 032 / 032.03].
- Basidia confronted Phylo, accusing him of betraying the circle and allying himself with corruption, and the confrontation escalated toward battle as the grove's trees moved to attack her [ch 032 / 032.03].
- Gyrgum persuaded Stool and Rumpadump to leave immediately, and Rasharoo shouted "If you want to live, come with me," leading survivors including Loobamub and his circle toward the escape route [ch 032 / 032.03].
- Zalthir seized Basidia and dragged her away from a suicidal charge at Phylo; seeing everyone she cared about fleeing convinced her to leave [ch 032 / 032.03].
- The party fled Neverlight Grove as Phylo cheerfully called after them, promising the Great Cedar would make them all happy and lamenting they refused the Circle of Welcome, which would have transformed them into heads rooted in the ground [ch 032 / 032.03].
- Basidia gave the party potions of greater healing and a box of treasure totaling 975 gold pieces as a reward for saving members of her circle [ch 032 / 032.03].
- Stool and Rumpadump departed with Basidia and the surviving myconids to be with their own kind; Rumpadump delivered Zalthir an exhaustive, detailed list of feedback via rapport spores that remained in his head for roughly fifteen minutes [ch 032 / 032.04].
- Jimjar settled a previous bet, paying Gyrgum five gold pieces after first claiming he lacked ready cash [ch 032 / 032.04].
- With Sarith dead, the party turned toward Blingdenstone as their best hope of reaching the Overbright; Rasharoo supplied myconid scouts to guide them through the first third of the journey and warned that Ilvara and her drow hunters remained at large [ch 032 / 032.04].
- The party speculated about misleading the drow into believing they became victims of the Circle of Welcome, with Zalthir noting Xinaya's presence could lend credibility, and Thorin suggesting Ilvara attend the wedding [ch 032 / 032.04].
- After a long rest, seven days of largely uneventful travel passed before Rasharoo's scouts bid them farewell; Jimjar again asserted he knew exactly where they were going and offered to bet Thorin would praise him on arrival [ch 032 / 032.04].
- The party entered a cavern with heavy, toxic, combustible gas collected in a depression, and edged along five-foot ledges where a giant rocktopus awaited between them and the exit [ch 032 / 032.05].
- Thorin struck the rocktopus twice with Dawnbringer for nineteen points of damage; the creature struck him for ten points and tried to grapple him, but he shook off its grip [ch 032 / 032.05].
- Zalthir restrained the rocktopus with a flurry of punches, Daz cast Magic Missile for nine points of force damage, and Gyrgum chanted a True Strike invocation and brought his mace down for seven points, killing it; Gyrgum exulted as "the octopus slayer" [ch 032 / 032.05].
- A sudden tremor caused a cave-in that blocked the party's original route, trapping them in an unfamiliar maze of passages with a passage still open to the north [ch 032 / 032.06].
- Thorin mapped their surroundings with cartographer's tools while Gyrgum used calligrapher's supplies to ensure the chart was well labeled in a tasteful font [ch 032 / 032.06].
- Jimjar argued the cave-in did not count as his failure, then attempted an increasingly cryptic bet about Zalthir's next emotion being "black and dark and sharp"; Spiderbait dismissed it as weak, and Jimjar admitted "It's not my best work" and confessed "I was just trying to cover for the fact that I got us lost here" [ch 032 / 032.06].
- Jimjar directed attention to the unexplored cavern to the north, betting the party would find something special; the party agreed to investigate, and the session ended before they entered [ch 032 / 032.06].
- Jimjar, the deep gnome guide, defended his navigational error through the Underdark as an expected consequence of shifting passages and claimed their current location was a convenient shortcut to Blingdenstone; Thorin predicted, “Yeah, this will end in tears,” and after clarifying the wager's terms (“there will be some unfortunate event before we get back on our way”), the bet was accepted [ch 034 / 034.01].
- The apparent route forward was the only open one, the alternate passage blocked by extensive rubble; the group briefly struggled to remember Blingdenstone as their destination, and Thorin chose the left branch at the next intersection by a die roll [ch 034 / 034.01].
- In a 10-foot-high glistening passage, Thorin refused to step into the pool fearing “creepy crawlies,” then discovered the water was rising; Daz determined the entire complex was leaking and would fill completely, due to a tremor cracking an underground reservoir or river into the temple [ch 034 / 034.02].
- The party rushed into the flooded square constructed chamber; a gray ooze emerged and Zalthir stepped forward and destroyed it with two powerful unarmed blows (10 then 12 damage) before anyone else could act [ch 034 / 034.02].
- Beyond the chamber, the party discovered the remains of a drow, a dark metal mace, and scattered coins held suspended inside a gelatinous cube with two visible eyes, which greeted them (“Well, hello, hello, hello.”) and introduced himself as Glabbagool, a recently sentient former ordinary cube [ch 034 / 034.03].
- Thorin invited Glabbagool to join the expedition, promising companionship, adventure, and food; Glabbagool accepted after asking, “You won’t be upset if you all die and I eat you, right?”, with the condition that he allow natural causes to do the killing [ch 034 / 034.03].
- Gyrgum tossed Glabbagool a lucky foot from his backpack as a welcoming gift, which he absorbed and declared tasty; the party declined to feed him ghost bones because the associated ghost reacts angrily [ch 034 / 034.03].
- In a narrow corridor, Thorin hurled darts at a gray ooze (two hits for 8 each), while Glabbagool's ten-foot body completely blocked the passage, separating Thorin from the party; Daz then cast Toll the Dead for 18 necrotic damage, destroying it (Thorin withdrew first, keeping armor out of reach) [ch 034 / 034.04].
- The party continued with the water rising “sort of like a bathtub that doesn’t drain well,” Jimjar asking whether the “tears” wager could be declared a mulligan on account of Glabbagool; Thorin was willing to call the bet a draw [ch 034 / 034.04].
- At a junction with four western passages and one eastern route, Thorin used a d5 die to pick a western passage, fell into a concealed pit but caught the edge and hauled himself out (“aided substantially by his strength”) [ch 034 / 034.05].
- A black pudding climbed from the pit; an Arcana check associated the ooze concentration with the demon lord Juiblex, and the party remembered black puddings can split when struck with certain weapons, so they retreated to a larger chamber and destroyed it with ranged attacks; Glabbagool eagerly consumed the remains [ch 034 / 034.05].
- Reaching a temple chamber with a stone fountain, broken statue (only clawed stone feet recognizable), seven niches (one admitting water through a crack, two empty, four with formless sculptures), and skeletons in the chamber, the party accepted Glabbagool's offer to scout (“Well, you know, the oozes can’t go through me.”) [ch 034 / 034.06].
- When Glabbagool touched a formless sculpture, it materialized into a gray ooze, prompting his complaint, “Well, that’s rude,” and Zalthir rushed in and punched it apart; the party then activated the remaining sculptures one at a time, Zalthir destroying each ooze, Glabbagool absorbing the debris until the floor was clean [ch 034 / 034.06].
- Glabbagool searched the fountain and discovered 112 silver pieces, three gold pieces, a drow dagger, and a vial of oil of slipperiness — then absorbed all of it, which the party joked made him “a cube of holding” whose contents they would have to wait days to retrieve [ch 034 / 034.06].
- The fountain room did not provide the exit, so the party retraced its path and took the eastern route; near a rough-hewn wall they heard pouring water, and streams entered through cracks in the 10-foot-high ceiling, with water rising roughly one foot per hour [ch 034 / 034.07].
- Jimjar bet that a massive quantity of water waited behind the stone; Thorin pointed out nobody had accepted that wager; Eldeth examined the site, recognized the signs of an underground river breaking through, and recalled stories of escape through such breaches, adding grimly, “I didn’t hear about all the people who did that and drowned anymore.” [ch 034 / 034.07].
- Daz determined Shape Water could affect a five-foot cube, last an hour when non-instantaneous, and support two maintained effects at once; with the only alternative escape hatch suspected to be just more oozes and a dead end, the party planned a moving, ten-foot-long air-pocket refuge [ch 034 / 034.07].
- Daz created successive water-free pockets by redirecting water to the cube's edges while the party beat the rising water; Glabbagool could not fit but said, “Oh, that’s totally fine. I don’t breathe,” also promising, “If any of you were to perish, I will absolutely grab you and digest you.” [ch 034 / 034.08].
- Glabbagool caused more of the ceiling to collapse, doubling the water inflow and revealing the diverted underground river; the party, protected by Daz's air pockets, endured the inundation and swam upward approximately 30 feet to surface in a large cavern [ch 034 / 034.08].
- Jimjar declared, “See, it did not end in tears,” while Thorin acknowledged shenanigans and promised Jimjar ten silver pieces once they emerged from Glabbagool (possibly “a little slimy”); Jimjar insisted, “I found us a shortcut, right?”, and the party conceded the shortcut either found them or the reverse, and that they had gained a friend [ch 034 / 034.08].
- The party arrived on the shores of the Darklake wet, cold, and miserable after leaving their boat; Jimjar announced "We are but a day's journey from the outskirts of Blingdenstone," and the party took a long rest, during which Gyrgum puzzled over the concept of a "long rest" [ch 035 / 035.01].
- After an undisturbed rest, the party began the day-long march toward Blingdenstone and reached a long staircase built for three-foot-tall gnomes, which was difficult terrain for larger members [ch 035 / 035.02].
- Jimjar declared "We've arrived at Blingdenstone!" and explained the city had belonged to the gnomes, then the drow, then the gnomes again, and described it as "a little bit of a mess" whose people knew routes out of the Underdark [ch 035 / 035.02].
- The party reached the main gate, built from steel and mithral, reinforced with magical and magic-resistant alloys, and plated with adamantine, and saw eight gnome guards beyond it; the head guard demanded "Who are you and what is your purpose?" [ch 035 / 035.02; ch 035 / 035.03].
- A guard immediately distrusted Daz, declaring "You are a drow, and we know that drow lie," to which Gyrgum countered "But I am an orc, and everyone knows that orcs only tell the truth," bewildering the guards when they realized he was an orc cleric of Bahamut [ch 035 / 035.03].
- Jimjar told the guards Daz was "all about exploring and growing, not about domination and world conquest," claimed Daz was reading a book called *The Up in the Overbright*, and Daz explained the party had been detained and exploited against their will, escaped, and sought good companions and a safer destination [ch 035 / 035.03].
- Glabbagool appeared at the gate, panicking the guards into preparing to attack until the party intervened, explaining he had saved their lives, fought other slimy creatures, and eaten enemy remains; the guards were astonished a gelatinous cube could speak [ch 035 / 035.04].
- Glabbagool explained he had been unintelligent until "something happened" and he began to think and talk; he reported far more oozes than usual in the region, all agitated and eager to expand, dubbing the phenomenon an "oozapalooza" [ch 035 / 035.04].
- The guards corrected themselves from a "fungi problem" to an "ooze problem," and the party recalled Daz's earlier information about Juiblex; at the demon lord's name, Jimjar's expression shifted as though remembering something painful, and he described Juiblex as "the demon lord of oozes and shapeless things" [ch 035 / 035.04].
- Jimjar distinguished the demon lords by their corruption styles—Zuggtmoy's decay, Demogorgon's carnage, Orcus's endless death, Juiblex's shapeless mess—and claimed, "I just happen to be someone who collects information and makes bets" [ch 035 / 035.04].
- Jimjar made a fifty-gold-piece bet that, before the end of the month, the party would need to equip an army with acid-resistant gear, and added a side bet that the ingredients for those weapons would have to be recovered from a fungal wedding celebration [ch 035 / 035.04].
- The party attempted to read Jimjar, found he knew far too many precise details, and discovered the surrounding gnomes treated him with peculiar deference; one guard admitted Jimjar looked very familiar but could not place him, reinforcing the sense that Jimjar was "some sort of VIP something" [ch 035 / 035.04].
- Glabbagool explained gelatinous cubes reproduce by leaving a smaller cube behind, cannot do so rapidly, and take a long time to grow; he had once left a smaller cube behind out of loneliness, but it could not speak, and he sadly concluded "I think I'm the only one in the entire universe that can speak" [ch 035 / 035.04].
- The guards admitted the entire company into Blingdenstone after Jimjar vouched for them and urged the party to consult Chief Dorbo Diggermattock and Quartermaster Senni for full permission to keep Glabbagool [ch 035 / 035.05].
- In the Maze, a defensive network of twisted, trapped tunnels, a gnome warned against "funny business" while pointing at Daz, who responded tactfully, "I mean, actually, I feel safer hanging out with you guys because of your powerful defenses. Thank you for hosting us," delighting the gnomes [ch 035 / 035.05].
- Eldeth admitted her inherited prejudice toward drow, telling Daz she had been raised to believe "The only good drow is a dead drow," but traveling with him had forced her to reconsider; she now believed the true enemy was "their crazed religious, uh, uh, religious cult of Lolth" rather than every individual drow [ch 035 / 035.05].
- Daz cautioned that even those raised knowing only Lolth's cult might be redeemable people shaped by a terrible environment, and Eldeth acknowledged his example had convinced her to rethink her culture's reflexive treatment of entire peoples as inherently evil [ch 035 / 035.05].
- Dawnbringer's bearer remarked during the poorly illuminated Maze entrance, "In darkness, my sword's getting very upset," reinforcing the sword's dislike of prolonged darkness [ch 035 / items].
- The party arrived at a gate within Blingdenstone's caverns, where the deep gnome guards who had previously admitted them caught up, huffing and puffing; a squeaky-voiced officer confirmed "So you escaped from Velkynvelve from the Drow. Is this correct?" [ch 036 / 036.01].
- The officer explained a "small semantical problem": the embarrassed sergeant had not read the latest report fully, and standing orders required all escapees from the Drow to be escorted immediately to Chief Dorbo of Clan Diggermattock [ch 036 / 036.01].
- The guards explained Clan Diggermattock had taken the territory from the Drow and considered any intelligence about their long-standing enemies critically important, and that Dorbo wanted to question the party to better prepare the deep gnomes' defenses [ch 036 / 036.01].
- An insight check established the request was sincere and contained no malevolence, and the party agreed to accompany the guards, with one member joking this was preferable to "trying to kill all the guards, which was the other thing I was considering" [ch 036 / 036.01].
- The party consulted Jimjar, who bet "five empty spell gems that meeting with them is the right thing to do" and added "But you're going to have to figure out how to get rid of the oozes"; Glabbagool corrected the terminology, "It's not an ooze, it's a cube" [ch 036 / 036.02].
- After carefully clarifying the wager's terms, the party accepted Jimjar's bet, recognizing he was predicting the clan would send them on a quest about the ooze infestation [ch 036 / 036.02].
- The guards escorted the party through a reception hall containing a cart jokingly described as a "medieval pickup truck," then through the Traders' Grotto, where less adventurous deep gnomes reacted nervously to Glabbagool's presence [ch 036 / 036.03].
- The party reached Diggermattock Hall, a well-lit cavern with roughly two dozen Svirfneblin, some hurrying with messages and others gathered around stone tables of maps, and atop a rock-carved dais sat a male and female Svirfneblin who introduced themselves as "Dorbo" and "Senni" [ch 036 / 036.04].
- A party member initially answered "We're the Emerald Vanguard," but the group became tangled among "Emerald Vanguard," "Ember Vanguard," and "Emerald Enclave," realized one of those names belonged to a group blamed for destroying a capital city, and instead introduced themselves as "a group of adventurers trying to find our way back home" seeking the Overbright [ch 036 / 036.04].
- Anticipating Jimjar's prediction, the party volunteered for an ooze quest, prompting Senni to reply, "We'd normally be delighted to help you go to the Overbright, but unfortunately, our community is suffering from an ooze problem. And since you so graciously volunteered to go look at it, we won't feel at all encumbered or guilty about asking you to do that before we help you" [ch 036 / 036.04].
- Glabbagool defended himself, insisting "Well, I'm not an ooze. I'm a gelatinous cube. A totally different species," and the party supported him by arguing "Have you ever seen a square ooze? I don't think so"; Glabbagool declared "I knew there was a reason I was just better than them" [ch 036 / 036.04].
- A party member described a shambling mound from both book learning and the group's previous encounter, which the party had fought using fire; Glabbagool asked whether it had "a lot of stuff inside of it when it blew up," and the party explained cubes retain objects but oozes do not [ch 036 / 036.04].
- Glabbagool revealed prior experience with oozes, describing them as "very not talkative," and said "No, no, I don't eat oozes. I mean, I do punch them sometimes, but no, I don't eat oozes," extending a pseudopod to demonstrate he could both strike and absorb things [ch 036 / 036.04].
- The session ended with the party in Diggermattock Hall, having been asked to address Blingdenstone's ooze infestation before the clan would help them reach the Overbright [ch 036 / 036.04; ch 036 / npcs].
- The party visited Diggermattock Hall, where Chief Dorbo and Senni argued over the ooze infestation hindering mining and housing construction, with Dorbo volunteering the party to solve it [ch 037 / 037.01].
- The party reaffirmed itself as the Ember Vanguard, then settled temporarily on "The Ash Enclave" with possible phoenix imagery [ch 037 / 037.01].
- A guard directed the party through the Traders' Grotto and beyond a stone wall where the oozes seemed to originate, initially pointing at the wrong spot on a map before correcting himself [ch 037 / 037.01].
- The guard shared the rumor that the Goldwhisker Clan were wererats, prompting discussion of colonization and a dry joke about inefficient use of prime real estate [ch 037 / 037.01].
- The party traveled to the cemetery beside the Temple of the Ruby in the Rough to honor a promise to Pelek, the ghost traveling with them [ch 037 / 037.02].
- At the temple, Gyrgum jingled his bag of Pelek's "Catholic relic-sized" bones, and priest Glyphic Shroomlight initially hesitated but relented after Pelek's ghost materialized [ch 037 / 037.03].
- Glyphic directed the party to the catacombs below, asking that Glabbagool remain upstairs; Glabbagool agreed, offering his cleaning services [ch 037 / 037.03].
- In the catacombs, Gyrgum buried Pelek's bones in a quiet corner with a dragon-themed ritual, planting a gold coin "to serve as the foundation for his word" [ch 037 / 037.03].
- Pelek's spirit vanished after the party said farewell, leaving a warm, comforting feeling—then another ghost immediately materialized [ch 037 / 037.03].
- Burrow Warden Jadger, a three-foot ghost, appeared and recognized Zalthir as "an excellent asset" because the monk carried no weapons [ch 037 / 037.04].
- Jadger offered one truthful Underdark answer per ghost laid to rest, counting Pelek as the first, and noted two more ghosts he wanted the party to deal with [ch 037 / 037.04].
- The party grew anxious about wasting its question, and Jadger indignantly declared, "I'm not some trivial genie. I am Burrow Warden Jadger" [ch 037 / 037.04].
- Jadger revealed that Chief Dorbo and Senni know how to leave the Underdark and that the route is well known [ch 037 / 037.04].
- The party asked how best to deal with the oozes; Jadger explained that massed oozes are unnatural and advised investigating what was directing them [ch 037 / 037.04].
- Zalthir performed a monk technique for Jadger, who imitated it incorrectly with his fists [ch 037 / 037.04].
- Jadger distinguished "Devils make deals" from demons who imbue agents with power to cause carnage, and theorized Juiblex had empowered a proxy rather than directly controlling the oozes [ch 037 / 037.04].
- Jadger advised the party to consult a young gnome he was mentoring who would soon lead the new Burrow Wardens [ch 037 / 037.04].
- The party traveled to the Traders' Grotto, Blingdenstone's central market, where a sergeant gave a tour of petrified fungi, stalls, and warehouses [ch 037 / 037.05].
- The party continued workshopping its name, settling on "Ash Vanguard" and rejecting "Ash Rearguard" [ch 037 / 037.05].
- Several non-sentient gelatinous cubes emerged from the rocks, causing panic, and Glabbagool dismissed them as "mindless, silly creatures" giving intelligent cubes a bad name [ch 037 / 037.06].
- Zalthir struck a cube twice and retreated; Thorin threw darts; Gyrgum channeled Divine Spark; cubes engulfed gnomes, and one gnome died dissolving inside a cube [ch 037 / 037.06].
- Daz carefully placed two Fireballs that burned the cubes; Zalthir activated his Eldritch Claw Tattoo (recharging about an hour before dawn) and struck at range [ch 037 / 037.06].
- Glabbagool asked whether Zalthir was an ooze; Zalthir replied, "No, no, just an aspiring ooze. Let's say that" [ch 037 / 037.06].
- Zalthir finished the last cube from afar just as Thorin was preparing to strike; Thorin complained, "Damn it. Let me have this" [ch 037 / 037.06].
- After the battle with gelatinous cubes in the Traders' Grotto, the party faced a crowd of questioning merchants and citizens; one citizen complained, "Taxes, we pay taxes," while a sergeant attempted to credit Chief Dorbo Diggermattock, declaring, "Chief Dorbo Diggermattock hired these adventurers to solve our problems. Chief Dorbo Diggermattock is on top of everything" [ch 038 / 038.01].
- A citizen praised Senni Diggermattock as an advocate for ordinary people, accusing Chief Dorbo of caring only for "the gold from the gems from the mines," saying "Everything is sacrificed on the altar of wealth" [ch 038 / 038.01].
- When the party suggested Dorbo spend wealth to rebuild damaged businesses, the sergeant called it "an excellent suggestion" but urged them to leave for their assigned mission, becoming nervous about their "revolutionary tactics" [ch 038 / 038.01].
- Glabbagool wondered whether he had been hired and would be paid; when "friends" was proposed as what the gelatinous cube who has everything might want, he replied, "Yes, I think I could do with some friends," and the party welcomed him along [ch 038 / 038.01].
- The party recovered a Short Sword +1 amid the aftermath, which Daz ultimately claimed as an upgrade [ch 038 / 038.01; ch 038 / items].
- The sergeant restated the mission — Blingdenstone has a serious ooze problem and cannot help the party leave while at risk of being overrun — pointing toward a rubble-choked route [ch 038 / 038.01; ch 038 / 038.02].
- A merchant recommended the Foaming Mug for its Darklake Stout and beds large enough for the party, comparing housing the adventurers to "trying to house a giraffe" [ch 038 / 038.02].
- The Chamber of Commerce granted the party a ten percent discount on purchases; when the offer sounded like an advertisement, the merchant wrote them a coupon [ch 038 / 038.02].
- The party learned the route led toward the disputed Goldwhisker Warrens; the sergeant dismissed the Goldwhiskers as wererat squatters, muttering they were "wererat gnomes," while merchants offered a more nuanced warning [ch 038 / 038.02].
- The party recalled former wererat companions Topsy and Turvy and recognized the dispute: drow conquered and abandoned the area, gnomish wererats occupied it, then Clan Diggermattock returned, claimed it, and began to "gentrify it" [ch 038 / 038.02].
- Glabbagool acted as a living bulldozer, engulfing loose stones and releasing them elsewhere; after about an hour he cleared a usable passage, impressing watching deep gnomes [ch 038 / 038.02].
- A deep gnome invited Glabbagool to visit the Foaming Mug after the mission to discuss mining work; Glabbagool delighted in the prospect, imagining people saying "Glabbagool, we need you" [ch 038 / 038.02].
- Glabbagool identified with Zalthir as a fellow ooze and kindred spirit, the pair defining themselves as "friendly oozes" [ch 038 / 038.02].
- Beyond the rubble, the party entered an abandoned residential cave where Daz discovered decomposing matter and two slimy, partially digested deep gnome corpses, victims of an ooze, and traced an ooze trail into a hallway [ch 038 / 038.03].
- Glabbagool moved toward the trail with unusual purpose, saying, "I can hear voices calling me," excited by the prospect of other speaking oozes; the party feared demonic influence [ch 038 / 038.03].
- Zalthir persuaded Glabbagool to let his friends go first; Glabbagool agreed, declaring, "You're my friend, so we should all go together," and praised the party for protecting him from "the bad oozes" [ch 038 / 038.03].
- Thorin led with Zalthir beside him; Zalthir detected an oddly placed pit trap along the ooze trail, and the party crossed safely [ch 038 / 038.03].
- At an intersection, Glabbagool indicated the voices came from the south; the party proceeded "onward and southward" [ch 038 / 038.03].
- The southern passage opened into an immense cavern — an "ooza palooza" with more oozes than the party had ever seen — with green slime on the walls and a great spherical structure on stone pillars at the center; engaging directly would kill the party [ch 038 / 038.04].
- A disembodied voice proclaimed the coming of the "Faceless Lord," and the assembled oozes urged Glabbagool to join them; he refused, sensing "something not nice" and reasoning, "Oozes don't talk. Something's weird" [ch 038 / 038.04].
- Gyrgum recalled that Juiblex is known as the Faceless Lord; the party remembered a ghost's explanation that devils make contracts while demons infuse people or objects with power, and concluded Juiblex was working through a proxy rather than appearing personally [ch 038 / 038.04].
- Daz summoned a bat familiar named Batman, who conducted a breadth-first reconnaissance finding abandoned gnomish settlement remnants, dripping caves with scattered oozes, a destroyed fungal partition, and wreckage scoured clean [ch 038 / 038.04].
- Batman found a cave illuminated by phosphorescent lichen with pools and drapes of green slime and a slime-covered throne facing east, where a small gnome sat holding court "in the insane way an insane madman holds court" [ch 038 / 038.04].
- The gnome on the throne spoke of cleansing Blingdenstone of everyone who had irritated him and declared he was the Pudding King and would become king of Blingdenstone; his voice matched the one projected through the ooze cavern [ch 038 / 038.04].
- Daz received inspiration for the successful reconnaissance that identified the enemy without exposing the party [ch 038 / 038.04].
- Zalthir devised a plan to Shadow Step up to sixty feet in dim light, reach the Pudding King, grapple him, and fly away with him, but green slime on the throne room ceiling complicated the escape [ch 038 / 038.05].
- The party realized incapacitating the Pudding King could release hundreds of uncontrolled oozes to wander through Blingdenstone in search of food, requiring a large coordinated distraction and defenders to contain the army [ch 038 / 038.05].
- Glabbagool warned that oozes could squeeze through extremely small gaps, making improvised rubble barriers unreliable [ch 038 / 038.05].
- Gyrgum offered to motivate Blingdenstone's troops with a speech, imagining a rallying cry: "We will fight them in the pudding court. We will fight them on the rubble. We will fight them by the glowing pool" [ch 038 / 038.05].
- The party concluded they needed the city's army and possibly Clan Goldwhisker, noting the common war might provide "an opportunity for reconciliation," and decided to report to Dorbo first, then secure the Goldwhiskers' aid [ch 038 / 038.05].
- The party returned to Blingdenstone, where Chief Dorbo demanded, "Has my ooze problem been dealt with?"; after hearing about the Pudding King and hundreds of oozes, he called it "awful. This is terrible. This is cataclysmical. This is a catastrophe" [ch 038 / 038.06].
- Gyrgum presented the plan: "The plan is I make a motivational speech to your army, and they distract the oozes while we kill their king"; Dorbo called it "the dumbest plan I've heard ever," but Senni told him he had spoken enough and asked whether he had a better one — he did not [ch 038 / 038.06].
- Zalthir demonstrated Shadow Step in the dimmed chamber, teleporting around with flips and airborne punches; Senni summarized that Zalthir would infiltrate, seize the Pudding King, and kill or extract him while Blingdenstone's forces occupied the ooze army [ch 038 / 038.06].
- Jimjar made a bet in the party's favor, telling Dorbo, "I'll make you a bet that by the time this is all done, we're all going to be living happily ever after," which helped move the skeptical chief [ch 038 / 038.06].
- Dorbo argued Blingdenstone's forces lacked preparation, allies, and equipment; Senni called for a full clan meeting involving the Stoneheart Enclave and the Miners' Guild [ch 038 / 038.06].
- Dorbo tried to exclude Clan Goldwhisker, insisting "They're not even gnomes"; Senni corrected that they were lycanthropes who merely turn into rats, and their resistance to non-silver weapons made them invaluable against oozes [ch 038 / 038.06].
- When Dorbo suggested the Goldwhiskers could participate only if they acknowledged his authority, Daz seized control, declaring, "We figured it out, and we're going to be in charge of it," and the room fell silent at the "management coup" [ch 038 / 038.06].
- Daz laid out the bargain: "You're going to get us out of the Underdark, and we're going to solve this problem for you, and we're going to do it our way. So you're on board" [ch 038 / 038.06].
- Senni accepted the new command structure, telling the adventurers, "You bring the Goldwhisker clans, and I think we could make that happen"; the party set out to recruit Clan Goldwhisker [ch 038 / 038.06].
- Daz recognized that Chief Dorbo had yielded operational control to use him as a "fall guy": if the plan failed, Dorbo could blame the drow who seized control; if it succeeded, he could claim supporting Daz was his own design [ch 039 / 039.01].
- Daz reframed Senni's appeal for common community, declaring: "It's important for us to have a common outcome and a common drive to that outcome. The rest of the commonality would be a nice-to-have" [ch 039 / 039.01].
- Gnome bureaucrats began writing down Daz's remarks as leadership aphorisms while papers shuffled around the chamber [ch 039 / 039.01].
- Thorin, driven by completionist curiosity, opened the eastern door of the bureaucratic hub and discovered a kitchen/break room where a gnome chef mistook the party for the newly hired heroes [ch 039 / 039.02].
- Thorin asserted the deal included "unlimited access to the snack room"; the chef agreed to provide a snack, but Chief Dorbo insisted the party eat at the Foaming Mug instead [ch 039 / 039.02].
- The party declined to send Glabbagool into Dorbo's private quarters, though Glabbagool volunteered [ch 039 / 039.02].
- The chef asked Glabbagool to clean the kitchen floor; Glabbagool eagerly agreed, and Thorin nicknamed him "Roomba" [ch 039 / 039.02].
- The party approached the monumental double doors of the Goldwhisker Warrens and considered sending Glabbagool beneath to scout, but rejected it fearing the wererats would mistake him for a hostile ooze [ch 039 / 039.03].
- Daz announced through the doors: "Hi, we're here to uh rid the Warrens and the grotto in the general vicinity of the slime problem. We're gonna need your help" [ch 039 / 039.03].
- A guard told the party not to leave and scampered off to consult Chief Chipgrin, who then requested they be brought inside [ch 039 / 039.03].
- Four gnome wererat guards escorted the party through the warrens; the party's non-judgmental attitude delighted the guards, and a philosophical debate about rat ecology broke out (one guard: "See, I told you") [ch 039 / 039.04].
- Chief Chipgrin assumed the Diggermattocks sent the party to drive his clan out; Daz explained the party wanted to leave the Underdark and needed help eliminating the ooze problem [ch 039 / 039.05].
- Daz offered to arrange a meeting with Dorbo on strict terms: cooperation, not "political wrangling," positioning, recrimination, or "I told you so"; otherwise the party would leave [ch 039 / 039.05].
- Chipgrin remarked, "I've been told that Drow are a difficult group to negotiate with, but you take it to the next level"; Daz replied, "It's not a negotiation. I'm here to ask you for your help" [ch 039 / 039.05].
- Chipgrin agreed to fight but refused to place his clan under Dorbo's command or expend them in a "grand crusade"; Daz accepted personal responsibility for any Goldwhisker deaths [ch 039 / 039.05].
- Chipgrin agreed to attend a meeting of the clan leaders ("Well, then, I think we can have a deal") [ch 039 / 039.05].
- Chipgrin shared what he knew of the Pudding King: he had known the gnome when he worked in the tunnels, his kin witnessed the gnome's return, and he knew what the Pudding King was doing and where he could be found [ch 039 / 039.06].
- The party concluded the Pudding King's fall combined vengeance, pride, madness, resentment and demonic power that amplified his worst qualities rather than replacing his personality [ch 039 / 039.06].
- Chipgrin asked if the party had noticed anything odd about Blingdenstone's other gnomes; they recognized abnormal greed and aggression, with Dorbo's profit fixation seeming unhinged [ch 039 / 039.06].
- The party shared its theory about Juiblex with Chipgrin, who connected it to the demonic effects they had witnessed in Gracklstugh, Neverlight Grove, and among the kuo-toa [ch 039 / 039.06].
- The group concluded the corruption was centered on the Pudding King but "bleeding through" into the broader community [ch 039 / 039.06].
- Daz used an advantaged appeal with matching results of 17 to secure agreement to hold a moot within a few hours [ch 039 / 039.07].
- The moot gathered Chief Dorbo, Senni, Chief Chipgrin, Nomi Pathshutter, Gurnik Tapfinger, Kazook Pickshine, and the ghostly Burrow Warden Uth-Jadger; Senni asked Daz to call the meeting to order [ch 039 / 039.07].
- Daz summarized the plan: all able-bodied gnomes would launch a full-scale assault on the ooze gathering while the party moved covertly to snatch the Pudding King [ch 039 / 039.07].
- Uth-Jadger praised the plan with "Bravo, Bravo," calling it the technical brilliance of the Burrow Wardens in their prime [ch 039 / 039.07].
- Dorbo proposed attacking from the Temple of the Steadfast Stone to draw oozes away from populated areas; Uth-Jadger noted the Rockblight must first be cleansed [ch 039 / 039.08].
- Gurnik produced a red spell gem containing Hallow that must be placed in the temple's menhir and defended until three temple guardians awakened; Ogrémoch's Bane would send servants to remove it [ch 039 / 039.08].
- The party asked why the army could not attack from the north; the room's silence revealed the gnome leaders were using the adventurers to solve additional civic problems [ch 039 / 039.08].
- The leaders hastily praised the northern approach, while Gurnik looked increasingly concerned his temple-cleansing opportunity was slipping away [ch 039 / 039.08].
- Gurnik and Nomi delivered an obviously rehearsed exchange about Earth Elementals being ideal against oozes but vulnerable to Ogrémoch's Bane; cleansing the Steadfast Stone would let the Stoneheart Enclave protect them [ch 039 / 039.08].
- Kazook warned ooze acid could destroy weapons and armor; he could make a protective treatment with ingredients from Neverlight Grove, or the gnomes could trade salt to Gracklstugh for weapons [ch 039 / 039.09].
- Uth-Jadger said other Burrow Warden spirits would join the fight if the party completed a quest for them [ch 039 / 039.09].
- Dorbo's committee offered a Stone of Controlling Earth Elementals as payment for cleansing the Rockblight [ch 039 / 039.09].
- The party objected to enslaving an elemental (Zalthir: "I don't want to enslave an Earth Elemental") and speculated about befriending it, giving it its own control stone "like giving Dobby a sock" [ch 039 / 039.09].
- The party debated ignoring preparations entirely; Zalthir noted acid-corroded armor mattered less to an unarmored monk, but Daz observed having the army killed would "not look good on us" [ch 039 / 039.10].
- Thorin rolled a physical d20 (a 17, above the "10 is safety. Below 10 is YOLO" threshold), selecting caution [ch 039 / 039.10].
- The party chose to cleanse the Rockblight first and to revisit Neverlight Grove for acid-protection ingredients; they rejected the Gracklstugh option due to uncertainty about the city and their troubled history there [ch 039 / 039.10].
- The party assigned Glabbagool, Eldeth, and Jimjar to assist Uth-Jadger and the other Burrow Warden ghosts while the main group handled the Rockblight and Neverlight Grove [ch 039 / 039.10].
- The session ended with the players reflecting on Daz's leadership: he seized command when political doublespeak made action impossible but deferred once meaningful decisions had to be made [ch 039 / 039.11].
- Daz insisted, "I'm not the decision maker. I just couldn't put up with the BS anymore" [ch 039 / 039.11].
- Eldeth, Jimjar, and Spiderbait took on the ghost problem; Glabbagool initially volunteered to help but the plan changed and he stayed with the main party, wondering aloud, “Would I be the first gelatinous cube ghost?” [ch 040 / 040.01; ch 040 / npcs]
- Burrow Warden Jadger wished the ghost-hunting group well and reassured them that if they died, they could still fight in the coming battle as ghosts [ch 040 / 040.01; ch 040 / npcs].
- The party chose the Rockblight as its first objective because it lay close to their current position, and planned a long rest before setting out [ch 040 / 040.01].
- That evening the party rested at the Foaming Mug Tavern, a staggeringly large cave converted into an inn built for visitors to Blingdenstone, with beds sized for dwarves, elves, dragonborn, ogres and other “normal-sized” guests [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / locations].
- The tavern served Darklake Stout imported through Mantol-Derith; its proprietor, Tappy Foamstrap, was a bored deep gnome desperate for new stories [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / npcs].
- Tavern patrons reported that the ghost problem was worsening, with spirits now haunting Blingdenstone’s catacombs [ch 040 / 040.02].
- Scouts had reportedly seen a crazy gnome from the first days of the reclamation skulking through unrecovered portions of the settlement, whom the party suspected to be the Pudding King [ch 040 / 040.02].
- The Stoneheart Enclave was said to be “closing in on a solution” to Ogrémoch’s Bane, a claim that amused the party, since the solution was evidently to send them to deal with it [ch 040 / 040.02].
- Debate continued over the wererats and whether they should be considered gnomes at all [ch 040 / 040.02].
- A merchant recently returned from Gracklstugh described the duergar city as outwardly calm but deeply on edge, with authorities having imposed controls while blaming the Ember Vanguard for recent chaos [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / npcs].
- The merchant carried a wanted poster reading “wanted dead or alive,” with “alive” misspelled; he noticed the party’s resemblance to the wanted fugitives but reassured himself because the poster depicted a red rather than gold dragonborn, and an older and more fearsome drow, orc, and dwarf [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / npcs].
- The merchant mentioned rumors that the Assassin’s Guild had returned, an influx of unexplained surface coins, and summary executions, decapitations, and derro heads displayed on pikes, with the duergar emphatic that the derro needed to “learn their place” [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / npcs].
- The merchant described Gracklstugh’s peculiarly portly red dragon, used as an extravagant matchlighter and distinguished by a double chin and two bellies [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / npcs].
- The party was relieved that their goal of leaving Gracklstugh without causing the city to burn down had been achieved, since the city remained intact and had not collapsed into open war [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / locations].
- A deep gnome returning from the Whiteshell Mine reported encountering a parade of dancing myconids communicating through Rapport Spores and announcing they were on their way to a wedding celebration; he briefly called it a “sweating celebration,” then corrected himself [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / npcs].
- The scout noted myconids do not normally marry and no one he knew had ever heard of them holding celebrations; the party connected the procession to Zuggtmoy’s intended wedding and suspected Neverlight Grove was emptying or its inhabitants had gone insane [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / npcs].
- Glabbagool cleaned the tavern floor by absorbing spilled drinks, became intoxicated, loopier than usual, and began singing traditional gelatinous cube sea shanties [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / moments].
- The accidental discovery inspired the party to consider using alcohol against hostile oozes and puddings [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / moments].
- The next morning the party asked Tappy for barrels of old or spoiled booze; he agreed on condition that its source never be attributed to the Foaming Mug to protect its reputation [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / npcs].
- The party countered that Tappy might instead advertise himself as the hero whose alcohol saved the city; Tappy agreed if Blingdenstone’s leaders would provide an official notice he could display behind the bar [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / npcs].
- The party discussed using Shape Water, a seltzer bottle, mugs, or other improvised delivery mechanisms to spray the alcohol [ch 040 / 040.02; ch 040 / spells].
- The party traveled to the Cultivation Cave, a vast complex where the deep gnomes grew useful fungi for cooking and crafting, managed by the Stoneheart Enclave [ch 040 / 040.03; ch 040 / locations].

- Glabbagool enthusiastically ate mushrooms and plowed through the dirt and gravel blocking the alternate entrance to the Rockblight, spraying debris aside like a gigantic transparent snowblower; after several hours of excavation, the party reached the caverns [ch 040 / 040.03; ch 040 / locations].
- Glabbagool initially appeared prepared to remain behind for “more important activities,” but happily accompanied the party after they made it clear they wanted his company [ch 040 / 040.03].
- In the Rockblight, the party entered a chamber where bioluminescent fungi shone through semi-transparent crystal formations, casting a kaleidoscope of colors across the walls [ch 040 / 040.04; ch 040 / locations].
- They found a stone statue of a deep gnome cowering beside a crystal outgrowth, holding a broken crystal toward his face as though trying to peer through it [ch 040 / 040.04].
- Thorin’s natural 20 Arcana check, combined with Daz’s expertise, revealed that looking through the crystals protects against creatures capable of harming or petrifying victims with their gaze, effectively making the crystals magical sunglasses against a Medusa [ch 040 / 040.04; ch 040 / moments].
- The petrified gnome had probably been trying to use the crystal but failed to position it correctly before being turned to stone [ch 040 / 040.04].
- The party warned Glabbagool not to eat the crystals and collected portable crystals in several colors, including a teal one [ch 040 / 040.04; ch 040 / items].
- The crystals’ colors did not alter this particular protective property; Thorin recalled that a crystal’s magic depended more on where and how it grew, while Daz noted different colors could still produce different effects when combined with particular spells [ch 040 / 040.04].
- The party knew the temple lay to the northeast or east but chose to explore first, reasoning the crystal chamber had already provided something potentially useful [ch 040 / 040.04].
- Exploring farther, the party entered abandoned residential caverns devoid of warmth or joy and marked by carnage from the old drow assault [ch 040 / 040.05; ch 040 / locations].
- A furious deep gnome ghost appeared and attacked; Zalthir surrounded it in magical darkness he could see through and struck it repeatedly with his fists [ch 040 / 040.05].

- Gyrgum recalled that frightened ghosts sometimes retreat to their lairs—and possibly their treasure—and reasoned, “Well, then I bet a ghost that’s been hit with a mace will be even more frightened,” striking it once more without destroying it [ch 040 / 040.05; ch 040 / moments].
- The ghost disengaged, turned ethereal, and fled into a nearby burrow; the party followed and found skeletal remains beside a stone bed [ch 040 / 040.05].
- Just before vanishing, the ghost had appeared to reach beneath the bed, leading the party to uncover a hidden compartment containing six gems worth 600 gold pieces each and a potion of invisibility [ch 040 / 040.05; ch 040 / items].
- The bones bore no signs of violent death, leaving the exact cause of death unknown; the remains belonged to Udhask, one of the spirits Burrow Warden Jadger wanted rescued [ch 040 / 040.05; ch 040 / npcs].
- The party briefly felt guilty about taking Udhask’s stash but noted that he had attacked them, and collected his bones to return to the temple and restore the ghost [ch 040 / 040.05; ch 040 / npcs].
- Continuing south past a waterfall, the party reached a shallow, swiftly flowing stream with crystalline formations on both sides and rough-hewn stairs climbing thirty feet to a barren ledge occupied by two winged statues [ch 040 / 040.06; ch 040 / locations].

- Before the party could finish the remaining gargoyle, a crazed earth elemental erupted from the ground near the water and prepared to attack, confirming that warnings about elementals being driven mad in the Rockblight were true [ch 040 / 040.06; ch 040 / npcs].

- The Galeb Duhr explained the temple had been hallowed from the corrupting power of Ogrémoch, the evil elemental earth god who had infested the rocks for centuries; they squabbled over whether explaining Ogrémoch would be "insulting" before describing him. [ch 043 / 043.01]

- The Galeb Duhr sat down next to the menhir and stared at it after their long explanation. [ch 043 / 043.01]

- The party fought a Shambling Mound and charmed Violet Fungus in the Festering Fissure; Violet Fungus remained stunned and charmed while Zalthir was grappled with the Mound ("We're passionately grappling in both directions") [ch 044 / 044.01]
- Daz cast Fireball, sculpting it to avoid Zalthir and the lichen, targeting only the Violet Fungus area, warned by the DM that the fireball might destroy the lichen [ch 044 / 044.01]
- Daz rolled a natural 20 on a check and learned the Mound is resistant to cold and fire and immune to lightning; the DM suggested radiant or force damage [ch 044 / 044.01]
- Daz cast Magic Missile dealing only 6 total damage, which "the target barely registers" [ch 044 / 044.01]
- Gyrgum cast Guiding Bolt hitting for 13 radiant damage (attack 17 vs AC 15) granting advantage to the next attack [ch 044 / 044.01]
- The Shambling Mound used its reaction to try to lure Daz closer, but Daz resisted with a Wisdom save of 14 [ch 044 / 044.01]
- The Shambling Mound hit Thorin for 10 damage, its second attack of the round landing with a 23 [ch 044 / 044.01]
- Zalthir punched the Mound multiple times with advantage from Guiding Bolt and his grappling feat, landing hits for 10 and 9 damage [ch 044 / 044.01]
- Thorin knocked the Mound prone with a Hill Strike (13 damage plus 5, failed DC 15 Strength save) and landed a follow-up for 10 [ch 044 / 044.01]
- Daz's Maximilian's Earthen Grasp and Gyrgum's Tasha's Caustic Brew both failed when the Mound rolled back-to-back natural 20s [ch 044 / 044.01]
- The Shambling Mound attacked Zalthir, who used Deflect Attacks to take no damage, and Thorin's opportunity attack dealt the last 8 points, killing it [ch 044 / 044.01]
- Gyrgum chanted during the lichen harvest; the party made three checks — Arcana (Daz 22), Nature (26, with Gyrgum's +7), and Sleight of Hand (failed) — succeeding on two of three [ch 044 / 044.02]
- The party realized they needed to deal with the Pudding King to save the gnomes from Zuggtmoy's plot [ch 044 / 044.03]
- The DM recapped the Entémoch quest: after the hollow was hallowed, the gnome leaders asked where Entémoch's boon was, and the wizard said the tunnels to his throne have "shifted and sealed themselves" [ch 044 / 044.03]
- The party arrived at Pickshine Mine and found four Galeb Duhr blocking the path, vibrating rhythmically and ignoring Dasco Pickshine's order to move "By the contract of the Stoneheart Enclave" [ch 044 / 044.04]
- Daz perceived the Galeb Duhr's vibrations as linguistic, 'a call waiting for an echo that never returns'; Gyrgum sensed they were sentinels waiting for a divine 'signature' [ch 044 / 044.04]
- Gyrgum held up his holy symbol, making the Galeb Duhr pause and look at him [ch 044 / 044.04]
- Daz rolled Investigation 21 to discover resonant geodes, then arranged them and hummed, rolling Arcana 21 to complete the 'handshake protocol' [ch 044 / 044.04]
- The Galeb Duhr relaxed and opened the path; the geode began to resonate and point toward Whiteshell Mine, acting as a signal tracer [ch 044 / 044.04]
- Dasco Pickshine initially said the party shouldn't expect payment; Daz threatened to undo the fix, and Dasco then offered two ruby gems worth 500 gold pieces through a crowd of miners [ch 044 / 044.04]
- The party followed the geode, which was "humming violently and pulling magnetically toward the south directly at the Whiteshell salt mine" [ch 044 / 044.05]
- At Whiteshell Mine, Perigrog Scrapedust explained that a tunnel had become impassable: impossibly dry, with blinding crystallite walls and absolute suffocating silence; even the lizards refused to go down it [ch 044 / 044.05]
- Zalthir recalled a metaphysical puzzle from his training: magical pressure had snagged time, and breaking it directly would cause a catastrophic explosion crushing the tunnels; only a Shadow Monk could create a hole for the backlog of reality [ch 044 / 044.05]
- The gnomes presented their specialized explosives ('rude powder'); Zalthir meditated and channeled his frustration into the void, Gyrgum offered Bless, and the DM granted +2 and advantage [ch 044 / 044.06]
- Zalthir rolled a 23 on Acrobatics, executing a perfect Shadow Step as the explosives detonated, safely redirecting the magical blast through shadow space without collapsing the tunnels [ch 044 / 044.06; ch 044 / moments]
- The party took a short rest before proceeding through the newly opened passage [ch 044 / 044.06]
- Beyond the wall, the party found a large cavern with a summoning circle inscribed with runes in giant script, guarded by two basilisks moving with mechanical precision [ch 044 / 044.07]
- The party worked through petrification rules: the gaze is a DC 12 Constitution save in a 30-foot cone, triggered by seeing the basilisk, averted eyes mean fighting blinded, magical darkness blocks the gaze, and Greater Restoration is the cure (the DM called Stone to Flesh an older-edition rule) [ch 044 / 044.07]
- Initiating combat, Zalthir engaged one basilisk in melee while averting his eyes, bringing his darkness, landing hits for 14, 11, and 11 damage and grappling it [ch 044 / 044.07]
- Daz cast Hypnotic Pattern on the second basilisk, incapacitating it (failed DC 15 Wisdom save against its -4) and reducing its speed to zero [ch 044 / 044.07]
- Thorin rushed to the summoning circle and deciphered the giant runes, learning that freeing Entémoch requires three sacrifices: wealth, a weapon, and blood [ch 044 / 044.08]
- Thorin placed a dart onto the runes as the weapon sacrifice; the stone swallowed it instantly, rusting it to dust, and the runes transformed from yellow to blinding orange with the air growing hot [ch 044 / 044.08]
- The uncharmed basilisk became frantic as the ritual began, while the other one was "still chilling"; the session ended with two sacrifices remaining [ch 044 / 044.08]
- The party completed the ancient ritual at Entémoch's Boon while fending off two basilisks; Thorin, who speaks Giant, directed the ritual with his spare dart already in the circle as the weapon sacrifice [ch 045 / 045.01].
- One basilisk remained charmed and immobile under Daz's Hypnotic Pattern, receiving no saving throw to break free [ch 045 / 045.01].
- The second basilisk lurked inside Zalthir's magical darkness; Daz first considered a sculpted Fireball but switched to Scorching Ray, missing all three rays (including a natural 1) because he could not see the target [ch 045 / 045.01; ch 045 / spells].
- Zalthir engaged the basilisk in melee, missing twice then landing a 26 to hit for 12 damage (leaving it at 4 HP), then finishing it with a punch; it died "in a burning gusto of flame" [ch 045 / 045.01; ch 045 / npcs].
- The GM ruled that a zero-damage punch would not wake the charmed basilisk, so the party left it alone since they only needed three rounds [ch 045 / 045.01].
- The party placed the two gems beside Thorin's dart, the ground rumbled with a soothing vibration, and "that which was wrong about the rocks has now become right," releasing Entémoch's boon [ch 045 / 045.01].
- The party gained the ability to summon Earth Elementals for the gnomes of Blingdenstone and a homebrew Stone Skin buff lasting one week of in-game time; Zalthir volunteered to remind the party [ch 045 / 045.01; ch 045 / spells].
- Dasco Pickshine was delighted that the gnomes could now hire Earth Elementals for mining, since they "don't cost a lot other than the summoning spell," prompting Thorin to object to subjugating them as unpaid workers [ch 045 / 045.02].
- The party met with Senni Diggermattock, Chief Dorbo, and Chipgrin to advocate that Earth Elementals be used for defense but not hard labor; Thorin argued "you can have this under the condition that you use it just for fighting, not for hard labor" [ch 045 / 045.02].
- Insight checks revealed tales of the Ember Vanguard's rampage in Gracklstugh had reached Blingdenstone's bars, with some speculating it was "merely the first foray of Menzoberranzan's plans to take over other parts of the Underdark"; the party stayed incognito [ch 045 / 045.02].
- Gyrgum argued on religious grounds that Bahamut and the good dragons oppose enslaving summoned creatures; the local clerics agreed it violated the Gnomish gods [ch 045 / 045.02].
- Zalthir argued from a monastic perspective for peace, harmony, light and shadow, and Daz compared elemental labor to Menzoberranzan's slave society, which was "the killer" [ch 045 / 045.02; ch 045 / memorable].
- With three of four natural 20s on persuasion checks, Senni seized control of the meeting from Chief Dorbo, formed "a coalition of the willing" against the Pudding King, and shouted "Gnome Labor, not Summoned Labor!" [ch 045 / 045.02; ch 045 / memorable].
- The leaders decided elementals could be used for war but not for building or labor, disrupting Blingdenstone's economic plans and making the party "the toasts of the town"; Thorin conceded remuneration would have to wait, "one thing at a time" [ch 045 / 045.02].
- During the pre-battle montage, Kazook Pickshine brewed the lichen into a weapon coating, Nomi prepared summoning spells, Chief Dorbo told the Captain of the Wall "prepare your men for combat. For tomorrow we shall go," and the Pudding King readied his oozes [ch 045 / 045.03; ch 045 / npcs].
- The traveling merchant Starlace set up Starlace Curios and Arcana in the Traders' Grotto; the party used their 10% coupon while shopping before taking a long rest [ch 045 / 045.03].
- Daz purchased Dustsight Spectacles (able to see invisible creatures and, per GM ruling, through Zalthir's magical darkness) [ch 045 / 045.03; ch 045 / items].
- Gyrgum had his mace enchanted to +1 for 360 gold, leaving him with exactly 1,024 gold pieces [ch 045 / 045.03; ch 045 / items].
- Thorin had his shield enchanted to +1 for 270 gold, bringing his AC to 20, and purchased the Stonegnarl Warband Token for a +1 AC bonus near stone for 270 gold after the discount [ch 045 / 045.03; ch 045 / items].
- The party sold their studded leather armor +2 to Starlace for 600 gold after both sides rolled natural 20s in a persuasion contest; both sides walked away feeling good [ch 045 / 045.03; ch 045 / items].
- The party sought out Valimor Brightgem at The Gilded Sheath to commission a scabbard for Dawnbringer; Valimor offered a 600-gold gem-encrusted showpiece or a practical Vaultmaster locking scabbard [ch 045 / 045.04].
- A humorous marketing exchange saw Thorin's "Vaultmaster 9000" suggestion and Gyrgum's product-tier ideas open Valimor's eyes to "the possibility of marketing and financial management," leading him to sell the locking scabbard for 200 gold (180 after discount) [ch 045 / 045.04; ch 045 / memorable].
- Dawnbringer was pleased to finally have a proper scabbard, though the sword muttered about adding bling [ch 045 / 045.04].
- The GM ruled Dustsight Spectacles see through magical darkness with 10 one-minute charges per day, and announced the heroic speech would come next week as the party heads out to fight; Thorin's player announced he would be away until March 2nd [ch 045 / 045.04].
- Chief Dorbo Diggermattock gave an impassioned speech before the assembled forces, declaring "Today is the day. Today we will triumph. Today is victory," pointing to Daz as the leader who would bring victory, then produced a map of the whole area he had apparently owned all along and never shown the party [ch 046 / 046.01].
- Dorbo laid out a battle plan: the party would break through the wall cleared by the Galeb Duhr and enter the Pudding King's court from the south while his forces attacked from another direction to distract the puddings, offering three ally groups: an elemental imbued with Entémoch's power, wererats led by Chipgrin, and the Pickshine Miners under Dasco Pickshine [ch 046 / 046.01].
- The party debated the plan; Zalthir recalled the earlier conclusion that once the Pudding King is taken the slimes revert to "vanilla boring old slimes," Senni Diggermattock worried about civilian areas and that undistracted oozes could hit the party with "an almost infinite supply," and reactions to a big battle were mixed among the forces [ch 046 / 046.01].
- The party settled on a minimal distraction, a probing feint, instead of a full assault, with Daz telling Dorbo "we have a plan that requires us to be discreet and careful" [ch 046 / 046.01].
- Dasco Pickshine promoted his brand, offering "branded tea" he buys and packages rather than mines, producing a Dasco Pickshine patch he personally sewed onto Zalthir's clothes after Zalthir asked for stickers, with everything Dasco wore bearing the Pickshine logo [ch 046 / 046.02].
- Gyrgum asked whether any ally had good stealth; the wererats took offense at the implication they would be noisy ("we are wererats"), and the party decided to bring the allies along but keep them at a distance in area 22, with Dorbo's forces positioned next to the Traders' Grotto and the wererats in the pudding warrens [ch 046 / 046.03].
- A wererat scout reported a 20-foot stretch of passage hollowed into a massive, semi-sentient basin of black pudding under an acid-dripping ceiling; Dasco Pickshine offered to bridge it with mithral braces and salt neutralizers, with his miners staying behind to maintain the anchors so they could not back up the party at the Pudding King's doors, and Daz accepted [ch 046 / 046.04].
- The Pickshine Miners built the bridge in about twenty minutes while singing songs, working with screws and magical devices under anti-acid canopies [ch 046 / 046.04].
- Jimjar bet five gold that Thorin couldn't clear the gap with his pack on without specifying how; Gyrgum's insight check of 20 showed him Jimjar never said how Thorin had to cross, and Gyrgum persuaded Thorin to walk across the bridge once it was built, with Dawnbringer arguing against jumping into acid [ch 046 / 046.05].
- Gyrgum told the wererat scout to keep scouting then caught himself, noting Daz was meant to give orders; Chipgrin insisted on a clear chain of command, and Daz named Gyrgum, Zalthir, and Thorin as lieutenants who could also give orders [ch 046 / 046.06].
- The wererat scout went further and did not return; after about ten minutes Chipgrin went after his own people without waiting for permission and found the scout frozen in terror in a tunnel where the salt walls had softened into sheets of gelatinous slime acting as dark, weeping mirrors, with a "big brain slime" in the ceiling singing a psychic song [ch 046 / 046.06].
- Chipgrin offered a choice: his pack would howl at a frequency to drown out the psychic effect, drawing every ooze in the vents onto the wererats and leaving the party without scouts or backup at the throne room door; the party accepted the offer [ch 046 / 046.06].
- Chipgrin bet ten gold that Daz's reflection in the psychic slime mirrors would be better looking than the real thing, daring him to look; Daz refused, saying "I'm not playing parlor games," and Chipgrin replied this "explains why this man should be the general" [ch 046 / 046.06].
- Daz's bat familiar found a grand vaulted corridor blocked by a colossal ochre jelly filling the hall from floor to ceiling, pulsing in time with the Pudding King's heartbeat, with the passage to the throne room open and visible through it [ch 046 / 046.07].
- The party debated options for bypassing the jelly: Glabbagool offered to carry them inside himself with acid burns and oxygen deprivation, the Earth Elemental indicated it could smash through as it was immune to the acid, and Zalthir planned to teleport past [ch 046 / 046.07].
- The party asked whether the Pudding King could control Glabbagool; Glabbagool promised he would never hurt his friends and recalled once hearing a voice calling him at the big pool of slimes, and the GM made clear asking Glabbagool to attack the King directly was untested [ch 046 / 046.08].
- The GM established out of character the community's view that the Pudding King had allied with a demon to kill everyone and convert their biomass into ooze biomass [ch 046 / 046.08].
- The Pudding King, standing and furious, monologued to his pets Princess Ebonmire (a black pudding) and Prince Livid (a gray one), promising Blingdenstone would become "Blingdenpuddle," and Gyrgum answered "Do not gloat. We still plot." [ch 046 / 046.09].
- The party sent the signal and the distraction force attacked; the Pudding King cheered as he described members of the Blingdenstone army being consumed by oozes and coached his troops to "Ooze them slowly" [ch 046 / 046.09].
- Zalthir activated his Eldritch Claw Tattoo and tentacles, the Earth Elemental smashed through the ochre jelly barricade and threw him 30 feet into the throne room, Zalthir teleported another 60 feet via Shadow Step, closed the rest on foot, and grappled the Pudding King with his tentacles from reach [ch 046 / 046.09].
- The Pudding King turned into ooze form in response to being grappled; the GM confirmed oozes can be grappled rules-as-written and ruled the ooze automatically hits its grappler, but Zalthir maintained the grapple and dragged the King 15 feet south, away from his ooze allies [ch 046 / 046.09].
- The GM awarded Zalthir inspiration for the tactic, and the session ended with Zalthir grappling the ooze-form Pudding King, who can now automatically hit Zalthir without rolling [ch 046 / 046.09].
- Gyrgum rolled a 21 for initiative but had a connection problem and never got into the order; he will act before the Pudding King next time [ch 046 / 046.09].
- The fight in the throne room resumed with Zalthir still grappling the Pudding King, who was revealed to be a sentient, telepathic ooze that had become one of Juiblex's minions [ch 047 / 047.01].
- The Pudding King telepathically screamed his intention to reduce all of gnome kind to ooze, and Gyrgum, Daz, and Thorin asked him to lower his mental volume [ch 047 / 047.01; ch 047 / moments].
- The Pudding King retorted that calm, rational speech was for those who made deals with devils, not demons [ch 047 / 047.01; ch 047 / moments].
- Zalthir proposed shoving the Pudding King into a Bag of Holding to deprive him of an action, but the DM ruled a sentient ooze could ooze back out through the opening, so the bag could only temporarily neutralize him [ch 047 / 047.02].
- Zalthir instead tried to push the Pudding King with his unarmed strike, and a rules check distinguished a shove (knock prone or push away) from a push (only move away) [ch 047 / 047.02].
- Daz cast Magic Missile at the Pudding King, dealing six damage and dropping him from 85 to 79 hit points [ch 047 / 047.03].
- Prince Livid retaliated with a psychic blast at Zalthir for fourteen damage, reduced to seven by a successful DC 10 intelligence saving throw [ch 047 / 047.03].
- Princess Ebonmire hurled an acidic blob at Zalthir that missed [ch 047 / 047.03].
- Gyrgum cast Guiding Bolt at the Pudding King, but it missed [ch 047 / 047.03].
- The Pudding King unleashed a slime wave at Zalthir for eleven acid damage, halved to five by Stone Skin, and used a lair action to drop green slime on Gyrgum from across the room, which Gyrgum avoided on a DC 10 dexterity save [ch 047 / 047.04].
- Zalthir, agreeing with Thorin's request to "bring the fight to us," used Step of the Wind to dash while maintaining his grapple and dragged the Pudding King out of his lair [ch 047 / 047.05].
- Zalthir dragged the Pudding King through Glabbagool, taking 3d6 acid damage rolled as 5 and halved to 2 by Stone Skin; Thorin joked that Glabbagool must be thinking "this tickles" [ch 047 / 047.05; ch 047 / moments].
- Zalthir attempted a stunning strike on the Pudding King but missed [ch 047 / 047.05].
- Gyrgum cast the deciding vote to go "straight through him," and Glabbagool's reaction was "that hurt" [ch 047 / 047.05].
- When Thorin struck what he thought was the Pudding King with darts, a familiar voice from the ooze body cried "why are you hurting me?", and the party discovered that Glabbagool and the Pudding King had swapped bodies [ch 047 / 047.06; ch 047 / moments].
- Thorin tested the swapped ooze by asking where they had first met, and the answer "we met in a cake," delivered in a voice that did not quite sound like Glabbagool's, convinced the party they had swapped [ch 047 / 047.06; ch 047 / moments].
- Thorin attacked the real Pudding King, now in Glabbagool's body, with Dawnbringer, dealing twelve points of radiant damage on a 14 to hit [ch 047 / 047.06].
- Daz cast a third-level Magic Missile at the Pudding King for twenty points of damage, and Gyrgum cast Inflict Wounds for twenty-three points of damage on a failed DC 15 Constitution save [ch 047 / 047.07].
- Prince Livid and Princess Ebonmire charged toward the party [ch 047 / 047.07].
- The Pudding King unleashed another slime wave (DC 15 Dexterity save) affecting Thorin, Daz, and Gyrgum for 17 damage, halved to 8 by Stone Skin for those who failed, and those who failed were pushed back and knocked prone [ch 047 / 047.07].
- Green slime dropped on the prone pair; Gyrgum made his DC 10 dexterity save, but Daz rolled a 7 and was covered in green slime, needing an action to clear it or take five damage next turn [ch 047 / 047.07].
- The Pudding King tried to move under the slime, but Thorin's opportunity attack from his movement reduced his speed to zero and dealt seven damage [ch 047 / 047.07].
- Zalthir released the real Glabbagool, who sprouted two googly eyes on his new gray ooze body and oozed away from the fight [ch 047 / 047.08].
- Zalthir used Shadow Step to teleport near the Pudding King and attacked with advantage, re-establishing the grapple, and continued with punches and Eldritch Claw force damage (his punches dealt 11, 11, and 10, plus 17 across the three hits from Eldritch Claw) [ch 047 / 047.08].
- The Pudding King dissolved, screaming telepathically 'Behold the true feast, the soul's final blend!', and the DM praised the party's grappling tactic [ch 047 / 047.08; ch 047 / moments].
- With the Pudding King dead, the green slime would now only fall on those directly beneath it [ch 047 / 047.09].
- Daz cast Fireball, dealing twenty-nine points of damage to both Prince Livid and Princess Ebonmire, though Prince Livid's fire resistance reduced his damage to fourteen [ch 047 / 047.09].
- Prince Livid used a psychic attack on Daz for five points of damage (DC 10 Intelligence save); Princess Ebonmire charged at Thorin using her action to dash [ch 047 / 047.09].
- Gyrgum stood up from prone and cast Inflict Wounds on Princess Ebonmire for eighteen points of damage on a failed DC 15 Constitution save [ch 047 / 047.09].
- Zalthir used Shadow Step to teleport and attack Princess Ebonmire, grappling her and dealing damage with advantage, relying on Dawnbringer's 20-foot-radius light for a shadow [ch 047 / 047.09].
- Thorin killed Princess Ebonmire with one attack and killed Prince Livid with his second attack, made at disadvantage [ch 047 / 047.09].
- With the last enemy dead, the party felt Juiblex's demonic power receding from the stones and air, but noticed the fungi in the cavern becoming more twisted and malevolent, suggesting Zuggtmoy was moving to fill the power vacuum [ch 047 / 047.10].
- A demonic voice, possibly Juiblex himself, declared 'You haven't won. I will be reborn. I will consume the banquet of the queen of the fungi, and I will grow.' [ch 047 / 047.10].
- The party found two spellbooks containing many spells inside Princess Ebonmire's stomach, and the DM told Daz "your spell book now just increased in size" [ch 047 / 047.10; ch 047 / npcs].
- The party leveled up [ch 047 / 047.10].
- Glabbagool expressed excitement about his new ooze form, suggesting he could travel in a jar or the Bag of Holding as a garbage disposal, and said he could control his viscosity so no lid would be needed [ch 047 / 047.10; ch 047 / npcs].
- Gyrgum offered his Revivify spell, but Glabbagool, not being dead, declined [ch 047 / 047.10].
- The party discussed beginning Glabbagool's training as a monk, and he demonstrated being totally motionless by sinking his eyes into his body, which the DM called "Very zen" [ch 047 / 047.10].
- The Ember Vanguard reached seventh level in the middle of a war zone without a long rest, the reward for defeating the Pudding King; Gyrgum gained Divine Strike and Daz began learning new spells up to fourth level [ch 048 / 048.01].
