- The party found an Earth Elemental cracking under strain while holding the throne room's passage open; Thorin failed to set the cracks with raw strength (rolled a 2), and Gyrgum cast Mass Healing Word (2d4+4, rolled 8) to mend it [ch 048 / 048.01].
- The healed Earth Elemental stretched "like Atlas finally setting down the world," dropped a small glowing geode as a token of favor, and dissolved back into the earth, leaving the hole stable [ch 048 / 048.01].
- The party sensed that Entémoch, Prince of Good Elementals, looked on them with favor, and noticed fungi throughout the chamber growing faster than they should have, a sign of Zuggtmoy's lingering influence [ch 048 / 048.01].
- Thorin's player asked what an ooze like Glabbagool could do; Glabbagool is now a gray ooze who can move stealthily and fit in the bag of holding at any size [ch 048 / 048.01].
- In the Hall of Melting Mirrors, Chipgrin and his wererats were chanting to suppress lingering psychic static; Daz (Arcana 14), Zalthir (Insight, "not great"), and Gyrgum (9) all failed to find the harmonic center [ch 048 / 048.02].
- Thorin spent a luck point to re-roll his Arcana check, paused, looked around, and simply pointed out the exact psychic center; Chipgrin said it must be "the old dwarf in you," then hastily clarified to Daz he meant no offense [ch 048 / 048.02].
- At the support beams, the party found Gnomish miners arguing over zurkhwood versus steel for over-engineered bridges; when Gyrgum asked about using magic, the miners replied "what's the fun in that?" and lectured that patch-magic produces contraptions that "can't be reproduced" [ch 048 / 048.03].
- Gyrgum treated burned miners with Lesser Restoration; Thorin winched a critical support beam into place, his 11 pushed to 19 with Tactical Mind [ch 048 / 048.03].
- Diggermattock personally threaded a needle and sewed a Diggermattock Miners patch onto Thorin's gear, declaring him an honorary member of the crew; Gyrgum then finished it with Mending to everyone's approval, and Zalthir noted he already had one on his hoodie [ch 048 / 048.03].
- Chipgrin shared a wererat tip about a shortcut out of the tunnels bypassing a dangerous faerzress pass; the DM ruled it will work once, whenever the party invokes it [ch 048 / 048.03].
- Jimjar appeared and commented on the party's knack for fixing things before they departed [ch 048 / 048.03].
- The party emerged into a farming area where a panicked gnome farmer shouted "Oh, you're alive. That's wonderful news," reported the oozes were still attacking, and said his farmers had been handed weapons [ch 048 / 048.04].
- At the Traders' Grotto the party found a large Gnomish army before a breached wall with a writhing "Ooza Palooza" of oozes, jellies, and black puddings no longer bound by the Pudding King's will; a ghost joked "back in my day at IBM, we solved this problem using buffer caches" [ch 048 / 048.04].
- Chief Diggermattock and Chipgrin argued over dividing the newly opened territory while it was still full of ooze; Daz pointed out the "legalese of like who's gonna own ooze town" wasn't in their interest [ch 048 / 048.04].
- Thorin attempted to persuade the leaders to focus on the immediate threat, spending his last luck point and a d8 boost but reaching only a 6, and the argument rolled on [ch 048 / 048.04].
- Jimjar approached Gyrgum with a bet of ten platinum pieces that Glabbagool had more sense than the Pudding King, asking Daz whether he'd brought a sponge; Daz said it was in the bag of holding [ch 048 / 048.05].
- Jimjar tossed a small bone die onto Glabbagool; a strange blink rippled through the air, and Glabbagool briefly became a bridge between every ooze in the cavern, telling them the world was fine and there was no need to kill anyone [ch 048 / 048.05].
- The oozes went mindless and peaceful, slithering away into cracks and stonework until the threat dissolved; Gyrgum paid the ten platinum pieces from party funds, and the party judged it the best investment they had ever made [ch 048 / 048.05].
- Jimjar explained he won the bone die off a blind Duergar in Gracklstugh in "a back-alley game of Three-Dragon Ante," and that the Duergar claimed it was carved from the knucklebone of a saint who never told a lie, a die with a memory for the truth [ch 048 / 048.05].
- The die popped out of Glabbagool a moment later, spent and inert; Glabbagool complained it "doesn't seem to be responding to the acid." Jimjar offered it to the party as a keepsake, and Gyrgum tucked it into his pouch [ch 048 / 048.05].
- Zalthir asked about Jimjar's "world math" philosophy; Jimjar explained it's all probability and that a saint's die counteracts demons brought by an "unbelievably unrandom event," confirming he has no other such tricks [ch 048 / 048.05].
- Glabbagool confirmed he no longer has any link to the oozes: "It's like I had, we were all part of one big mind and then we weren't"; the party observed the oozes dispersing naturally, judging the area clear within days to a week [ch 048 / 048.05].
- The party was ushered into a council meeting where the factions of Blingdenstone were at each other's throats; Dorbo stood on his chair warning "miracles don't pay the guards," reporting no elementals left to dig, ghosts claiming the North Tunnels as a graveyard, and rats marking territory in the pipes [ch 048 / 048.06].
- Gyrgum delivered a passionate appeal for unity, ending with "If all of us come together, we can defeat the demons for good," followed by stunned silence [ch 048 / 048.06].
- Daz rolled a 19 on Insight and spotted the Diggermattocks didn't want to pay the working class a hero's wage; the party brokered a wage agreement between the miners guild and the working class, with details deferred to be discussed "underground" [ch 048 / 048.06].
- Insight rolls of 17, 25, 13 and 15 surfaced Diggermattock's hatred of paying for military protection; the party proposed the Goldwhisker wererats take control of the upper boroughs as a self-funded defense force, which Senni supported [ch 048 / 048.06].
- The party brokered a compromise on sustainable mining practices satisfying both the Miners Guild and the ghosts who considered the sanctum sacred ground [ch 048 / 048.06].
- Dorbo, growing nervous the gnomes might prefer Daz as a leader, announced the party would be sent to the Overbright the next morning to proclaim Blingdenstone "open for business," stressing "not for tourism," with Gyrgum adding "None of those ugly shireites" [ch 048 / 048.06].
- After a long rest, an honor guard led the party to the last set of tunnels; working-class gnomes lined the tunnels chanting "Gnome labor, not summoned labor," and the Burrow Warden ghosts dipped their spectral banners in salute as Jadger watched [ch 048 / 048.07].
- Chief Chipgrin pressed a braided rat's foot into Daz's hand, calling it the Voucher of the Varmint, a token recognized by wererats and outcasts from Blingdenstone all the way to Menzoberranzan [ch 048 / 048.07].
- The party overheard gnomes recounting wild tales of the "Ember Vanguard" as an ultra-powerful evil drow strike force: Daz a 20th-level magic user casting meteor swarm, Gyrgum using Earthquake to shatter mountains, Thorin smashing rocks with his biceps, Zalthir a Red Dragonborn, and the Vanguard slaying an ancient red dragon rather than Themberchaud "the fat and tired and overweight" [ch 048 / 048.07].
- The party never formally introduced themselves; Daz reasoned letting the legends spread might make it easier to negotiate out of fights later [ch 048 / 048.07].
- Jimjar pointed toward a pale white puffball mushroom growing at impossible speed from a crack in the wall with a cloyingly sweet scent, remarking "Juiblex was a loud, messy neighbor, but this sister, she prefers to move in while you're sleeping," then turned and walked back toward Blingdenstone; Gyrgum fed the mushroom to Glabbagool ("That was good calories") [ch 048 / 048.07].
- Thorin used Know Your Enemy to size up Jimjar and discovered a profile matching no ordinary gnome: immune to poison and psychic damage, could not be charmed, frightened, or poisoned, and shrugged off radiant energy and mundane weapons alike [ch 048 / 048.07].
- Zalthir noted the profile was "suspiciously similar to the stat block for angels"; the DM reminded the table that deities are not supposed to walk Faerûn since the Sundering [ch 048 / 048.07].
- Jimjar noticed the scrutiny, offered a knowing wink, and vanished entirely from existence, leaving only the faint metallic clink of platinum coins echoing in the tunnel and the distinct sense that this was not the last they would see of him [ch 048 / 048.07].
- The session concluded with the party preparing to finally exit the Underdark and see the sun; the DM suggested sages on the surface might shed light on who Jimjar is [ch 048 / 048.07].
- The party reviewed their map in Blingdenstone and prepared to depart for the surface, noting the escape route marked with a white hole leading up rather than down [ch 049 / 049.01].
- Zalthir brooded over the lack of a meaningful boon from Jimjar after weeks spent with the deity, reflecting that all he received was "a damn used token" [ch 049 / 049.01; ch 049 / moment].
- Gyrgum sought guidance from Bahamut on a Religion roll of 17, and the Platinum Dragon revealed that a gnomish god had gone into the Abyss and ended up in the prime material plane, reminding Gyrgum not to forget "who's buttering your bread" [ch 049 / 049.01].
- The DM noted that under updated rules clerics can change gods, so deities compete for followers, and that Bahamut—believing in the inevitable triumph of dragonkind—sees no need to rush [ch 049 / 049.01].
- Daz expressed concern that Lolth might be stirring once more in Menzoberranzan, recalling that she once vanished and left her clerics powerless [ch 049 / 049.01].
- The party began their first day of travel through the tunnels, which was relatively painless, though they noticed faint fungal spores, disturbed stones, and labored breathing behind them [ch 049 / 049.01; ch 049 / 049.02].
- Daz cast Find Familiar to summon a spider and left it behind to observe their pursuer, while Gyrgum cast Bless on the three party members at the front [ch 049 / 049.02; ch 049 / spells].
- Through the familiar's eyes, Daz rolled History 19 and identified the drow's symbol as that of House T'sarran, a minor house loyal to Ilvara, and an Investigation check with advantage confirmed the scout was part of Ilvara's own party [ch 049 / 049.02].
- The party observed that the drow was heavily infected with fungal growth—marbled skin, mushrooms rooting in his patchy white hair, eyes a mix of drow red and fungal yellow—and was doing a spectacularly poor job of concealing himself [ch 049 / 049.02].
- The party debated whether to capture, parley with, or ambush the scout; Gyrgum suggested playing on drow hierarchy ("we're your supervisors") [ch 049 / 049.02].
- The party discussed the fungal patterns they'd seen before—the spore-zombies of the Neverlight Grove and the laughing spore victim in Blingdenstone—and Zalthir clarified that the foe they defeated was the Pudding King, on the slime demon's side, not the fungal queen [ch 049 / 049.02].
- The party doubled back and Daz cast Maximilian's Earthen Grasp, restraining the scout after he failed a DC 15 Strength save, choosing to restrain rather than crush [ch 049 / 049.03; ch 049 / spells].
- The drow surrendered immediately, introduced himself as Valen of House T'sarran, and revealed that Ilvara had been hunting the party across the Underdark with a force of twenty, reduced to eight through disasters [ch 049 / 049.03].
- Valen described the fiasco with the kuo-toa and Demogorgon, and the Duergar of Gracklstugh ambushing Ilvara's party after mistaking them for the Ember Vanguard—which is in fact the party itself, recast by the Derro High Council as a powerful drow strike force led by a 19th-level wizard who "casts wishes as if they were going out of style" [ch 049 / 049.03].
- Valen revealed that Ilvara had descended into madness, communing with a vast, pulsing fungal mass she believed to be the goddess Lolth, and that several scouts sent to the Neverlight Grove never returned [ch 049 / 049.03].
- Valen reported that Ilvara now spoke of an entity she called "the bride," who demanded the party be hunted down and killed; the party recognized this as Zuggtmoy but kept that knowledge to themselves [ch 049 / 049.03; ch 049 / moment].
- Valen described a schism in Ilvara's camp: the junior priestess Asha Vandree accused Ilvara of heresy, and the scarred warrior Jorlan Duskryn, a discarded former lover, seethed with resentment and might sabotage anything out of spite [ch 049 / 049.03].
- Gyrgum's Medicine check of 22 revealed that Valen had only days before becoming a docile spore servant, and that a lesser restoration could buy him another week [ch 049 / 049.03].
- Thorin used the restorative power of Dawnbringer to cast lesser restoration on Valen, halting the fungal growth and buying him time, quipping "it's clear that you're a fun guy" [ch 049 / 049.03; ch 049 / moment; ch 049 / items].
- Grateful for the food the party shared, Valen agreed to guide them directly to the Fungal Altar in exchange for protection [ch 049 / 049.03].
- Valen told the party that Ilvara could track them through the pervasive fungi of the Underdark: "wherever there's fungi, Ilvara seems to have eyes and ears," and that she would know if anything happened to him [ch 049 / 049.03].
- The party decided to launch a preemptive strike on the Fungal Altar rather than race blindly for the surface and be hunted from behind, with Gyrgum arguing they could "choose the place" [ch 049 / 049.04].
- Daz proposed psychological warfare with his noise-making spells and traps, Gyrgum offered Thaumaturgy for distractions, and the party discussed exploiting the divisions between Ilvara, Asha Vandree, and Jorlan Duskryn [ch 049 / 049.04].
- Zalthir asked whether Valen was contagious; after lesser restoration his growths were less dangerous, and the party confirmed infection could be managed with Dawnbringer's restoration and Gyrgum's lesser restoration [ch 049 / 049.04].
- Valen led the party to a treacherous cavern where Ilvara had made camp, featuring elevated stone platforms, bridges, poisonous vents, corrupted pools of fungal liquid, and a massive roughly thirty-foot-wide heart fungus creature at the center [ch 049 / 049.04; ch 049 / 049.05; ch 049 / locations].
- The party realized they could reach only one of the two dissidents, Asha or Jorlan, without tipping someone off [ch 049 / 049.05].
- On Valen's advice, Daz sent a spider familiar to observe Asha Vandree on her platform, finding her obsessively scraping fungal growth from her robes and muttering about the theological absurdity of Ilvara's claims: "Lolth is not a bride" [ch 049 / 049.05; ch 049 / moment].
- An Investigation check of 22 revealed that Asha had been maintaining a private stash of sealed, uncontaminated food that kept her free of the infection [ch 049 / 049.05; ch 049 / items].
- The party checked the rules and confirmed that a familiar can only deliver touch spells, so Daz couldn't speak through it with Minor Illusion [ch 049 / 049.05; ch 049 / spells].
- Daz rolled Insight 12 and sensed that Asha, on the far edge of sanity, might be persuaded that the spider was an avatar of Lolth [ch 049 / 049.05].
- Daz cast Dancing Lights, shaping them into a medium, drow-like form of Lolth with a fuzzy lower half, letting the light fall like a column into the spider and blink out [ch 049 / 049.05; ch 049 / spells; ch 049 / moment].
- The session ended as Asha Vandree seemed convinced that Lolth herself had sent her a sign, and the DM awarded Daz an Inspiration point for the idea [ch 049 / 049.05; ch 049 / moment].
- Daz orchestrated a deception in the fungal cavern, commanding his spider familiar to return while casting Dancing Lights to make it appear a radiant form of Lolth shrank into the spider and was absorbed into his body, dismissing the familiar at the same instant so it seemed to vanish into him [ch 050 / 050.01].
- Glabbagool attempted to disguise himself as an inanimate rock but kept starting to talk and his blinking eyes darted left and right, completely undermining the illusion; he had to be reminded that "Invisible Rock doesn't talk" [ch 050 / 050.01; ch 050 / moment].
- Asha Vandree watched the display with wide, crazed eyes and searched for signs of trickery; Daz adopted a possessed persona and addressed her as "Dear servant," but his Deception rolls failed, including a 13, a 5, and a rerolled 5 despite spending inspiration [ch 050 / 050.01; ch 050 / 050.02].
- Daz's Insight roll (20) revealed that his divinity claim was failing but Asha believed he believed he was communing with Lolth and might see him as a useful ally [ch 050 / 050.01; ch 050 / 050.02].
- Daz pivoted from claiming to be Lolth to presenting himself as a powerful, perhaps spore-touched mage who had communed with Lolth and led a band of capable allies; the final Deception succeeded, and Asha accepted them as potential allies [ch 050 / 050.01; ch 050 / 050.02].
- Thorin suggested passing off Glabbagool as Daz's familiar, and Asha was impressed by the notion of an ooze familiar [ch 050 / 050.01; ch 050 / 050.03].
- Asha revealed that Ilvara, once a high priestess of Lolth, had been seduced by the heretical "bride" cult of Zuggtmoy after visiting the Neverlight Grove, now carried a corrupted mushroom artifact as a conduit for Zuggtmoy's influence, and had turned most of her followers into mindless fungal servants [ch 050 / 050.02].
- Asha explained she had avoided infection by staying clean of fungal dust, never coming within 20 feet of Ilvara, and eating only her own food, which was why she needed someone else to do the killing [ch 050 / 050.02].
- Asha offered first "the gratitude of Lolth," which Daz waved off because he "already" felt it, then offered Ilvara's treasures and magical items, even though they technically belonged to Asha's house [ch 050 / 050.02].
- The party agreed that Ilvara had to die, with Daz noting they "can always stab Asha in the back later" and that worst case they could get Greater Restoration before falling ill [ch 050 / 050.02].
- Gyrgum inscribed a Glyph of Warding (explosive runes, cast at 3rd level) on one of the bridges leading into the cavern, set to detonate against anyone approaching from Jorlan's side, since the party had picked Asha over Jorlan as its ally [ch 050 / 050.03].
- Asha told the party the Heart Fungus's spores caused only poisoning, pain and breathing difficulties, not the "bride" infection, adding after a pause "I believe" [ch 050 / 050.03].
- Asha agreed to rush in and defend the party from any spore servants Ilvara might summon, and retreated to wait for the signal [ch 050 / 050.03].
- The party planned to use Dust of Suleiman on the heart fungus while Zalthir attempted to grapple Ilvara into the area of effect [ch 050 / 050.03].
- Thorin borrowed a light crossbow from Gyrgum to compensate for his lack of ranged options, declining poisoned bolts for now [ch 050 / 050.03].
- Zalthir used Shadow Step to reach Ilvara and unleashed a furious flurry of blows for 31 damage, grappling her, then attempted to haul her over the Heart Fungus, but she used a legendary action to dissolve into a fungal patch and reappear elsewhere, screaming in fury [ch 050 / 050.04].
- The Heart Fungus released a choking cloud of poisonous spores (DC 15 Constitution), poisoning Zalthir, who flew 10 feet upward out of the haze but stayed poisoned until his next turn [ch 050 / 050.04].
- Thorin taunted Jorlan into charging forward into Gyrgum's waiting trap; the explosion tore through Jorlan, several sporewalkers, and the Heart Fungus itself, destroying one sporewalker outright and leaving another at 3 hit points [ch 050 / 050.04].
- Thorin waded into melee with Jorlan and struck him with the blazing sun-blade Dawnbringer on a 20 for 14 damage, sapping him so his next attack would have disadvantage; a sporewalker climbed up and struck back at Thorin for 5 damage [ch 050 / 050.04].
- Gyrgum unleashed Dust of Suleiman centered on the Heart Fungus, dealing significant force damage to it (Gyrgum read 24, GM recorded 20, no save) [ch 050 / 050.04].
- Daz hurled a carefully shaped 4th-level fireball that engulfed both Ilvara and her Zuggtmoy mushroom; Ilvara saved with a 19 and took 12 damage, and the mushroom took 12 damage [ch 050 / 050.04].
- The session ended mid-combat [ch 050 / 050.04].
- Zalthir launched himself through the air toward Ilvara, the corrupted drow priestess above the massive heart fungus, grappling her with eldritch tendrils from fifteen feet away and dealing sixteen damage [ch 051 / 051.01].
- Zalthir flew backward while holding Ilvara, dragging her forty feet into the air above the party's thirty-foot-radius protective sphere, then delivered Flurry of Blows with two critical strikes while holding her aloft [ch 051 / 051.01].
- Zalthir released his grip, dropping Ilvara forty feet to the stone floor for another seventeen damage; she retaliated with a legendary action, frightening Zalthir with a Wisdom save he failed, then commanded a drone to attack Daz and moved thirty feet toward cover [ch 051 / 051.01].
- The enormous heart fungus struck Zalthir with a critical hit, dealing bludgeoning and poison damage; Zalthir used Deflect Attack to prevent twelve bludgeoning damage, leaving him with fifteen total after the poison [ch 051 / 051.02].
- Ilvara conjured a sphere of biting insects at the cave entrance targeting Gyrgum, Daz, and Glabbagool; the plague killed her own low-hit-point minions outright and dealt twenty-six damage to Glabbagool, who survived [ch 051 / 051.02].
- Jorlan Duskryn broke away from the party, crying "Ilvara!" and charging toward her to hunt her himself; Thorin chose not to take an opportunity attack on Jorlan, letting the enemies fight each other [ch 051 / 051.02].
- Thorin descended into the difficult terrain and struck the heart fungus with Dawnbringer; the fungus was "doubly not resistant" to radiant damage, dying in a single round of strikes from the sentient sun-blade [ch 051 / 051.02].
- Gyrgum dropped concentration on his thirty-foot-radius sphere once the heart fungus died, then used a bonus-action dash granting three temporary hit points while moving toward Jorlan without trapping himself between the former lovers [ch 051 / 051.02; ch 051 / 051.03].
- Five drow warriors of House T'sarran emerged from the shadows, but their initial crossbow volley against Daz was retaken under obscured conditions and all three bolts missed after Zalthir reminded the table his magical darkness covered the position [ch 051 / 051.03].
- Kaelira Duskryn braved the insect swarm and pulled Daz northeast to safety, telling him "Come with me if you want to live, you idiot"; she succeeded on the save but took thirteen damage [ch 051 / 051.03].
- Asha Vandree denounced the newcomers as "Traitors to Lolth!" but Daz's persuasion narrowly succeeded when he assured her, "No, these are friends of Lolth, and they're here to help" [ch 051 / 051.03].
- A drow mage in the darkness fired a lightning bolt blindly, missing everyone; the House T'sarran elite warrior threatened that the matron mother would hear of the failed easy kill [ch 051 / 051.04].
- Daz cast a fourth-level fireball into the darkness, dealing thirty-seven fire damage, incinerating the three drow guards, badly injuring the drow mage, and wounding the elite warrior who saved [ch 051 / 051.04].
- Zalthir, unable to approach Ilvara due to fear, teleported in a different direction and finished the wounded mage with a single punch, then missed the surviving elite warrior [ch 051 / 051.04].
- Ilvara used a legendary action to create a fungal bloom in Jorlan's path, turning the ground into difficult terrain and preventing him from reaching her, then dropped concentration on the insect plague [ch 051 / 051.05].
- Ilvara muttered invocations to Zuggtmoy, declaring the bride — Zuggtmoy — would come to spread chaos, then cast Flamestrike on Daz and Nym Duskryn; both took thirteen damage despite Daz rolling a twenty on his save [ch 051 / 051.05].
- Thorin attacked the bridge beneath Ilvara at Zalthir's suggestion, destroying it and sending her and Jorlan leaping clear to the cavern floor below without serious injury [ch 051 / 051.06].
- Kaelira urged Daz to stay out of combat because "We only get paid if you live," but Daz refused, declaring the drow's best chance of being paid was helping the party win [ch 051 / 051.06].
- With Nym's permission after Nym called Jorlan "the dumber one," Kaelira struck Jorlan with a lightning bolt; he saved and took fourteen damage [ch 051 / 051.06].
- Gyrgum checked whether Daz, at eighteen of forty-four hit points, and Zalthir needed healing; both believed they could survive another round, so he cast a third-level Guiding Bolt at Ilvara [ch 051 / 051.06].
- Gyrgum's Guiding Bolt struck Ilvara, whose radiant vulnerability killed her outright; she exploded into a massive twenty-foot cloud of poisonous spores, catching Zalthir and Thorin in the toxic blast [ch 051 / 051.06].
- The surviving House T'sarran elite warrior promised "We will meet again. Enjoy your victory for the moment" before disengaging and vanishing into the tunnels [ch 051 / 051.06].
- Asha Vandree, amid the settling spores, denounced "the evil" and "the undead," then dropped to her knees and murmured prayers and beatitudes to Lolth [ch 051 / 051.06].
- The party was informed they had reached level eight, devoting the session to choosing feats, adjusting ability scores, reviewing spells, and discussing new capabilities [ch 052 / 052.01].
- The GM explained D&D rewards deep specialization, estimating 20 damage per round is substantial at eighth level, 40 at fifteenth, and 70 expected at twentieth; he also warned that a dragon standing sixty feet away could devastate a party with only one ranged attacker [ch 052 / 052.01; ch 052 / 052.03].
- Thorin evaluated Shield Master, Speedy, and raising Strength to twenty; after feeding his sheet to Gemini, which first recommended Sharpshooter then reversed itself, he settled on Sharpshooter with the GM permitting the ability increase to apply to Strength instead of Dexterity [ch 052 / 052.02; ch 052 / 052.03].
- Thorin retained Blind Fighting and selected the javelin's Slow mastery; he calculated his javelin attacks would have a 120-foot range, +7 attack bonus, and deal 1d6+4 damage [ch 052 / 052.02; ch 052 / 052.03].
- Daz considered War Caster, Lucky, Telekinetic, and Fey Touched; he chose Fey Touched, which freed prepared-spell capacity by removing spells he had already learned and expanding his options from the recovered spellbook [ch 052 / 052.02; ch 052 / 052.03].
- Zalthir abandoned his planned +2 Dexterity improvement after discovering Street Justice, which grants allies advantage against creatures he has grappled and supports binding targets with rope, chains, or manacles [ch 052 / 052.02; ch 052 / 052.03].
- The party worked through the Street Justice action economy: Zalthir can use a bonus-action martial attack to grapple, then spend his action binding the target with rope, consuming nearly all his turn [ch 052 / 052.03].
- The party devised the "Ember Grapple" synergy: Zalthir pins an enemy with a grapple and Daz casts Cloud of Daggers over the captive, who cannot voluntarily leave the damaging area; the GM confirmed the damage can occur only once per turn [ch 052 / 052.03].
- The GM clarified grappling does not pull the target into the grappler's five-foot space, so Daz can center Cloud of Daggers on Zalthir's target without automatically striking Zalthir [ch 052 / 052.03].
- The GM rejected any infinite-damage interpretation of spells like Spirit Guardians, stating flatly, "I don't care what the rules say, there's not an infinite damage hack" [ch 052 / 052.03].
- After the battle settled, Nym and Kaelira Duskryn, the two Drow mercenaries who had fought alongside the party, moved quietly toward the exit until Daz stopped them to thank them and ask who had hired them [ch 053 / 053.01].
- The mercenaries explained they were hired in Menzoberranzan by an unknown benefactor, knowing nothing about their employer beyond the money's source; their contract required them to keep other Drow factions from killing Daz, specifically protecting him from Ilvara of House Mizzrym and agents of House T'sarran, and the protection extended only to Daz [ch 053 / 053.01].
- Daz's knowledge of Drow politics let him recognize the patron had spent heavily to counter a rival noble house's operations; when Thorin asked what it would take to retain the mercenaries, they struggled not to laugh and said the party could not afford them [ch 053 / 053.01].
- The party pressed the mercenaries about Asha Vandree still living and a demonic power trying to kill them; Nym and Kaelira dismissed Asha as a low-level cult initiate and said evil demon gods were outside their contract [ch 053 / 053.01].
- Daz persuaded the mercenaries that letting him die within minutes of their departure might harm their fee collection, and they grudgingly agreed to escort him the remaining few miles to the exit [ch 053 / 053.01].
- Asha Vandree, convinced she was divinely blessed by Lolth after surviving her crew's ruin, marched forward and declared, "Excellent! Now that I am in charge!," treating non-Drow and Drow males with contempt [ch 053 / 053.02].
- Gyrgum opened the confrontation with a guiding bolt, which missed as Asha cried "I am blessed by Lolth!"; Thorin then advanced, grappled her, dealt five points of damage, and clamped a hand over her mouth [ch 053 / 053.02].
- Nym returned to criticize the grappled approach, saying one takes a blade and cuts off a cleric of Lolth's head; Zalthir judged Asha an evil cleric whose death would benefit the Underdark and killed her outright with a flurry of strikes and tentacle attacks [ch 053 / 053.02].
- Kaelira applauded and told Nym, "See, he wasn't completely defenseless," while the mercenaries remained baffled by Thorin's reluctance to kill an obvious enemy [ch 053 / 053.02].
- The party looted the Fungal Altar, recovering Velkynvelve's payroll float of 480 gold pieces and 1,200 silver pieces, a jailer's ring of keys, and a chest of confiscated prisoner belongings [ch 053 / 053.03].
- From the confiscated chest, Thorin recovered his sentimental tooth, which Ilvara had carried throughout the pursuit; the chest also held a half-water-damaged Dwarvish journal of a presumed dead prisoner and a stoppered clay flask of Overbright brandy [ch 053 / 053.03].
- From Asha's body the party recovered a holy symbol of Lolth, spider-fretted scale mail, obsidian-and-bone prayer beads, coins, and a sealed wax pouch whose tampers-evident seal Daz recognized; inside was a post-corruption theological argument that Zuggtmoy's claims upon Lolth were heresy [ch 053 / 053.03].
- Daz claimed a Drow cloak of elvenkind from the spoils, a sunlight-fragile garment granting an advantage in stealth that required attunement [ch 053 / 053.03].
- Ilvara's prayer book contained Zuggtmoy-related marginalia in another hand tracing her corruption from ordinary devotion; two recent sealed orders from House Mizzrym showed she was still following instructions as Zuggtmoy overtook her, and later messages grew frantic when she stopped responding [ch 053 / 053.03].
- Daz and Gyrgum inferred that House Mizzrym's inability to locate Ilvara had become known, prompting House T'sarran to investigate and dispatch its own force [ch 053 / 053.03].
- A partially legible letter from Neverlight Grove, written by Ilvara's agent before the agent died, referenced "the bride" and a meeting that never occurred [ch 053 / 053.03].
- Ilvara's corrupted five-tailed scourge with living serpent heads could aerosolize poisonous spores around its wielder, but the party learned it could be purified; after theatrical objections from Dawnbringer, the cleansing was accomplished during the party's rest [ch 053 / 053.03; ch 053 / 053.05].
- On the Drow mage's body the party found a signet ring bearing House T'sarran's seal, which Daz recognized as proof of involvement and a tool for forging convincing orders in the house's name, plus an elaborate nonmagical obsidian arcane focus worth 150 gold pieces [ch 053 / 053.04].
- Partially burned orders contained a surviving instruction concerning "the mage Daz" and confirmed "the extraction" was to be alive and unharmed; damaged portions could not be restored through ordinary mending [ch 053 / 053.04].
- Gyrgum found at the heart fungus's center a fist-sized, pearl-like node radiating residual necromantic energy worth about 300 gold pieces, and the party recovered three intact spore sacks an alchemist might transform into something useful [ch 053 / 053.04].
- Zalthir turned his fire breath on the remnant of Zuggtmoy's corruption atop Ilvara's elevated stone platform; after a weak first attempt drew jokes, a second blast burned it away, revealing a leather-wrapped bundle [ch 053 / 053.04].
- The bundle contained two unused incense cones, a small silver bowl, and a folded cloth stitched with the sigils of Lolth and Zuggtmoy nested inside one another, physical evidence of the syncretism driving Asha and Ilvara mad [ch 053 / 053.04].
- When Nym and Kaelira examined the burned orders, they confirmed House T'sarran's team had been paid to abduct Daz and bring him out alive; Kaelira mocked Daz's speculation about hidden importance with "daddy issues" or "mommy issues" jokes, though even the mercenaries admitted the expense was unusual [ch 053 / 053.04; ch 053 / moment].
- The party established they had all traveled together when captured, raising the possibility Daz was the true target and the others suffered for befriending "the wrong Drow"; Daz proposed the party help investigate the factions interested in him, splitting any eventual proceeds [ch 053 / 053.05].
- Nym and Kaelira guided the party through the final miles, and the companions crawled into the blinding sunlight of the Overbright; Daz packed away his sunlight-fragile cloak before emerging [ch 053 / 053.05; ch 053 / 053.06].
- Glabbagool, the sentient grey ooze, peered out from a bag of holding with enormous googly eyes, having not known the Overbright existed; Dawnbringer protested being used as a ritual implement until reminded of the Vaultmaster Locking Scabbard [ch 053 / 053.05].
- The party agreed to remain together during several months of downtime, selecting Candlekeep, the fortress-library south of Waterdeep, to research House T'sarran, Jimjar's true nature, Daz's hidden history, Bahamut, and traumatized intelligent weapons [ch 053 / 053.06].
- Thorin acquired javelins during the downtime, intended to improve Dawnbringer's offensive potential, and proposed finding a "sword whisperer" therapist for the sentient weapon, which Dawnbringer rejected as unnecessary [ch 053 / 053.06].
- Gyrgum offered to assist Daz's research and planned to study Bahamut at Candlekeep, even though the fortress had no dedicated shrine [ch 053 / 053.06].
- The party discussed using their accumulated wealth to improve adventuring equipment and joked about becoming a private-equity firm that adventured on the side [ch 053 / 053.06].
- The party emerged from the Underdark onto the surface, and Glabbagool gazed up at the sky with visible emotion, moved by seeing the sun for the first time [ch 054 / 054.01].
- Zalthir reflected on having, in some sense, offered to teach Glabbagool the ways of a monk; the GM noted Glabbagool looks forward to doing the "run, run, slap, slap, slap" of a monk's combat style despite moving extremely slowly [ch 054 / 054.01].
- Thorin observed Glabbagool's advantages as a prospective monk — unpredictable, no fixed appendages, could theoretically land 12 hits in a round; Zalthir's ideas of becoming an ooze or equipping Glabbagool as a weapon were shut down by the GM [ch 054 / 054.01].
- Eldeth bade farewell, telling Thorin "Giant friend, we are like brothers. We have fought together. We have bled together. We have faced the abyssal creatures together," and asked Thorin to wait before following her [ch 054 / 054.01; ch 054 / moment].
- Eldeth explained she must go first to Mithral Hall to explain matters to the king and speak to the lords of Gauntlgrym and Mithral Hall about Underdark dangers; she swore "I will make it a statement of purpose that you shall be embraced by our clan" [ch 054 / 054.01].
- Thorin told Eldeth "I'm taking this seriously. I don't think you're just being socially nice here. I think you really do want me to," and she confirmed with a nod and a handshake [ch 054 / 054.01].
- Dawnbringer threw a hissy fit at being described as "a really cool sword who's kind of annoying sometimes"; Thorin acknowledged her, saying "I'm annoying also, Dawnbringer, look, you know, we got each other" [ch 054 / 054.01].
- Spiderbait declared excitement about exploring the surface, confident goblins were "totally integrated into the society"; the party corrected him, with Gyrgum noting "the state of goblin equality and inclusion is rough at the moment" [ch 054 / 054.01].
- Spiderbait asked to travel with the party to Candlekeep to find innocuous work like making food; Gyrgum endorsed the plan, though the GM noted Spiderbait is terrible at making food [ch 054 / 054.01].
- The party traveled west for several days; on the third day at dusk, they realized they were being followed [ch 054 / 054.02].
- Gyrgum rolled a 9 on Insight and concluded the presence was a deer — the GM confirmed "You conclude that what is following you is a deer" — and prepared to nap [ch 054 / 054.02; ch 054 / moment].
- Thorin (20), Daz (16), and Zalthir (16) realized someone had been following them for some time, within 20 to 40 feet, coming from the northern area [ch 054 / 054.02].
- Thorin ranted that his Tremor Sense requires stone and the party was on a dirt road through a forest; Dawnbringer commented Thorin has anger issues [ch 054 / 054.02].
- The party debated options; Daz proposed getting intelligence rather than burning everything down [ch 054 / 054.02].
- Daz used Misty Step to teleport into the branches of a large tree to scout; he rolled Stealth with advantage (a 1 and an 11, total 12), the spy rolled Perception 3, and the GM ruled she noticed where he landed [ch 054 / 054.03; ch 054 / spells].
- The spy rolled Stealth 18; Daz rolled Investigation 26 and spotted her; the GM cautioned players "Do not read the word assassin. That's just the profile of the character" [ch 054 / 054.03; ch 054 / moment].
- Daz signaled the party with a prearranged bird call impersonating a cardinal — which Zalthir noted are not native to these trees [ch 054 / 054.03].
- Gyrgum and Glabbagool, still at camp, heard the call; Glabbagool did a bad bird impression and was excited about the concept of a bird, and was confused when Gyrgum told him birds are feathered [ch 054 / 054.03].
- Zalthir teleported onto the spy, hitting AC 24 to grapple her and AC 19 for a combined 25 damage, then used Stunning Strike — the spy failed a DC 13 Constitution save and was stunned and grappled [ch 054 / 054.03; ch 054 / spells].
- Thorin rushed toward the spy but could not close the distance to attack, and yelled at her while she was stunned [ch 054 / 054.03].
- Daz climbed down and tied the spy up, telling her "It's very rude to follow people without just coming up and talking to them, so we'll talk to you now" [ch 054 / 054.03].
- Zalthir asked the spy "Who are you? Why are you following us? Are you a friend or foe?" as the stun wore off; she responded "And why should I tell you anything? You will kill me on the spot and leave nothing. What do I have to gain from talking to you?" [ch 054 / 054.04].
- The spy identified herself as a member of House T'sarran, refused to give her name, and offered her piwafwi as a trophy: "Take my piwafwi if you want a trophy. My matron will know one of hers fell out here and that is enough" [ch 054 / 054.04].
- Thorin attempted Intimidation twice — the second attempt failed and the spy laughed in his face [ch 054 / 054.04].
- Gyrgum read the spy as a low-ranking agent stuck with a miserable above-ground assignment, casually offered her a job, then silently handed her the prayer beads of Bahamut and told her to put them on — the religious implications causing visible panic [ch 054 / 054.04; ch 054 / moment].
- Gyrgum rolled Persuasion 19; the spy revealed "I was just assigned to track you, not to engage. My orders were to report your surface destination, your contact, who approaches you... My matron was waiting for you to surface, and she sent me up — she sent a couple of us up to the Overbright. I got lucky and I spotted you" [ch 054 / 054.04].
- Daz rolled Insight 27 and observed the spy was specifically trying not to make it obvious she was watching him, not the whole party [ch 054 / 054.04].
- The spy said "The Matron Mother was interested in you"; when Daz claimed not to have heard of her, she laughed — "You have not heard? Are you not drow?" — and the GM noted the Matron Mother of House T'sarran has ruled for 200 years [ch 054 / 054.04].
- Under further pressure, the spy revealed "My matron believes the one who paid for him will reveal themselves once he reaches the surface. We are looking for that person, not for you" [ch 054 / 054.04].
- Daz realized "So basically, you're treating me as bait. You're watching and observing so that when something happens to me, you can go report back" — the spy confirmed "Yes. Because we are looking for the one who paid to have you protected" [ch 054 / 054.04; ch 054 / moment].
- The spy proposed a mutual information-sharing arrangement; Thorin was skeptical: "The minute she has the information she needs, she's off, and we get jack shit" [ch 054 / 054.04].
- The party discussed temporarily killing Daz to draw out his protector; Gyrgum noted he has a Revivify spell, making it "just a death light" — the plan was not pursued but remained on what Zalthir called "the maybe list" [ch 054 / 054.04; ch 054 / spells].
- The spy suggested being escorted to Candlekeep and turned over to the guards, implying she knew how to escape — which the party took as a bad argument [ch 054 / 054.04].
- Thorin and Gyrgum conspired loudly in front of the spy, asking whether the prayer beads retain full power even when shrunken; Gyrgum confirmed they may be more potent at reduced size [ch 054 / 054.04].
- The party forced a shrinking mushroom down the spy's throat, reducing her to a tiny, squeaky, furious size; Thorin nicknamed her "Polly Pocket" [ch 054 / 054.05; ch 054 / moment].
- The tiny spy was placed in the bag of holding — with Thorin proposing poking holes "like a hamster" — with Glabbagool assigned as her guard, fixing one eye permanently on her [ch 054 / 054.05].
- Thorin offered the mercy that "At least the bag of holding is shade, you know, so she's not gonna be exposed to full sun." The GM noted the indignity was getting to her, "starting to wonder whether there are other fates" she might prefer [ch 054 / 054.05].
- The party discussed which books to donate at Candlekeep: Princess Ebonmire's spellbook for Daz's magical lineage, Ilvara's prayer book for questions about Jimjar, the Lolth-related theological argument for drow questions, the Prisoner Journal for Daz's own history, and Plinki's Journal for demonic lore [ch 054 / 054.05].
- Glabbagool did not understand why they wouldn't simply donate every book; Thorin proposed giving all five books so Glabbagool gets one question of his own, and everyone agreed [ch 054 / 054.05].
- The party arrived at Candlekeep and learned from the gatekeeper that one book per group is the minimum, and donating more books earns more time with the Avowed [ch 054 / 054.06].
- The party donated all five unique texts: the Velkynvelve Prisoner Journal, Princess Ebonmire's spellbook, Ilvara's prayer book with Zuggtmoy marginalia, Asha Vandree's theological argument, and Plinki's Journal [ch 054 / 054.05; ch 054 / 054.06].
- The First Reader received the Velkynvelve Prisoner Journal with barely contained excitement, calling it "a masterpiece of interesting value" that would "go great with my other editions in drow prison journals"; Thorin suggested the party could be interviewed as primary sources [ch 054 / 054.06].
- The party presented their shrunken drow captive as a living exhibit; she declared "This is highly irregular, and House T'sarran will get its vengeance!" — Daz replied "Tiny, tiny little vengeance" [ch 054 / 054.06; ch 054 / moment].
- Glabbagool informed the spy that his googly eyes are affectations — "I can actually see different ways, this is how ooze work, but I discovered that if you have these cute eyes, people take you more friendly" — then demonstrated his authority by manifesting a large number of eyes at once, which was "very creepy very quickly" [ch 054 / 054.06; ch 054 / moment].
- The spy demanded to know about the prayer beads; Thorin told her "You don't get a question. You did not donate a book" [ch 054 / 054.06].
- The First Reader assigned scholars: Zalthir to learn about Jimjar, Thorin and Dawnbringer to Philemon (a specialist in the psychological care of sentient magical artifacts), and noted that avowed scholar Khell-Vire wished to study Gyrgum — Thorin was enthusiastic: "I'm all up for couples counseling with Dawnbringer" [ch 054 / 054.06].
- The frantic elderly half-elf scholar Vareth rushed in, furious he had not been told about the arrival of a cleric of Bahamut; his previously obscure research on orc Bahamut converts has "become a flood" since the Stroud-Protanther events [ch 054 / 054.06].
- Vareth grabbed Gyrgum's hand and dragged him toward his office, describing him as "a real living orc cleric of Bahamut" and a "living data point" [ch 054 / 054.06].
- Gyrgum mentioned "Stroud's Gambit" (a legendary chess game recorded by Vareth), triggering Vareth's near-collapse of excitement: "This is all novel news! I can't wait to take advantage of it! The field of Bahamut orc conversions will benefit tremendously from your interview" [ch 054 / 054.06; ch 054 / moment].
- The party reflected on Daz's habit of citing Milo Goodbarrel's multi-volume Account of the War of the Dragons; Daz mused privately about writing his own account to obscure certain truths so the real story remains larger than what is published [ch 054 / 054.06].
- The session ended with each party member pointed toward their relevant scholar and research findings promised for the next session [ch 054 / 054.06].
- The party emerges from the Blingdenstone surface exit into the Silver Marches on a mid-spring afternoon [ch 055 / 055.01; ch 055 / 055.02]
- Eldeth bids the party farewell as they begin their journey south across the surface [ch 055 / 055.01; ch 055 / 055.02]
- The party observes burned hamlets, a broken standing stone, and a defiled Tempus shrine along the road through the Silver Marches [ch 055 / 055.02]
- Gyrgum performs a basic reverence at the defiled Tempus shrine — genuflecting, muttering a blessing, and continuing on [ch 055 / 055.02]
- The party encounters a refugee family heading south — three generations, one ox, no grandfather [ch 055 / 055.02]
- The party learns at a wayhouse from a local about Uthgardt raids following the War of the Giants: adventurers stole religious artifacts, triggering a crusade against civilized areas of the Silver Marches [ch 055 / 055.02]
- The GM explains the Ordning hierarchy restored by the War of the Giants, from Storm Giants at top down to Hill Giants [ch 055 / 055.02]
- A local at the wayhouse notes unusual quiet in the land — fewer bears, no pixies singing in the sown lands — which Gyrgum, having recently been adjacent to the Fey, finds suspicious [ch 055 / 055.02]
- The party detours to Mirabar, the dwarven mining city visible miles away by forge smoke and red light [ch 055 / 055.03]
- The party observes refugees sleeping in shifts at the Smith's Gate, with the Smith's Quarter ringing with hammers all night, half the orders for militia armor [ch 055 / 055.03]
- Thorin feels like a fish out of water in Mirabar despite his dwarven heritage — overwhelmed and unfamiliar with the city [ch 055 / 055.03]
- A traveling Stroud School polemicist spots Gyrgum in the Mirabar market and attempts to recruit him to the "human agency hypothesis" [ch 055 / 055.03]
- Gyrgum replies he stands for "strength and luck"; he rolls a 6 on persuasion, and the preacher hands him a pamphlet titled *Stroud and the Human Agency Hypothesis: A Critique — Dragons and Gods Do Not Rule*, which Gyrgum pockets for later [ch 055 / 055.03]
- Gyrgum senses factionalism in Bahamutian religious culture surrounding Stroud and the events around Protanther [ch 055 / 055.03]
- The party visits an Order of the Gauntlet shrine in Mirabar featuring a sculpted bronze frontispiece reproduction of Milo Goodbarrel's Account, Volume 1, and a nearly full-height painting *Malfire, the Vindicator of Nature* by Milo Goodbarrel, tended by two half-orcs in shifts [ch 055 / 055.03]
- Thorin begins considering whether Mirabar's smiths could outfit him with something worthy of the road ahead [ch 055 / 055.03]
- The party arrives at Triboar, a caravan town one-third in ruins, with a cairn of silver fieldstones naming 243 of the Triboar dead before an inn with recently laid flagstones [ch 055 / 055.04]
- The party overhears trade-route rumors of the Ardragon, a Kenku hooded in black hovering a foot off the ground on the caravan trails north of Yartar [ch 055 / 055.04]
- A drover explains the Ardragon formerly flew routes for the Black Network and stole anything not nailed down; something happened out east, and now he stops banditry, takes nothing, mimics a hawk, and rides the next thermal [ch 055 / 055.04]
- Another voice notes:"We don't pay him. We leave a coin on a rock, and he never picks it up." [ch 055 / 055.04]
- At the Mountain's Mouth inn, Kestler tells the party about a temple brother who stopped writing five years ago; the temple fire still burns, and Kestler asks them to carry a written note; Gyrgum immediately agrees, calling it an omen [ch 055 / 055.04]
- Thorin buys a drink for Eldred, a local man at the inn speaking in two voices — his own and a younger one that answers questions he never asks [ch 055 / 055.04]
- A neighbor explains Eldred ran courier from Triboar to Sundabar in the war and came back "both of them"; the town feeds him and does not ask [ch 055 / 055.04]
- Gyrgum and Zalthir roll above 14 on insight and notice both voices are Eldred's, the second a younger version of himself; the madness has the same broken, fractured quality they witnessed throughout the Underdark [ch 055 / 055.04]
- Gyrgum realizes whatever was festering below is bleeding through into the surface world [ch 055 / 055.04]
- Glabbagool is gifted an entire wheel of cheese by the Triboar innkeeper, dissolves it entirely, declares "This is very good," and asks whether dairy is bad for people — his first encounter with cheese [ch 055 / 055.04]
- Throughout the stay, Glabbagool pestered Zalthir about shadow monk training and periodically attempted to demonstrate walking [ch 055 / 055.04]
- Gyrgum performs a ceremonial ten-minute mass healing word for injured refugees in Triboar, drawing attention from local Bahamutians, Stroudites, and Protanthians [ch 055 / 055.04; ch 055 / 055.05]
- Gyrgum's response — "Are we not all the playthings of fate?" — makes the Bahamutian faction erupt with enthusiasm, while a third of the audience leaves frustrated and another third cries "Deist!" [ch 055 / 055.04; ch 055 / 055.05]
- The Bahamutians declare "It was the great and glorious power of Bahamut that directed everything. Protanther and Stroud were merely agents!" [ch 055 / 055.05]
- Gyrgum deflects a warning that his position is "heretically close to the Protanther Doctrine," responding "But the differences between Bahamut and Protanther are Legion." [ch 055 / 055.04]
- The party enters Waterdeep, where Glabbagool is forced to remain hidden in a bag because the city does not tolerate oozes; Thorin considers taking a stand in solidarity [ch 055 / 055.05]
- Glabbagool peers out of the bag with two small eyeballs and is allowed out occasionally to eat cheese [ch 055 / 055.05]
- In Castle Ward, the party observes a bronze statue of the chess match between Stroud and Protanther, the board in inlaid marble, with a brass plaque reading *This is the position. The move is yours.* [ch 055 / 055.05; ch 055 / 055.06]
- Gyrgum muses "If only Protanther had moved his Rook, all could have gone another way," immediately drawing a crowd of passionate strangers eager to debate the question of the age [ch 055 / 055.05; ch 055 / 055.06]
- Thorin acquires mithral plate armor in Waterdeep, bringing his armor class to 22 [ch 055 / 055.05; ch 055 / items]
- Gyrgum expresses interest in a javelin of lightning and wants to visit a Bahamut shrine or temple [ch 055 / 055.05]
- Daz visits Rishaal's Pageturners to purchase Volume Three of Milo Goodbarrel's Account; he previously had Volumes 1 and 2 [ch 055 / 055.06]
- Above the desk hangs a framed original painting by Milo Goodbarrel depicting Stroud, Milo, Malfire, and Whistler — the Kenku hooded in black, hovering a foot off the ground, hand raised in mimicry [ch 055 / 055.06]
- Rishaal explains:"That painting saved the print run! Milo finished it two days before the signing. We had it framed. People come here for the painting and left with a book." [ch 055 / 055.06]
- Rishaal describes the "real-world fiction enthusiasts" as "exasperating, but they buy three copies of everything" [ch 055 / 055.06]
- Fans at the shop's back debate whether Whistler could have flown from Triboar in two days per the Weber Report's wind conditions, and split on his redemption arc — Whistlerites versus Miloites [ch 055 / 055.06]
- The party discovers a sign at the bookshop reading *Whistler, Kenku of the First Faction, fallen at Glazhael's lair, the year of the Alliance, redeemed in life and death. May his silence speak.* [ch 055 / 055.06]
- Zalthir seeks shadow monk training in Waterdeep and is directed toward Candlekeep and a master named Khell-Vire, described as "the best of the best" [ch 055 / 055.07]
- A Waterdeep monastery offers a letter of introduction for a price, citing a leaky roof [ch 055 / 055.07]
- Zalthir's persuasion check yields a 4 against the monastery's 18, settling the demand at 140 gold pieces [ch 055 / 055.07]
- Daz spends the better part of an hour on the eaves repairing the roof tear-by-tear with the Mending cantrip, within the spell's per-cast one-foot limit; the monks sheepishly provide the letter [ch 055 / 055.07]
- In the Field Ward, the party encounters a ragged street preacher chanting "The kraken under the keep! The kraken under the keep! The chant will break, and the kraken will rise!" [ch 055 / 055.07; ch 055 / 055.08]
- The preacher spots Daz as a drow and breaks his chant:"You came up! You came up, you will go again. You will go down again. Do not—" before cycling back into the chant [ch 055 / 055.07; ch 055 / 055.08]
- Thorin rolls 16 on insight and gets the distinct impression the preacher is right about something he cannot name [ch 055 / 055.07; ch 055 / 055.08]
- Thorin notices the preacher's broken, looping speech rhythm matches that of the dual-voiced man in Triboar [ch 055 / 055.07; ch 055 / 055.08]
- Gyrgum notes Candlekeep may be a place to seek answers about the kraken reference, since the word "keep" is suggestive [ch 055 / 055.07; ch 055 / 055.08]
- Over several days Glabbagool remains mostly unnoticed in his bag, but at dusk a city guard insists on inspecting it in a shadowed alley behind the inn [ch 055 / 055.08; ch 055 / 055.09]
- Thorin takes an immediate stand for ooze rights, arguing sentience deserves respect regardless of form [ch 055 / 055.08]
- Dawnbringer supports the argument:"One day they're going to ban oozes, the next day they'll ban sentient swords!" [ch 055 / 055.08; ch 055 / memorable]
- The guard, bewildered, asks whether Thorin needs to be taken to a cleric; Gyrgum notes he is a cleric, and Dawnbringer offers to perform lesser restoration [ch 055 / 055.08]
- While the guard is distracted, Zalthir uses Shadow Step to vanish with the bag, reappearing in another shadowed alley [ch 055 / 055.08; ch 055 / 055.09]
- The guard notices the bag is gone and tells Thorin he may want to walk away; Thorin finishes his coffee and walks away [ch 055 / 055.08; ch 055 / 055.09]
- The confrontation draws a crowd of mixed reactions, planting seeds of a new idea in the city [ch 055 / 055.08; ch 055 / 055.09]
- Thorin subsequently spends time in Waterdeep's poorer quarters channeling Dawnbringer's restorative power to heal orphans and the sick, with Zalthir alongside; only Thorin can direct the Lesser Restoration as the attuned wielder [ch 055 / 055.08; ch 055 / 055.09]
- The party continues south and arrives at Daggerford, with the road to Candlekeep ahead [ch 055 / 055.09]
- The GM indicates events in Daggerford will continue in the next session, including at least one overheard story pulled back before being revealed [ch 055 / 055.09]
- The party arrived in Daggerford during their journey south toward Candlekeep, where they found the town unusually quiet — the townsfolk moving carefully without explanation — and stopped for the night at the River Shining Tavern [ch 056 / 056.01].
- In Daggerford's village square, the party examined a bronze cast of a severed forearm and hand on a stone pillar, a monument to Travis Houlin, a paladin of Bahamut who died there three years prior fighting the Cult of the Dragon, which had been actively recruiting in the town [ch 056 / 056.01].
- The monument bore a Dwarven inscription reading "The move is yours" — mirroring the phrase beneath the Stroud statue in Waterdeep's Castle Ward — and a smaller Common inscription noting Milo Goodbarrel, an Order of the Gauntlet member whose work was published by Rishaal, witnessed and recorded it [ch 056 / 056.01].
- A local village priest arrived selling small pewter reproductions of Travis's hand cast to raise donations for the Order of the Gauntlet, declaring himself a "pure Bahamutian" who did not believe in the heresy of the Stroudites; he conceded the Dwarven Stroudite inscription could remain on the monument since the casting had been a gift, funded by the Metalworkers Guild — possibly with Stroud-school sponsorship [ch 056 / 056.01].
- Gyrgum donated a gold piece and received a pewter Hand of Travis medallion [ch 056 / 056.01].
- That evening at the River Shining Tavern, a weaver named Maerith of the Ford approached the party about her seven-year-old daughter Elin, who had been silent for six days, drawing the same shape on every flat surface, and speaking only in her sleep, saying "it is only the shape that holds" [ch 056 / 056.02].
- Gyrgum and Daz examined Elin but rolled poorly (a 10 and a 7) — both failed to reach a definitive diagnosis [ch 056 / 056.02].
- Dawnbringer, Thorin's sentient sun-sword, performed a lesser restoration on Elin with a pompous "Let me bring the light to you" — cast through Thorin as her attuned wielder, not from any of Thorin's own resources [ch 056 / 056.02].
- After the restoration Elin spoke briefly — "The wells are not wells anymore" — before falling silent again; the party concluded this was no ordinary disease but something twisting her mind from within, causing auditory hallucinations, with Gyrgum noting it was interior rather than exterior — "not something that can be slapped, or grappled" [ch 056 / 056.02].
- The party connected Elin's condition to the madness previously seen in the Underdark bleeding into the surface world, and Gyrgum's medicine check (23) confirmed a tenuous connection between Elin's condition and the growing pressure in Daz's own head [ch 056 / 056.02].
- The party traveled south to Beregost, the last meaningful stop before Candlekeep, and took shelter at the Burning Wizard Inn [ch 056 / 056.03].
- The gnome innkeeper Festrum recounted the inn's namesake — a wizard burned alive during a dragon cult uprising decades past — pointing to a permanent soot mark on the south wall and reciting his grandfather's words: "The wall remembers" [ch 056 / 056.03].
- A traveling bard performed, with her closing number a four-verse drinking song about a Kenku thief who learned to steal no more, the whole inn singing the chorus of "Black Hood"; Gyrgum noted the song's reference to stealing "what was needed" mapped well onto Whistler's behavior in taking only what was needed from Tiamat's horde [ch 056 / 056.03].
- After Gyrgum requested a song about Stroud — Thorin pressing a silver piece into the bard's hand on his behalf — she asked whether Gyrgum was Bahamutian or Stroudite, then sang a love song about the famous chess game, which Gyrgum observed wound "the usual claptrap about love and emotions and flowers" into a drinking song [ch 056 / 056.03].
- During the performance Daz noticed a young human scholar reciting a chant whose breathing and cadence were "almost right but wrong by a hair" — the same syncopated wrongness he had observed in Eldred, who went insane earlier in the adventure, and in the Field Ward Preacher; Daz quietly recorded the chant, planning to bring it to the scholars of Candlekeep [ch 056 / 056.03].
- That evening Thorin sat near the sea — his first real exposure to it after months underground and in the mountains — reflecting with Dawnbringer, who lit up everything around them because she does not like the dark; Thorin concluded "Life takes you in weird directions and weird places. You just gotta go with it" [ch 056 / 056.03].
- Zalthir climbed to the inn's roof to meditate and found himself — perhaps not entirely disappointingly — at peace with nothing interrupting him [ch 056 / 056.03].
- The party arrived at Candlekeep the following morning, traveling the Way of the Lion along the cliffs above the Sea of Swords, with the keep visible from miles out [ch 056 / 056.04].
- As they approached Candlekeep, Daz's pressure headache grew sharper, intensifying with each step toward the keep [ch 056 / 056.04].
- The party was received by Bookwyrm, the First Reader, a dragonborn sage entirely unbothered by the jokes about her name; she assigned the party to mentors — Daz to Sister Yvenne, Zalthir to Brother Khell-Vire, Thorin to Master Archivist Philemon, and Gyrgum to Vareth [ch 056 / 056.04].
- The matter of the shrunken House T'sarran spy in the party's bag of holding was addressed — she had been discovered when the party had to open the bag at the gate to show Glabbagool [ch 056 / 056.04].
- The Gatewarden Kalan Strongbranch was summoned and explained Candlekeep's position on prisoners: "The bell tower has cells. Comfortable, localized anti-magic. They are very rarely used. Anyone we find guilty of a major offense is exiled rather than imprisoned. Which is to say, we hold prisoners. We do not keep them. If you give her to us, what you are giving us is a delay. We will not deliver to House T'sarran, and we will not execute her. Eventually, we will release her at our gate with a warning" — leaving the decision entirely to the party [ch 056 / 056.04].
- The miniature drow spy was furious, demanding to be restored to her normal size and formally imprisoned by Candlekeep's guards; when Thorin asked Glabbagool's opinion, Glabbagool explained he had been doing prison-watching for days, was "kind of bored," but that the spy provided entertainment — "She keeps trying to kill me! And then I slap her around, and then she tries to run away, but there's nowhere to run away to, so I just sort of expand, and then she's trapped, and then she starts screaming at me, and then I try to explain to her how the way of the shadow monk is to be quiet, and that she should learn meditation, and then she screams at me about the meditation, and then we talk about things. We've become great friends!" [ch 056 / 056.04].
- Glabbagool asked Zalthir whether watching the prisoner was pre-monk training; Zalthir confirmed it absolutely was, and Glabbagool took the task with utmost seriousness, fixing all six pseudo-eyes on the spy [ch 056 / 056.04].
- The party decided to keep the spy under Glabbagool's watch rather than hand her over to Candlekeep [ch 056 / 056.04].
- Zalthir was taken to the Echoing Hall within the Atheneum, where he met Brother Khell-Vire, a 200-year-old half-elf who had left the Garden of Shadow eighty years ago after refusing an order, wearing a faded shadow monk under-robe beneath the avowed gray of Candlekeep [ch 056 / 056.05].
- On Khell-Vire's desk lay a cryptic note reading "Zalthir, the Underdark, until the Bone King sings," the meaning of which neither of them could explain [ch 056 / 056.05].
- Khell-Vire challenged Zalthir with the cryptic question "What were you sent toward?" and rephrased it when Zalthir's patience showed its limits, but Zalthir found no meaning in it; Khell-Vire sent him to the Trial of the Broken Mirror, stating: "I will not be there. This is your work, not mine" [ch 056 / 056.05].
- In the Sea Warden's Tower sparring rooms, a blind half-elf monk — Master Kenshi — tested Zalthir's shadow stepping, criticizing his form — "You skip the third breath. You don't enter your ki in the hara. You let it bleed into your fingertips like a poison. It is technically wrong in every scroll" — but noting Zalthir arrived "three seconds faster than the grandmaster" [ch 056 / 056.05].
- Zalthir failed every formal demonstration — acrobatics (3), athletics (1), and stealth (11 total) — causing the monk to nearly dismiss him and walk away in disappointment, at which point Zalthir landed a powerful hit on the departing monk from behind (rolling 30 to hit), prompting "Perhaps not all is lost" [ch 056 / 056.05].
- The monk set a final challenge — shadow stepping to intercept and smash a thrown brick mid-air; Zalthir succeeded (rolling 29 to hit), shattering the brick completely, and the monk declared the combination of shadow step and strike "redeems your lack of skills in all other possible areas" [ch 056 / 056.05].
- Zalthir demonstrated his shadow tentacles and eldritch tattoo, which the monk found less impressive, but agreed that combining those elements in an interesting way was the next frontier [ch 056 / 056.05].
- A sage of xenobiology asked whether "a mindless digestive system" could achieve enlightenment; Zalthir defended Glabbagool's intelligence and progress with conviction, noting he was by no means mindless, and the sage provided a vial of Resonant Slime [ch 056 / 056.05].
- Master Kenshi ultimately acknowledged Zalthir's unconventional mastery and taught him one of three specialized shadow techniques; Zalthir chose option C [ch 056 / 056.05].
- Thorin was brought to Master Archivist Philemon, a clinical specialist in sentient artifacts who initially assessed Dawnbringer as "a remarkable specimen — late Netherese-era recovery, sun-worship origins, sustained darkness exposure of approximately 3,000 to 4,000 years" [ch 056 / 056.06].
- Thorin corrected Philemon immediately — "What do you mean, specimen? This is my friend Dawnbringer" — and Philemon adjusted, noting with some surprise that the bond between Thorin and the blade was stronger than he had feared [ch 056 / 056.06].
- When Philemon asked Dawnbringer whether the corpse she was found with was her wielder, the sword faltered and went silent [ch 056 / 056.06].
- Daz was taken to the Hall of Divination, where spectral mage hands drifted through the air fetching tomes, and met Sister Yvenne, a meticulous and authoritative keeper of the public record [ch 056 / 056.07].
- Yvenne explained that information in Candlekeep was exchanged reciprocally, and Daz told her everything: the amnesia, the jail, the Underdark, the mercenaries trying to protect him and the ones trying to kill him, and the headache growing worse since he arrived — leaving Yvenne visibly surprised by the totality of his disclosure [ch 056 / 056.07].
- Yvenne explained drow noble house politics — matron mothers, the rule of intrigue, that betrayal was upward-rewarded if it strengthened Lolth's web, and the rule that if even one member of a targeted house survived, the attacking house was forfeit and destroyed — illustrated by a story of a house that left "some blithering child idiot alive," after which the drow community tore the attacking house to the ground, leaving a black puddle; Yvenne did not know what happened to the surviving child [ch 056 / 056.07].
- Yvenne read Daz a list of houses extinct within living memory — the second seat, the fourth seat marked "struck," the eleventh seat, and the twenty-third seat; Daz's natural-20 investigation check noticed the word "struck" was strange and out of place, implying political, paid censorship rather than elimination [ch 056 / 056.07].
- Yvenne admitted there was one house she could not speak of in the room: "There is one house I cannot speak of in this room, not because I will not, because the public record is sealed, and I am today the public record. If you want what is behind the seals, you will have to want it through other doors" [ch 056 / 056.07].
- Yvenne noted the fourth seat held ritual archive privileges in Menzoberranzan, making a struck-fourth seat "a very political, expensive erasure" [ch 056 / 056.07].
- In a 200-year-old heraldry book — Geography and Cities, Menzoberranzan, Pillars of Woe — Daz discovered an entire appendix had been mechanically removed with a razor at least fifty years ago; a surviving footnote referenced the name "Daz'issin," which Yvenne explained was "a softening name. Mothers use it for boys they wanted to live," and the houses that had used it "most of them, did not last" [ch 056 / 056.07].
- Yvenne stated "It is possible your name is related, but it is unlikely. The houses that had those names have all gone," and Daz pushed back quietly: "But what if they weren't successfully all gone?" — sensing through Yvenne's careful silences and precise body language that he was close to something she could not say aloud [ch 056 / 056.07].
- Brother Vareth led Gyrgum through the West Inner Ward to the Immortal Chambers, Candlekeep's theology library of rectangular buildings with green roofs and a tile floor bearing one deity symbol per tile; Gyrgum followed Vareth's footsteps to avoid stepping on specific deity tiles [ch 057 / 057.01; ch 057 / locations].
- Pizwog, an ancient tortle librarian, shuffled past at approximately one mile per hour and pressed his own tract, *On the Necessary Absence of the Gods Who Are Present*, into Gyrgum's hands; Vareth looked irritated but respectful during the roughly five-minute handoff, and Gyrgum tucked it into his satchel promising to read it [ch 057 / 057.01; ch 057 / items].
- In Vareth's side office, Vareth laid out the three dominant schools of thought on the Question of the Age — the Stroudite school (mortal choice is sovereign), the Bahamutian school (divine providence prevails), and the Draconic school (ancient agency rules) [ch 057 / 057.01].
- Vareth reported the controversy had become serious: libraries, taverns, and chapter houses were arguing, courts of two cities had asked for written opinions, Stroudite pamphlets crossed the Sea of Swords, and three duels had broken out at Candlekeep itself — in a library where nobody had dueled in two hundred years — prompting Sister Yvenne to threaten confiscating everyone's bookplates [ch 057 / 057.01].
- Gyrgum proposed a fourth school of thought — fixed points where choice becomes constrained while remaining free between them — and Vareth, electrified, labeled it the "strong-weak hypothesis," calling Gyrgum a "weak Stroudite and strong Bahamutian around fixed points, and a weak Bahamutian outside of fixed points" [ch 057 / 057.01; ch 057 / moment].
- Vareth pressed a collection of Stroudite notes and papers into Gyrgum's hands and rushed off to begin his own research; Gyrgum promised to return a marked-up copy when done [ch 057 / 057.01; ch 057 / items].
- That evening the party dined at the Refectory, where the eight Great Readers sat at the high table; the party noticed one reader visibly drunk, another pale and unwell (Sylvira Savikas), and two locked in hushed conversation, and saw a hooded member of the Avowed hurry past with something hidden beneath their robes [ch 057 / 057.02; ch 057 / npcs].
- From the high gallery the party heard the recited prophecies of Alaundo — recited continuously, with each removed when fulfilled — and one line stood out: "On the longest night of winter's deepest cold, the keeper falls, the heart is sold" [ch 057 / 057.02; ch 057 / moment].
- Before dawn, Fembris Lancer, the First Reader's adjutant, banged on the party's door at the House of Rest and the Hearth, shouting that the Keeper of Tomes was dead and the First Reader wanted them through the Emerald Door before the Avowed awoke [ch 057 / 057.03].
- Glabbagool tucked himself into the bag of holding with eyeballs peering out as the party set off into snow-blanketed Candlekeep; Fembris led them past the Astronomicon, the Whispering Dome, and the Drakonoikos, the latter built from the scales and bones of the ancient red dragon Fyrentennimar and containing a dragon breath simulation chamber that had claimed one Avowed life and now required the Keeper's permission to enter [ch 057 / 057.04; ch 057 / locations].
- In the Chapter House the party found the Council of Twelve in uproar: A'lai Aivenmore shouted for a vote to restore leadership, a dowdy halfling reader urged determining whether Janussi had named a successor, a slender woman cried to find the murderer, a red-cheeked wizard watched shrewdly, Daral Yashenti demanded to know why Sylvira Savikas was absent, and a bronze dragonborn academic called for the Gatewarden's immediate dismissal [ch 057 / 057.05].
- Gyrgum derailed the crisis by introducing himself as founder of the "Gyrgumite School of thought," sparking academic curiosity among the council, until Bookwyrm, shaken and weary, restored order with "FOCUS, PEOPLE." [ch 057 / 057.05; ch 057 / moment].
- Daral dismissed the party contemptuously as sell-swords unknown from Alaundo; the party defended their credentials by recounting Underdark survival and the discovery of Dawnbringer, who spoke up herself to argue the party were the greatest discoverers for finding her two-thousand-year-imprisoned tomb [ch 057 / 057.05; ch 057 / items].
- Bookwyrm appointed the party as independent investigators citing impartiality given suspicious relationships within the council, and required them to sign confidentiality papers; Thorin's remuneration phrasing drew appreciative laughter, then stunned the council into silence [ch 057 / 057.05].
- After negotiation, Bookwyrm agreed that upon successful resolution each party member would receive a copy of any book in the library, made at the House of the Binder; Thorin's demand for carte blanche to go anywhere and read anything made the council deeply uncomfortable [ch 057 / 057.05; ch 057 / locations].
- Bookwyrm publicly stripped Gatewarden Kalan Strongbranch of the investigation in front of the full council, citing his troubled relationship with the victim; Kalan looked visibly pissed, recovered himself, bowed curtly, and stormed off [ch 057 / 057.05; ch 057 / moment].
- Bookwyrm explained Janussi had asked for Kalan's resignation the morning before the murder, concerned Kalan was losing mental faculties and becoming negligent; she declined to elaborate to avoid prejudicing the investigation [ch 057 / 057.05; ch 057 / 057.06].
- Bookwyrm escorted the party up the spiraling staircase past a shrine of Oghma and a large apartment with an ajar door; a gray cat lying asleep in the middle of the stairs opened her yellow eyes without otherwise moving, and even Bookwyrm stepped carefully around her [ch 057 / 057.06; ch 057 / locations].
- Bookwyrm admitted nobody had conclusively proven whether the cat was some power dwelling in Candlekeep or not, so it was left to sit where it wished; Fembris dismissed the Avowed's reverence as superstition until Zalthir quietly asked "Is it?" [ch 057 / 057.06; ch 057 / moment].
- Outside the chamber, the 23-year-old watcher Tadric provided a timeline: the body was discovered at 7:30 a.m. by A'lai Aivenmore and Miss Hollypocket; the Gatewarden arrived with Tadric around 8 a.m.; Kazryn Nyantani appeared weeping shortly after and viewed the body only from the doorway; Fheminor Scrivenbark arrived at 8:30 wanting to inspect but was turned away; Bookwyrm arrived at 9 from the baths; the Gatewarden returned at 11 for his own inspection, looking more determined on the way out than on the way in [ch 057 / 057.06; ch 057 / npcs].
- Tadric admitted he had not watched closely enough to know whether the Gatewarden touched anything inside, and defended Kalan as a great, honest man, troubled by his removal; when asked if he had done a good job, Tadric reflected: "I don't think I did a good job." [ch 057 / 057.06; ch 057 / npcs].
- Bookwyrm instructed the party to report back when done and remove nothing from the room, then departed; Fembris was officially assigned as adjutant and hastily qualified his offer of help as anything legal upon noticing the party's composition [ch 057 / 057.06].
- The party entered Janussi's chambers — a spacious circular room with a dozen lancet windows, hazy with lingering tobacco smoke; Janussi, a human male of around seventy, lay near the foot of the mahogany desk, white robes bloodied, rib cage caved in, and a gaping hollow where his heart had been [ch 057 / 057.07; ch 057 / npcs].
- The room was in disorder: a toppled chair, a shattered marble statue and pedestal, candles burned entirely to their stubs with a waxy mess below, and a spilled ink pot; near a safe on the far side, blue gemstones and empty pouches lay scattered as though the thief had found them worthless [ch 057 / 057.07; ch 057 / items].
- Fembris took one look at the body, sprinted to the nearest window, flung it open, and vomited outside the tower; a gust of wind flowed in but nothing disturbed the evidence, and he retreated outside [ch 057 / 057.07; ch 057 / moment].
- The party examined the desk: the spellbook The Discoveries lay open at the center with notes in Celestial on papyrus musing on unfamiliar spells; a quill with midnight blue ink lay on the floor, a damp linen ink carrier sat in a wicker basket, and atop the back pile of notes were annotations in the same blue ink on The Golden Ass, beneath which lay older notes in dry purple ink, suggesting Janussi had switched inks within the last day or so [ch 057 / 057.07; ch 057 / items].
- Zalthir's Medicine roll of 17 confirmed the rib cage was smashed by what appeared to be an axe or similar weapon in several precise blows close together, and only the heart was missing — no other organs — with blood surprisingly localized, suggesting Janussi may already have been dead before his chest was opened [ch 057 / 057.07].
- Gyrgum theorized the heart was forcibly removed after death to prevent *Raise Dead*, which requires vital organs; the party considered whether someone had already cast *Speak with Dead*, which would block further attempts for ten days, with the GM answering only "perhaps" [ch 057 / 057.07; ch 057 / spells].
- Thorin asked whether waiting ten days would allow another *Speak with Dead* attempt and whether any ice was available to preserve the body, as the corpse would deteriorate considerably by then; the session ended with the party mid-investigation [ch 057 / 057.07; ch 057 / spells].
