- The party investigated the body of Janussi, the Keeper of Tomes, in his chamber, finding his rib cage smashed by an axe-like weapon and his heart removed from his chest, with blood strangely localized around the wound [ch 058 / 058.01].
- Daz conducted a meticulous examination, uncovering clotted blood beneath the victim's nose and smeared ink stains along the right sleeve of his tunic [ch 058 / 058.01].
- Daz noticed a dark, blackened substance on Janussi's left forefinger, lower lip, and tongue, darker than the blue ink he used for notes; Glabbagool attempted to identify it using his knowledge of Underdark substances but failed his Intelligence check (6) [ch 058 / 058.01].
- The party theorized Janussi had been licking his finger to turn pages and ingested poison applied to a book [ch 058 / 058.01].
- Gyrgum's medicine check confirmed Janussi's nose was fractured and bruised before death, since bruising cannot occur after the heart stops, placing a violent altercation hours before the killing [ch 058 / 058.01].
- The party discovered a chipped front tooth, with the fragment found a few feet from the body, suggesting Janussi fell face-first upon death and was then rolled over by whoever removed his heart [ch 058 / 058.02].
- Daz identified three roughly circular impact wounds on the back of an armchair — two punched clean through, scattering horsehair stuffing — as the signature of magic missiles [ch 058 / 058.02].
- The party examined a wall-mounted safe left open, finding one sapphire and one scrap of parchment missing from among spell component pouches, while a fortune in diamonds, platinum, and gold was left untouched [ch 058 / 058.02].
- Beneath the victim's neck lay broken lead-chain links, remnants of a locket forcibly torn away [ch 058 / 058.02].
- The party found a silver pedestal with a finely carved ebony wood pipe and cherry-flavored tobacco, which may have been used to mask the scent of poison [ch 058 / 058.02].
- Tadric explained that a fallen three-foot marble statue of a hooded, bearded man holding a candle was likely knocked over by the Great Reader A'lai Aivenmore, who discovered the body at dawn [ch 058 / 058.02].
- Thorin's question about shaking the candle caused Tadric to become visibly distressed, fixating on the candle as if it might awaken the statue [ch 058 / 058.02].
- The party found a gift tag in the wastebasket written in a "sour, cramped hand" reading: "To Janussi, read it and weep, you old snob, a proper Deadwinter gift, your loving rival." [ch 058 / 058.02].
- On the desk, the party found two sets of notes written in blue ink — one annotating a comedic book called *The Golden Ass*, and another with musings on unfamiliar spells from *The Discoveries* [ch 058 / 058.03].
- The party realized *The Golden Ass* was missing from the room despite Janussi having recently taken notes from it [ch 058 / 058.03].
- Daz theorized the missing book might be a key to a cipher or code, possibly hiding arcane instructions within its jokes [ch 058 / 058.03].
- Zalthir noted the heavy tobacco smoke in the room might have been used to mask the scent of poison; the DM confirmed Janussi kept his chamber full of tobacco smoke despite periodically needing magical treatment for lung cancer [ch 058 / 058.03].
- Their assistant Fembris Lancer identified Daral Yashenti as Janussi's most bitter professional rival — the same man who had dismissively called the party "sell swords" upon their arrival [ch 058 / 058.04].
- The DM clarified that Fembris is a male half-elf assistant assigned to the party by Bookwyrm (spelled with a Y), the green dragonborn First Reader, and warned that Fembris will repeat everything the party says [ch 058 / 058.04].
- The party descended to the apartment of Hollypocket, the elderly gnome housekeeper, who received them with herbal tea and a flood of carefully observed gossip [ch 058 / 058.04].
- Hollypocket recounted that Kalan Strongbranch had stormed out of Janussi's office with a face like thunder, nearly knocking the tray from her hands [ch 058 / 058.05].
- Hollypocket recounted that Teles Ahvoste visited around the ninth bell, with the meeting descending into shouting, including Teles yelling "hypocrite!" before storming out [ch 058 / 058.05].
- Hollypocket recounted that Daral Yashenti visited around the eleventh bell while hungover, shouting that Janussi was "not fit to be Candlekeep's Keeper" and calling him "nothing but a glorified book peddler" [ch 058 / 058.05].
- Hollypocket recounted that Daral returned later that night, drunk, pounding on Janussi's door until admitted, then having another loud argument before fleeing down the stairs [ch 058 / 058.05].
- Hollypocket described a violent confrontation with the tiefling scholar Sylvira Savikas, whom she called the "devil woman," during which Sylvira screamed that Janussi was a traitor before storming out [ch 058 / 058.05].
- The DM clarified that "devil woman" is being used as a description of Sylvira's work studying demons and devils, not as an insult [ch 058 / 058.05].
- Hollypocket reported hearing a loud thud from the chamber above shortly after the midnight bells, but did not investigate as Janussi did not like to be disturbed after eleven [ch 058 / 058.05].
- Hollypocket claimed to have seen a vision of Sylvira Savikas's face glowing red in the candlelight around two or three in the morning, going downward — notable because Sylvira is a tiefling with darkvision and would have no need for a lantern [ch 058 / 058.05].
- Hollypocket added that Sylvira and Janussi had a volatile, cyclical romantic relationship — "the hookups were explosive, the breakups were explosive" [ch 058 / 058.05].
- Hollypocket confirmed Janussi always wore a locket on his person, never removed it, and it was "intimate" [ch 058 / 058.05].
- Daz used the Stonespeaker Crystal to communicate with Queenie, the tower's resident five-year-old female cat, while Zalthir and the others spoke through him [ch 058 / 058.06].
- Queenie revealed that Sylvira Savikas blasted an armchair with magic missiles on the morning of Janussi's final day during what appears to have been a lover's quarrel with him [ch 058 / 058.06].
- Queenie described seeing Fheminor Scrivenbark, the "short-legged one," arrive at the chamber in the evening, followed later by Daral, the "stinky breath one" [ch 058 / 058.06].
- Queenie recalled seeing Daral flee the room carrying a "hypnotic block," which the party identified as the missing book, *The Golden Ass* [ch 058 / 058.06].
- Queenie mentioned seeing a figure that looked like Sylvira leaving the tower going downstairs, but noted the person did not smell like her, suggesting a magical disguise or shapeshifter [ch 058 / 058.06].
- Fembris informed the party that Janussi possessed one of the two keys to the High Tower Library and that Kalan Strongbranch holds the other, and the two keys may be required together to access it [ch 058 / 058.06].
- Queenie ended the interview with a haughty dismissal: "You may now leave, and please give yourself a proper licking before returning to my tower." [ch 058 / 058.06].
- The party believed the locket torn from Janussi's neck contained one of the two keys to the High Tower Library, and suspected the murder, the missing book, sapphire, parchment, and locket were connected parts of a targeted scheme rather than an ordinary robbery [ch 058 / 058.06].
- Daz earned an Inspiration point for referencing Milo's Murder Mystery as in-world research that informed his investigation technique [ch 058 / moment].
- Gyrgum deduced that Janussi's nose was broken before he died, narrowing the window of the attack [ch 058 / moment].
- The party returned to the Great Chapter House and attempted to overhear Bookwyrm's conference with Kei Tigersteel, but nobody rolled above the perception threshold and they heard nothing [ch 059 / 059.01].
- Moon elf scribes moved to halt the party at the entrance before Bookwyrm waved them through [ch 059 / 059.01].
- Bookwyrm questioned the party about their investigation, probing "was there nobody that you wished to start your investigation with?"; the party shared only generalities, declining to name suspects [ch 059 / 059.01].
- Bookwyrm dismissed Kei Tigersteel, instructing him to take Janussi's body to the Shrine of Oghma for purification [ch 059 / 059.01; ch 059 / npcs].
- Bookwyrm led the party to Deneir's Sanctum as their private office, assigned Fembris Lancer to fetch suspects, and posted Vooshadi Moonriver to guard the door [ch 059 / 059.01].
- The party noticed Bookwyrm had a marked preference for them to speak with Sylvira Savikas, though she never said so directly [ch 059 / 059.01].
- The party decided to split up to cover more investigation ground, even though each member would have to roll alone [ch 059 / 059.01].
- The party reviewed the council meeting reactions of A'lai Aivenmore, Fheminor Scrivenbark, Kazryn Nyantani, and Alkrist, and noted Daral Yashenti was absent from the council, having been at the Hearth [ch 059 / 059.02].
- The party discussed the significance of the impersonation of Sylvira Savikas on the night of the crime, noting the impersonation makes it less likely Sylvira herself is the murderer [ch 059 / 059.02].
- A theory emerged that two separate actors may have been at the crime scene: one who knew where the locket-key was, and one who rifled the safe [ch 059 / 059.02].
- The party divided responsibilities: Gyrgum would research the poison in the library; others would pursue suspect interviews with Fheminor Scrivenbark, Daral Yashenti, and Kalan Strongbranch [ch 059 / 059.02].
- Kalan Strongbranch appeared in the corridor and pressed a small brass key into Gyrgum's hand, presenting it as one of the High Tower's two keys, saying he feared being targeted for it [ch 059 / 059.03].
- Kalan warned the party: "Whatever Bookwyrm is telling you, listen for what she isn't," and said that if he does not see them tomorrow, they should not look for him at his post [ch 059 / 059.03].
- Kalan showed the party his now-empty locket and confirmed both keys are required simultaneously to enter the High Tower; entry would be noticed even when the keys are elsewhere [ch 059 / 059.03].
- Kalan shared his forensic conclusions: Janussi was poisoned by something he touched or held in his left hand that interacted with his saliva; the heart was hacked out after death, most likely to prevent resurrection [ch 059 / 059.03].
- Kalan provisionally suggested Knock had opened the safe [ch 059 / 059.03].
- When the party challenged Kalan's single-culprit assumption with their two-actor theory, Kalan conceded: "Perhaps I was a little bit too hasty. Perhaps it was two different people" [ch 059 / 059.03].
- Kalan voiced suspicion that one of the Great Readers was the murderer and that Bookwyrm may have been manipulated into removing him from the case [ch 059 / 059.03].
- Kalan bluntly told the party: "I believe you're only being employed so you're likely to bungle the case, or else be silent if you do happen upon the right scent. You don't matter. You're outsiders" [ch 059 / 059.03].
- When the party revealed that someone had been impersonating Sylvira Savikas, a detail Kalan had not known, he conceded: "You are very good at this" [ch 059 / 059.03].
- Kalan and the party agreed to a collaboration framework; Gyrgum proposed dividing it into work streams, Kalan proposed five, and the exchange ended with a mutual commitment to "merge all the things" [ch 059 / 059.03].
- Kalan provided his timeline: 8 AM meeting with Janussi, 10:15 AM visit to Sylvira Savikas, then return to the Chamber of Lost Lore; on the murder day he arrived at the crime scene shortly after 8 AM, cordoned it off, posted a watcher, declared a state of emergency, and doubled the guard at the High Tower Library [ch 059 / 059.03].
- Kalan confirmed he has truth-detection spell capabilities and would consider deploying them under appropriate circumstances [ch 059 / 059.03].
- Kalan warned the party to be wary of shapeshifters and those who can change their face before departing [ch 059 / 059.03].
- Gyrgum, guided by Fembris, went to the Flora Theca and with a roll of 17 discovered the tome *Fey-mous Flowers & Fungi* by Archibald Roots, Jr. [ch 059 / 059.04].
- Gyrgum identified the poison as midnight tears: a highly toxic pale ochre dust from a Feywild plant, deliverable through food or liquid, with no effects before midnight and potentially lethal poison damage when it takes effect [ch 059 / 059.04].
- Gyrgum learned that sap from a particular Feywild tree neutralizes midnight tears and produces a powerful pain reliever [ch 059 / 059.04].
- Gyrgum researched the stolen sapphire and identified it as the costly component for Drawmij's Instant Summons, a spell that marks an object so it can later be recalled to the caster's hand [ch 059 / 059.04].
- The party theorized that the sapphire was connected to retrieving the stolen High Tower key; the DM noted the spell would not function if the object was being worn on a lanyard [ch 059 / 059.04; ch 059 / items].
- Fembris was sent to summon Daral Yashenti and returned with a two-word reply; Daral was not coming to them [ch 059 / 059.05].
- The party went to the Hearth and found Daral drunk, boisterous, and in conspicuously good spirits; Daz's drow instincts explained this as relief at not facing Underdark-style consequences [ch 059 / 059.05].
- Daral greeted the party with mocking toasts, including "Oh, look, it's Thorin's most famous detectives! Bookwyrm's Brown-Nosers!" and ordered spiced brandies at his own expense [ch 059 / 059.05].
- Daral admitted to his 11 AM meeting with Janussi where Janussi rejected his proposal to preserve poetry, intending to sell the works to fund ritual materials for Sylvira, "his horned tart" [ch 059 / 059.05].
- Daral admitted returning to Janussi's chambers that evening, finding him reading *The Golden Ass* — Daral's Deadwinter gift — and taking it back; "In defending myself, I may have caught him lightly on the nose" [ch 059 / 059.05].
- When the party pointed out the contradiction between the gift and Daral's anger, Daral replied: "I was drugged. I was upset. I don't know what I was thinking" [ch 059 / 059.05].
- Daral casually flipped through *The Golden Ass*, licking his fingers to turn each page, exposing himself to possible midnight tears poison in real time [ch 059 / 059.05].
- When the party warned him, Daral dropped the book in panic: "You've got to do something!" [ch 059 / 059.05].
- The party secured *The Golden Ass* as evidence and resolved to keep Daral close to test and possibly treat him before midnight [ch 059 / 059.05].
- In his rattled state, Daral named suspects: Teles Ahvoste, A'lai Aivenmore, Fheminor Scrivenbark, and Bookwyrm herself [ch 059 / 059.05].
- Daral made a disparaging remark about Bookwyrm's "lizard brain," prompting an indignant response from a party member on behalf of lizardfolk; Daral backpedaled, calling it barroom chatter [ch 059 / 059.05].
- Glabbagool went to the Whispering Dome with Zalthir following; he asked "What is the art of movement?" and went still for roughly sixty seconds [ch 059 / 059.06].
- Glabbagool answered his own contemplation: "The body that mimics nothing is already shaped by what it watches," and the monk smiled and nodded [ch 059 / 059.06].
- Glabbagool flowed up onto Zalthir's arm and declared a "Glabbagool-Zalthir combo action," formalizing his apprenticeship and combat partnership with Zalthir [ch 059 / 059.06].
- Glabbagool became an eighth-level sidekick capable of Shadow Step, flurry of blows, forcing acidic shadow-ki into wounds, and a corrosive embrace against an enemy grappled or restrained by Zalthir [ch 059 / 059.06].
- Glabbagool manifested several eyes on Zalthir's arm; Zalthir suggested he blend into his scales instead [ch 059 / 059.06].
- Glabbagool reminded Zalthir that the drow captive in the bag of holding still needs checking on [ch 059 / 059.06].
- A former shadow monk observing the exchange remarked dryly that Zalthir would have understood what the exchange meant if he had paid attention in training — which had apparently been the day Zalthir was asleep [ch 059 / 059.06].
- Brother Vareth unveiled a fourth theoretical option to the Gyrgumite triangle, turning it into a quadrangle and throwing Candlekeep's scholarly community into an academic frenzy [ch 060 / 060.01].
- The Avowed organized an emergency disputation in the Oval Theatre, and most scholars and Avowed abandoned their duties to attend, including Bookwyrm, Teles, Fheminor, and half the Avowed [ch 060 / 060.01].
- Fembris Lancer sheepishly asked the party for permission to attend the disputation, and his departure left the keep's corridors and sensitive areas unmonitored [ch 060 / 060.01].
- Daral clung to Thorin and Daz, having concluded they were the only two people in Candlekeep who did not actively want him dead [ch 060 / 060.01].
- Gyrgum encountered the party while traveling from the library and handed Daz the key previously worn around Kalan Strongbranch's neck, preferring not to carry it into a crowded room of Avowed [ch 060 / 060.01].
- The party discussed Midnight Tears: midnight is determined by the sun being directly opposite one's location, and escape strategies include continually traveling west, changing planes, or receiving *Lesser Restoration* [ch 060 / 060.01].
- The party noted why Midnight Tears appeals to drow: underground victims may have no way to know when local midnight will occur [ch 060 / 060.01].
- Daz cast *Identify* as a ritual on Kalan Strongbranch's key, confirming it was not linked to an instant-summoning spell [ch 060 / 060.02].
- The party clarified that the sapphire—not the summoned object—must remain with the caster to retrieve the linked item, and that a dead person counts as possessing nothing [ch 060 / 060.02].
- Thorin concluded that possession of the sapphire might function as a concealed proxy for possession of the key, provided the bearer knows the appropriate magic [ch 060 / 060.02].
- The party reconstructed the timeline of the poisoned book: Daral placed it wrapped beneath the evergreen tree in the Southern Dining Hall at approximately 11 a.m. on Deadwinter's Eve, and Kazryn Nyantani distributed it to Janussi after dinner [ch 060 / 060.03].
- The party determined the poison turns black only after activation by contact with a living humanoid, so unused residue would remain inconspicuous [ch 060 / 060.03].
- The party divided the work: Thorin and Daral questioned the dining-hall staff, Daz inspected the apothecary, and Zalthir and Glabbagool searched suspects' private chambers [ch 060 / 060.03].
- Daral argued that half the Great Readers would do anything to obtain Candlekeep's highest office, naming Teles, A'lai Aivenmore, Fheminor, and others among his suspicions [ch 060 / 060.04].
- Asked who would benefit from both Janussi's death and Daral's disgrace, Daral identified A'lai Aivenmore, who he believes wants to rule Candlekeep and sees Daral's love of poetry and fiction as a threat [ch 060 / 060.04].
- Thorin and Daral went to the Southern Dining Hall, where Thorin discovered a severed heart and a blood-stained meat cleaver hidden inside Milil's lead-lined brass chalice [ch 060 / 060.05; ch 060 / moment].
- The party identified the heart as one of the missing pieces of Janussi's body and inferred its removal was intended to interfere with resurrection or magical questioning [ch 060 / 060.05].
- The party noted the chalice's lead lining blocked divination such as *Locate Object*, confirming the killer's intimate knowledge of Candlekeep's artifacts and magical resources [ch 060 / 060.05].
- The waitstaff interviews revealed the Deadwinter supper began at the 5 p.m. bell with twelve dishes served on communal platters and shared carafes [ch 060 / 060.06].
- Irony, a junior Avowed with a compulsive need to tell the truth, recounted that Janussi praised Alkrist's proposed work *Tales of War by the Generals That Waged Them*, and that Daral muttered "Oh, you've been right behind one of us all night," after which Sylvira Savikas stormed out [ch 060 / 060.06].
- Irony described a suspicious encounter at approximately 1 a.m. when a figure resembling Sylvira Savikas searched the kitchens by candlelight, refused to speak by pointing at her throat, and accepted help finding a pot of honey [ch 060 / 060.06].
- The party noted two incongruities: Sylvira's voice was avoided entirely, and a tiefling with darkvision should not have needed a lantern, suggesting magical impersonation [ch 060 / 060.06; ch 060 / moment].
- Sprig Summerfoot disclosed that Irony had been fending off unwanted advances from Great Reader Teles Ahvoste, and recommended Orrin Glass as a witness [ch 060 / 060.07].
- Orrin Glass, deaf in one ear but highly observant, reported seeing Alkrist burn a small scrap of paper toward a candle when two junior Avowed arrived [ch 060 / 060.07].
- Orrin also noticed that the string around Janussi's gift had clearly been cut and retied, establishing the package had been tampered with while beneath the evergreen tree between 11 a.m. and the post-dinner gift distribution [ch 060 / 060.07].
- At the Oak Tree Apothecary, Daz discovered broken glass and a peppermint-covered rag below a shelf labeled "dangerous toxins" in Elvish [ch 060 / 060.08].
- Leuwin recalled Fheminor visiting at approximately 2:30 p.m. for cold medicine and A'lai arriving around 3 p.m. for fresh fennel, which led him to the greenhouse [ch 060 / 060.08].
- Daz cast *Speak with Animals* and, bribing the squirrel Nibbles with nuts, learned that a "huge lizard skin" entered the pharmacy while Leuwin was with A'lai, caused a bang, shattered the vial, and departed [ch 060 / 060.08; ch 060 / moment].
- Daz concluded that A'lai's request for fennel was a diversion allowing a Dragonborn accomplice to steal a dangerous toxin or precursor [ch 060 / 060.08].
- Zalthir and Glabbagool searched A'lai Aivenmore's chambers and found a cage sized for five messenger doves but only two birds, with signs three had been recently dispatched [ch 060 / 060.09].
- In Alkrist's chambers, Zalthir found sealed glass jars of dead rats with blistered and pustulated skin, a locked safe, and a copy of *1001 Tashalian Nights* bookmarked to a tale of a commander poisoned by Midnight Tears applied to a gifted horn [ch 060 / 060.09; ch 060 / moment].
- Zalthir's search of Bookwyrm's spacious chambers uncovered nothing significant, and Glabbagool detected no trace of poison there [ch 060 / 060.09].
- Gyrgum returned from a public lecture that had "taken everybody by storm" and was briefed on the murder investigation's progress; Daral, believing himself poisoned, lavished praise on Thorin, Dawnbringer, and Gyrgum in hopes of a Lesser Restoration [ch 061 / 061.01].
- Gyrgum confirmed he could cast Lesser Restoration but told Daral, "In due time, my dear. In due time," the party deliberately not rushing his salvation until his cooperation was secured [ch 061 / 061.01; ch 061 / npcs].
- Fembris Lancer arrived with his own murder-theory notes and asked Gyrgum to collaborate; Gyrgum directed him to deposit them with his secretary, Glabbagool, who declared them "not nutritious" and "not food" before reminiscing about cheese [ch 061 / 061.01].
- Fembris proposed a "duograph," then settled on acknowledgment in Gyrgum's monograph; Gyrgum conceded "There's always room in the acknowledgement section," and Fembris promised a draft by the next day [ch 061 / 061.01].
- The party debated whether the sapphire and locket were taken by different people, concluding possession of the locket protected it from being summoned away while its bearer lived, making the sapphire unnecessary to whoever already carried the locket [ch 061 / 061.01].
- The party reviewed open leads: Alkrist's unopened safe, the person who assumed Sylvira's appearance, Sylvira herself, Bookwyrm, and unexplored evidence in Janussi's chambers [ch 061 / 061.01].
- Glabbagool realized he could have eaten through Alkrist's safe, reminding Zalthir, "Walls don't stop us. I can eat through walls," while Zalthir noted he could have punched it open if stealth had not been required [ch 061 / 061.01].
- Clerk Elian delivered a paper championed by the recently fired Kalan Strongbranch, arguing that "Magic does not make evidence meaningful. It makes evidence adversarial," and claiming institutional pushback against rigorous murder investigation caused Kalan's firing [ch 061 / 061.02].
- Thorin raised the unreliability of Speak with Dead witnesses; Gyrgum noted the dead can sometimes be brought back, and Elian said the paper addressed that complication; Gyrgum falsely claimed authorship of the maxim "Once you have eliminated the impossible, whatever remains, however unlikely, must be the truth" [ch 061 / 061.02; ch 061 / 061.03].
- Elian proposed a monograph with Thorin, then settled on writing it himself with a Thorin introduction or endorsement, even suggesting a failed investigation could still support the paper if the party claimed the techniques would have solved the murder [ch 061 / 061.02; ch 061 / 061.03].
- The party split leads: Fembris left to find Kalan, Gyrgum headed to Janussi's chambers, and Daz agreed to interview Sylvira, with Gyrgum reminding the Alkrist interviewers to ask what Alkrist burned at the feast [ch 061 / 061.03].
- Gyrgum climbed to the third floor of Janussi's chambers, a "love nest," and found a note whose opening contained academic insults and showed Janussi regarded Kalan Strongbranch's proposed investigative controls as ridiculous and solipsistic, establishing another motive for murder [ch 061 / 061.04].
- Daz visited the ailing tiefling Sylvira, who was feverish with an abyssal plague in an oppressively hot room; she initially blamed Daral Yashenti, then dismissed him as too disorganized, and named Kazryn Nyantani, Janussi's discarded former lover, as a suspect [ch 061 / 061.05].
- Sylvira explained she was a convenient target because her tiefling ancestry and infernal-plane research made prejudiced people in the Overbright see an "abyssal spawn" [ch 061 / 061.05].
- Sylvira admitted Kalan had asked her to intercede with Janussi to save his position, but she never did because she agreed he was "a clown, a bore, and his research was third rate at best" [ch 061 / 061.05].
- Sylvira recounted leaving the feast early, secretly visiting her son to coax him back into the Mordenkainen's Magnificent Mansion she built for his protection, and returning to her chambers between 12:30 and 1:00 a.m. seeing no one afterward [ch 061 / 061.05].
- Sylvira categorically denied being the double seen near the kitchen, exclaiming, "Honey? I would never drink honey. What kind of nonsense is this?" [ch 061 / 061.05].
- Sylvira named Teles Ahvoste, hostile toward Janussi for nearly twenty years after being passed over for Keeper of Tomes, and identified Bookwyrm as the most forceful opponent of Kalan's investigative reforms [ch 061 / 061.05].
- Gyrgum recovered the discarded wrapping paper from the dining-hall trash, reassembled it with Mending, and discovered a second deliberate cut confirming someone had opened Daral's gift, tampered with the book, and carefully re-wrapped it before Janussi received it [ch 061 / 061.06].
- Thorin and Zalthir interrogated Alkrist in his chambers while concealed Kalan Strongbranch cast Zone of Truth; Alkrist, a thirty-three-year-old bronze dragonborn "tech bro," failed his saving throw and was compelled to confess [ch 061 / 061.07].
- Alkrist admitted Janussi told Bookwyrm she would not be his successor and that he expected A'lai to receive the position; Bookwyrm was distraught and felt betrayed [ch 061 / 061.07].
- Alkrist recounted his 9:30 a.m. annual review where Janussi denied his leadership potential and ordered his germ-warfare research terminated as dangerous; Deadwinter was revealed to be Candlekeep's annual-review day when budgets, research directions, and careers were set [ch 061 / 061.07].
- Alkrist admitted meeting A'lai at the baths at approximately 11:45 a.m., where "We were discussing how to deal with our mutual problem of Janussi," and confessed to proposing the method from *1001 Tashalian Nights* [ch 061 / 061.07].
- Zalthir opened Alkrist's copy of *1001 Tashalian Nights* to the bookmarked passage describing murder with Midnight Tears [ch 061 / 061.07].
- Alkrist confessed he and A'lai went to the Oak Tree Apothecary, where A'lai distracted Leuwin while Alkrist stole a vial from a high shelf and spilled ground peppermint; he then opened Daral's gift at the Deadwinter Tree, coated the book with Midnight Tears, and re-wrapped it [ch 061 / 061.07].
- Alkrist tore the poison vial's label off and tied it to the back of the Deadwinter Tree as decoration, attempting to burn it with a candle until Irony and Orrin Glass arrived to prepare the supper table; Irony's basin of water let him wash possible poison from his hands [ch 061 / 061.07].
- Alkrist admitted visiting Bookwyrm after midnight with a bottle of fine wine and two glasses, telling her Janussi had been poisoned; she was appalled, they argued loudly, and she stormed out sometime between midnight and roughly 12:30 a.m. [ch 061 / 061.07; ch 061 / 061.08].
- When asked when he tore out Janussi's heart, Alkrist reacted with genuine shock — "Ripped out his heart? What? His heart was ripped out?" — separating the poisoning from the later mutilation and theft [ch 061 / 061.07; ch 061 / 061.08].
- Alkrist said obtaining powerful transformation scrolls at Candlekeep was simple: "finding the right goblin and the right Avowed to retrieve a scroll" [ch 061 / 061.07; ch 061 / 061.08].
- The party concluded at least three people may have participated in the broader events, that whoever hid the heart in the chalice knew its concealment properties, and that Janussi had high intelligence but not high wisdom [ch 061 / 061.07; ch 061 / 061.08].
- The party weighed the disposition of evidence: Thorin proposed giving it to Kalan Strongbranch to "clean house," Gyrgum raised blackmailing conspirators, Daz sought A'lai's Underdark knowledge, and the party considered demanding a rare book per suspect from Candlekeep [ch 061 / 061.08].
- The party debated handing the murder case to Kalan Strongbranch, confronting A'lai, or going directly to Bookwyrm, settling on letting Kalan take the lead while protecting their own access to the reward [ch 062 / 062.01].
- Dawnbringer lamented that the promised therapy with Candlekeep's scholars had stalled after a productive first session, and Thorin agreed they had been making progress [ch 062 / 062.01].
- Kalan argued that the evidence (a confession, a heart, and a cleaver) did not place Bookwyrm at the scene and insisted on patience, predicting Teles Ahvoste or Fheminor would be elevated at the naming ceremony [ch 062 / 062.02].
- The party agreed to use their reward to obtain a book or resource capable of proving Bookwyrm's role as an accomplice later, and Kalan promised to identify a suitable book before they left [ch 062 / 062.02].
- Fembris Lancer returned to report that Bookwyrm wished to see them, and the party was received in her office with tea already poured [ch 062 / 062.03].
- Thorin refused the tea and produced his own flask, while Zalthir directed Glabbagool to absorb the tea through his arm, which the ooze burned away with acid [ch 062 / 062.03].
- Bookwyrm dismissed Alkrist's confession as a borrowed compulsion spell, but Kalan countered with the supporting evidence; Daz's scrutiny revealed Bookwyrm was not genuinely sad but curiously relieved [ch 062 / 062.03].
- The party withheld Alkrist's accusation that A'lai was the mastermind, telling Bookwyrm only that accomplices were still being investigated [ch 062 / 062.03].
- Bookwyrm praised the party, publicly credited their work with proving Kalan's methodology, apologized to him, and offered to restore him to his former position, which he accepted [ch 062 / 062.03].
- At the party's request, Kalan confirmed Alkrist would be imprisoned and his safe guarded until it could be opened [ch 062 / 062.03].
- Bookwyrm ordered Kalan to escort the party to Deneir's Sanctum and later to secured chambers, describing it as protection; Zalthir asked if they were under house arrest, which Bookwyrm denied [ch 062 / 062.03].
- Zalthir read that Bookwyrm was sincerely worried about their safety, while Daz picked up that she was also pleased to keep them contained [ch 062 / 062.03].
- A search of Alkrist's safe produced a potion of flying, a potion of superior healing, 750 gold pieces including a coin bearing a literal red herring, and nothing that directly implicated Alkrist or revealed accomplices [ch 062 / 062.04].
- Zalthir used Glabbagool to surreptitiously pocket the potion of flying, and no one noticed [ch 062 / 062.04].
- A Locate Object attempt to find the sapphire on Bookwyrm failed, indicating she did not have it with her [ch 062 / 062.04].
- Daral, still suffering from his suspected poisoning, pleaded with Dawnbringer to cure him [ch 062 / 062.04].
- The party was escorted to Deneir's Sanctum to wait under guard [ch 062 / 062.04].
- In Deneir's Sanctum, Fembris remained with the party as part of his punishment, and food arrived around evening; the party let Fembris eat first and jokingly adopted him as their official taster [ch 062 / 062.05].
- The party concluded that Daral probably had no role in the murder and allowed Dawnbringer to cure him; Daral announced he would make sentient magical swords a major subject of future research [ch 062 / 062.05].
- Daral attempted to leave once cured but discovered the door remained locked [ch 062 / 062.05].
- Three helmed horrors flew to Deneir's Sanctum, smashed through its windows and door, and attacked, converging almost entirely on Daz [ch 062 / 062.06].
- Daz opened with three scorching rays dealing 24 fire damage to one horror, while another horror battered Zalthir and a third joined the attack on Daz [ch 062 / 062.06].
- The horrors nearly killed Daz, one sequence of blows dealing 32 damage and leaving him at 3 hit points [ch 062 / 062.06].
- Daz realized the horrors were tracking the mysterious key he carried, and when he passed it back to Gyrgum, the constructs redirected their efforts toward him [ch 062 / 062.06].
- Zalthir grappled his targets while Glabbagool used Corrosive Embrace to eat through the horrors' plating, and Gyrgum released a Word of Radiance searing two horrors at once [ch 062 / 062.06].
- Thorin used a sapping blow on one horror, and when that construct attacked Gyrgum instead of him, Thorin retaliated immediately and destroyed it [ch 062 / 062.06].
- Zalthir and Glabbagool brought down another horror, and Thorin delivered the final pair of strikes, reducing the last one to rust and broken armor [ch 062 / 062.06].
- The party stood amid the wreckage unsettled: the constructs could not speak, could not be interrogated, and had known exactly where to find the party and who held the key [ch 062 / 062.06].
- The party wondered whether a magical technique could trace the constructs back to their creator or use them as a divining rod to find the other key [ch 062 / 062.06].
- The party stood among the wreckage of three destroyed helmed horrors in Deneir's Sanctum after the constructs were defeated; suspicion settled on Fembris as the only witness to the key's transfer from Gyrgum to Daz [ch 063 / 063.01].
- Daral initially rushed toward the door the horrors had smashed open, then stopped when he realized the only four people who did not seem to want him dead were inside; he asked, "What do we do next, gentlemen?" [ch 063 / 063.01].
- The group reviewed known facts: Alkrist killed Janussi, Bookwyrm covered it up, A'lai Aivenmore was involved, and constructs had invaded Candlekeep to seize a key [ch 063 / 063.01].
- Gyrgum recalled Kalan's exact words when handing over the key: "I have given it now to someone who is not me," and wondered whether carrying the key was a "hot potato" drawing danger to its holder [ch 063 / 063.01].
- Gyrgum showed the key to Daral, who admitted, "Well, I'm not a locksmith, so I couldn't tell you for sure," but insisted the true keys were deeply magical artifacts [ch 063 / 063.01].
- Daral read a scroll of Detect Magic; all magical equipment in the room glowed — with Dawnbringer shining more brightly than usual — but the key remained dark, leading Daral to declare, "This is not the key. This is not the key." [ch 063 / 063.01].
- A Watcher's bell began tolling an institutional alarm pattern, and an Avowed messenger rushed in announcing, "Bookwyrm is dead," her throat torn apart but her heart left in place; Gyrgum replied to the messenger's disbelief, "Well, reality disagrees." [ch 063 / 063.01].
- The party raced to Bookwyrm's office with Daral and Fembris, finding her throat ripped open by claws and teeth — not the surgical precision of Janussi's death — and a half-finished note on her desk: *He is using the beast to—* [ch 063 / 063.02].
- Daral denied knowing what "the beast" meant; Daz, with Gyrgum's assistance, felt Candlekeep's wards were subtly wrong, comparing the sensation to a hacked firewall with another port opened [ch 063 / 063.02].
- Daz recalled that Sylvira had an abyss-born son kept in a magical sanctuary, who had been outside it for some time; the party connected the creature to abyssal madness and A'lai's interest in the keys as a possible directing motive [ch 063 / 063.02].
- Thorin briefly argued the party was hired for one murder and should renegotiate fees for a second, then agreed the crimes were likely connected and not to be distracted [ch 063 / 063.02].
- Daral explained Candlekeep's anti-teleportation ward also suppressed fire, with its source in the high tower room locked by the two keys; Gyrgum tested it with Thaumaturgy and the flame evaporated upon attempting to ignite paper, confirming the ward still held [ch 063 / 063.02].
- The party concluded Bookwyrm likely possessed one real key and was murdered for it, connecting the theft to the simultaneous helmed-horror attack on Daz [ch 063 / 063.02].
- Daz recalled Kalan's warning, "If I don't see you tomorrow, do not look for me at my post," and Daral deduced Kalan was hidden among the Watchers in the Sea Warden's Tower [ch 063 / 063.03].
- At the Sea Warden's Tower the party found Kalan buckling on his armor; he asked, "Gyrgum! Daz! I heard the bell. Is everything okay?" and was told Bookwyrm was dead [ch 063 / 063.03].
- Kalan first hoped the murder was an accidental act of madness by Sylvira's son, but after learning helmed horrors had deliberately attacked Daz for the other key, he exclaimed, "Gadzooks. Someone is definitely after the keys." [ch 063 / 063.03].
- Kalan admitted the key he had given Gyrgum was a fake decoy; the genuine artifact had been entrusted to Tadric, posted alone in the North Galleries without knowing what he carried [ch 063 / 063.03].
- Kalan declared, "I hereby deputize you as Watchers of Candlekeep," at which the party asked about privileges and stipend; he offered a small badge and "truly an honorarium," then became exasperated they were discussing pay with Bookwyrm dead [ch 063 / 063.03].
- Pressed about how the conspirators knew the key's location, Fembris admitted A'lai Aivenmore had been in the room when he reported the transfer to Bookwyrm; Thorin warned him, "Loose lips sink ships, dumbass." [ch 063 / 063.03].
- Kalan congratulated the party for "two of the pillars of the thrice fold proof," while Thorin preferred "three strikes are out" and Kalan suggested "three slashes, perhaps" [ch 063 / 063.03].
- Kalan handed the party a Watcher's stair key as a shortcut to the North Gallery; when Thorin asked, "Is this key for real this time? Are you just fucking with us again?" and Kalan answered, "On my honor," Thorin replied, "And the other one wasn't?" [ch 063 / 063.04].
- Kalan, older and out of shape, fell behind huffing during the run, defending his deception: "Just for the record, I figured that if they saw you with the key, it would be fake. The fake key would be safer. I couldn't trust you with a key of that importance." [ch 063 / 063.04].
- At the top of a domed rotunda in the North Galleries, the party found a massive creature — Moziqodo, Sylvira's abyssal spawn son — having leapt onto Tadric, grappling and tearing him apart [ch 063 / 063.04; ch 063 / 063.05].
- Thorin charged in first, planted himself between Moziqodo and everyone else with his Guardian stance, and struck repeatedly; Dawnbringer reminded him her damage was radiant, not fire, and he apologized: "I just, I was fooled for a second because you are a flaming sword, but you're right. You're a radiant sword." [ch 063 / 063.05].
- Thorin used a sapping blow to hinder Moziqodo's next attack and a Menacing Attack that left the fiend frightened of him; his attempt to knock it prone failed [ch 063 / 063.05].
- Zalthir struck twice with magically enhanced fists, spent a focus point on a Stunning Strike that left Moziqodo completely helpless, then spent another point for two more devastating attacks [ch 063 / 063.05].
- Gyrgum repositioned to keep allies clear of his javelin of lightning's line of effect, but the throw narrowly missed Moziqodo [ch 063 / 063.05].
- Daz, reasoning fire would be ineffective against an abyssal creature, cast Magic Missile at first level, striking the stunned creature with three unerring bolts for twelve points of force damage [ch 063 / 063.05].
- Moziqodo never recovered from the stun and never took an action; Thorin's critical radiant blow from Dawnbringer killed the demon spawn outright, with the Dungeon Master confirming, "You killed him. You killed him." [ch 063 / 063.05].
- Gyrgum cast Cure Wounds on Tadric; a single casting closed his lacerations and stopped the bleeding [ch 063 / 063.05; ch 063 / 063.06].
- Tadric clutched the key and stared at the corpse, identifying the attacker as Sylvira's son and asking what had happened; told of the conspiracy, he sat down in shock [ch 063 / 063.05; ch 063 / 063.06].
- Tadric asked whether Kalan was alive; the party reported he had been alive but breathing heavily when they left him, joking he might have suffered a coronary [ch 063 / 063.06].
- The party deduced A'lai Aivenmore likely held the key stolen from Bookwyrm and was probably already at the high tower waiting for Moziqodo to arrive with Tadric's key [ch 063 / 063.06].
- The party discussed using the fake key decoy to deceive anyone who did not know which key was genuine, and Daz observed that claiming to hold two keys could expose whoever already knew the location of the missing one [ch 063 / 063.06].
- The party resolved to head toward the high tower, recover the second key, and finalize deferred negotiations with Candlekeep's leadership; Daz joked that obtaining both keys would mean they owned Candlekeep [ch 063 / 063.06].
- The party took a short rest beside the healed Tadric; Daz struggled briefly with the interface for spending hit dice before restoring himself after being badly hurt [ch 064 / 064.01].
- Tadric surrendered the genuine High Tower key but refused to withdraw, saying, "I held it all night and I didn't know what it was. Now that I know what it is, I'm not giving it to you and going to bed" [ch 064 / 064.01].
- Tadric insisted on accompanying the party, declaring, "I faced Moziqodo," and Gyrgum relented, noting he now possessed resurrection magic "if worst comes to worst" [ch 064 / 064.01].
- The endless chant of prophecies at Candlekeep abruptly fell completely silent; Tadric was stunned because the chanting never stops [ch 064 / 064.01].
- Tadric urged the party, "Sylvira, someone needs to tell Sylvira that her son is dead"; the party deferred the task as a later problem for the Ember Grapple [ch 064 / 064.01].
- Gyrgum cast Bless on the companions present before departing; Tadric led the party toward the High Tower [ch 064 / 064.02].
- The party discovered a dead Watcher on an indoor walkway, killed by a crossbow bolt with fletching foreign to the region and inconsistent with Watchers' ammunition, proving outside attackers; Gyrgum estimated the kill occurred within the last half hour [ch 064 / 064.02].
- The party arrived at the High Tower lobby and found two Zhentarim raiders looting books according to a written manifest; one carried the bow that killed the Watcher [ch 064 / 064.03].
- A'lai Aivenmore waited on a landing fifteen feet above, near the sealed inner door requiring both High Tower keys, expecting Moziqodo to arrive [ch 064 / 064.03].
- Gyrgum's surprise Guiding Bolt at A'lai failed to penetrate his Mage Armor [ch 064 / 064.04].
- Zalthir and Glabbagool surged into the chamber; Zalthir struck A'lai twice for twenty-two points of damage [ch 064 / 064.04].
- Daz cast Maximilian's Earthen Grasp; A'lai countered the first casting, but Daz cast it again ("I'm persistent"), A'lai failed the save, took fifteen points of damage, and was restrained [ch 064 / 064.04].
- A'lai escaped the restraint by teleporting with Misty Step, which consumed his spell and prevented a more devastating response that turn [ch 064 / 064.04].
- A'lai's Arcane Bursts hit Daz for twenty-six points and Zalthir for twenty-nine [ch 064 / 064.04].
- A'lai paused combat to compliment the party and, when Gyrgum asked about the motive, revealed Janussi had given him ten days to surrender his chair, and that he would "do a great deal to keep it" [ch 064 / 064.04].
- A'lai confessed to serving Manshoon for eleven years, removing books from lists delivered by a courier with a metal right hand under a glove [ch 064 / 064.04].
- The party's history checks identified Manshoon as the wizard tied to the Zhentarim, the Moonsea, and Zhentil Keep, who has died more than once [ch 064 / 064.04].
- A'lai produced the sapphire and attempted to smash it, but Daz used Telekinesis to wrench it from his hand, then feigned retreat and secretly passed it to Gyrgum with a sleight-of-hand result of nineteen [ch 064 / 064.04].
- Robbed of the sapphire, A'lai turned four Arcane Burst attacks against Zalthir, knocking the monk unconscious [ch 064 / 064.04].
- Glabbagool settled protectively over the fallen Zalthir, positioned to stabilize him [ch 064 / 064.04].
- Thorin engaged the Zhentarim raiders with Dawnbringer, dealing thirteen points plus three on a Menacing Attack, but the thug resisted the intimidation [ch 064 / 064.04].
- Gyrgum cast Tasha's Caustic Brew through both raiders; both failed their saves, one took eight points of acid damage and died, the other took two and remained standing [ch 064 / 064.04].
- Casting Tasha's Caustic Brew ended Gyrgum's Bless concentration [ch 064 / 064.04].
- The session ended with one acid-covered raider standing, Zalthir unconscious, A'lai deprived of the sapphire but still active, and the sealed High Tower door unopened [ch 064 / 064.04].
- Daz's fireball detonated successfully on the High Tower walkway, dealing twenty-five points of fire damage and leaving the surviving acid-soaked Zhentarim thug alive by exactly one hit point; the blast's success proved that Candlekeep's legendary magical wards had been broken, sending screams, ringing bells, and panic through the keep while the Avowed began taking notes and preparing monographs on the unprecedented ward failure [ch 065 / 065.01; ch 065 / Memorable Moments].
- The acid-wounded Zhentarim thug continued to burn for eight more points of damage at the start of his turn; he chose to attack Gyrgum with his mace instead of scraping off the acid, and both attacks missed [ch 065 / 065.01].
- Gyrgum's earlier *bless* ended because he was concentrating on *Tasha's caustic brew* [ch 065 / 065.01].
- A'lai Aivenmore shouted to the surviving thug: "Don't go after Gyrgum. Don't go after the cleric. Go after the wizard. The wizard's the one that we want. He's got the key." He was wrong—Gyrgum quietly held the real key, the fake key, and the sapphire [ch 065 / 065.01].
- The thug broke away from Gyrgum and Thorin to pursue Daz, granting both an attack of opportunity; Gyrgum missed with his mace, but Thorin struck with Dawnbringer for nine points of damage [ch 065 / 065.01].
- Daz used Misty Step to reposition around the corner and cast *fireball* at the thug; after several virtual-dice failures, the damage resolved to twenty-five, and the thug survived his saving throw with one hit point [ch 065 / 065.01; ch 065 / Spells].
- Daz recognized the implication of the successful fireball: "Everybody is going to realize that the Candlekeep is now in its underwear" [ch 065 / 065.01; ch 065 / Memorable Moments].
- A Zhentarim assassin entered the room, walking past Daz and the thug while disengaging; the GM began narrating A'lai panicking at the sight of him, then retracted it: "Sorry, take it back. That's not what happens," a discarded narration Daz described as a premonition [ch 065 / 065.01].
- A'lai Aivenmore killed the remaining wounded thug himself, then proposed a pause carefully distinguished from capitulation: "I'd like to stop, but not surrender. I'm aware how that sounds" [ch 065 / 065.01; ch 065 / NPCs].
- Daz's investigation revealed A'lai was listening toward the stairwell rather than watching the party, as though waiting to hear who was coming—a frightened academic whose plan had escaped his control [ch 065 / 065.01].
- A'lai admitted his spells were nearly exhausted—"I have nothing left. I have nothing left. You will have probably counted how many spells I've casted"—and offered three pieces of intelligence: "But I know which two lines of the cryptogram matter. I know where the room under the keep is, and I know the name of the person in Menzoberranzan who has been paying to keep your drow breathing." Canon records this as an intuition about a *house*, not a name [ch 065 / 065.01; ch 065 / NPCs].
- A'lai asked to deliver the information "to somebody with a locked door," explaining: "Because in about four minutes, people will be coming up these stairs will be very disappointed in me, and you strike me as the more reasonable disappointment" [ch 065 / 065.01].
- A'lai hoped the party would persuade the Avowed to imprison him inside Candlekeep's magic-nullifying prison, the one place he believed he might survive what was coming, preferring a Candlekeep cell over expulsion or exposure outside its walls [ch 065 / 065.01; ch 065 / Locations].
- Gyrgum crossed to the fallen Zalthir and used *healing word* to restore him to consciousness; Zalthir had been reduced from fifty-seven hit points to zero by A'lai's earlier barrage [ch 065 / 065.01; ch 065 / Spells].
- The party debated A'lai's fate and decided to arrest him and turn him over to the Avowed's council; Gyrgum observed that losing his academic privileges—or even his library card—may be the cruelest punishment [ch 065 / 065.02].
- Zalthir used his Street Justice feat to bind the cooperative wizard, ensuring he could not easily escape [ch 065 / 065.02].
- The party remained suspicious of the restraints, recalling their standing expectation that "the statues always come alive and attack you" [ch 065 / 065.02].
- A Zhentarim assassin suddenly appeared, visibly dressed as a member of the organization; A'lai held out his bound hands and greeted him: "Took your time. They have had me since the landing," then recast his capture as positioning: "It's not a loss. It's a position. I've been in the room. I know exactly who is carrying what," nodding toward Daz [ch 065 / 065.02; ch 065 / Memorable Moments].
- The assassin attacked Daz, whom both A'lai and the assassin believed still carried the sapphire, striking twice for twenty and twenty-three points of damage and dropping Daz from thirty hit points to zero [ch 065 / 065.02].
- Thorin's guardian ability let him retaliate immediately, though his first attempted roll was corrupted by the malfunctioning virtual tabletop; the corrected attack dealt thirteen points of damage [ch 065 / 065.02].
- Daz made an immediate death save and succeeded with a seventeen, then suffered one failure, leaving him with one success and one failure [ch 065 / 065.02].
- Rather than spend his final attack elsewhere, the assassin searched Daz's unconscious body and, horrified, found the sapphire was not there—Daz had secretly passed it to Gyrgum; A'lai, realizing his deception failed and his rescuer could not secure the artifact, fell into genuine panic [ch 065 / 065.02].
- Thorin retaliated with Dawnbringer—first two blows dealing eight and fourteen points of damage, then Menacing Attack adding five points and forcing a failed Wisdom save that frightened the assassin, then Action Surge for another full attack action landing thirteen more points of damage, then a critical radiant strike inflicting eighteen points and leaving the assassin with five hit points, then Hill Strike for another 1d6 delivering the final blow and killing him; Thorin searched the body [ch 065 / 065.02; ch 065 / NPCs].
- Thorin's fury was immediate and absolute; after the kill he said, "Oh, I'm so badass," and "Like, that's what you get for taking out one of my…" [ch 065 / Memorable Moments].
- Gyrgum rushed to the fallen Daz, asked for his maximum hit points—fifty-six—and used a potent but unnamed restorative spell, restoring forty hit points and pulling him back from the edge of death [ch 065 / 065.02; ch 065 / Spells].
- A'lai looked on in tearful panic, his last hope of rescue lying dead at his feet and his attempted betrayal exposed; the party debated whether A'lai deserved justice, academic disgrace, or something more inventive [ch 065 / 065.02].
- Thorin suggested making the Zhentarim believe A'lai had become an informant, describing it as "hoist him by his own petard" and fitting his chaotic-neutral instincts [ch 065 / 065.02].
- Thorin suggested letting Glabbagool "have his way with him"; Glabbagool mused, "I've never eaten a vowed wizard" and "Hmm, do they dissolve like other people?" and A'lai responded, "Keep that thing away from me" [ch 065 / 065.02; ch 065 / Memorable Moments].
- A'lai began to cry; the party expressed no sympathy but kept him alive because his information remained valuable [ch 065 / 065.02].
- The party identified the nearby chamber as Candlekeep's security control room, containing magical artifacts associated with the keep's defensive systems, whose door normally required both real High Tower keys [ch 065 / 065.03; ch 065 / Locations].
- The party took A'lai's High Tower key to prevent the two real keys from being brought together; Thorin took the key, Gyrgum slipped it to Zalthir when nobody was looking, and it was ultimately tucked inside Glabbagool for safekeeping [ch 065 / 065.03].
- Glabbagool appeared visibly proud of his new responsibility, gaining roughly half a centimeter in height and becoming "a little less pancakey" [ch 065 / 065.03; ch 065 / Memorable Moments].
- Because slashing attacks can divide an ooze, the party briefly wondered whether splitting Glabbagool would produce two sentient Glabbagools or one nonsentient half; they declined to experiment on their friend [ch 065 / NPCs].
- The party decided to move A'lai out of the High Tower lobby before questioning him further; the GM confirmed, "And yes, as you walk out of the room, you made the right choice" [ch 065 / 065.03].
- Manshoon's simulacrum arrived moments later, conjuring a wall of force and shattering the magical door that the High Tower keys were meant to protect, breaching Candlekeep's inner sanctum through sheer, overwhelming power [ch 065 / 065.03; ch 065 / Spells].
- The simulacrum arrived alone and visibly weakened, having expended enormous magical resources and artifacts to penetrate Candlekeep, while the keep's senior defenders were dead, disgraced, captured, or missing; none of his usual goons could accompany him [ch 065 / 065.03].
- The GM clarified the keys still mattered: forcing entry cost Manshoon enormous magical power and prevented him from arriving at full strength with his retinue [ch 065 / 065.03].
- The party recognized the coming confrontation as the boss fight, but one they had materially reshaped: Manshoon's simulacrum is alone, weakened, and partially ignorant of his objective [ch 065 / 065.03].
- A'lai, trembling in the party's custody, revealed he had never told Manshoon the truth about what lies inside the depository or precisely what he must seek there; he intends to trade that secret for protection long enough for Candlekeep's wards to be restored [ch 065 / 065.03; ch 065 / NPCs].
- The party recognized they must keep A'lai safe from Manshoon and from Avowed seeking vengeance for Bookwyrm, Janussi, Moziqodo, and the other casualties of his conspiracy [ch 065 / 065.03].
- A'lai Aivenmore, in custody after Manshoon's simulacrum breached the inner sanctum, realized Manshoon had never come for him and expressed despair at his own insignificance — Manshoon had sent only a gloved, faceless courier for eleven years [ch 067 / 067.01; ch 067 / moments]
- Gyrgum summarized A'lai's predicament: "It's not even worth killing." The party joked he was not worth the price of a bullet or a spell slot [ch 067 / 067.01]
- The party identified Tadric the Watcher among those present in the High Tower aftermath, and recognized the race now: Manshoon was already pursuing whatever lies beneath Candlekeep [ch 067 / 067.01]
- A'lai revealed the existence of a secret room beneath Candlekeep containing artifacts of extraordinary power, guarded by a cryptogram he had researched for years, and provided two answers: "Fustilugs" (base insult carved into the black marble knight in the Philosopher's Court) and "Bow" (the reply of halfling bard Batbayar's statue when applauded) [ch 067 / 067.02]
- Gyrgum pressed A'lai on Manshoon's purpose and Daz asked who funded the forces around him; A'lai could not trace the payments, only the patience — he deduced only House Baenre in Menzoberranzan could afford to wait eleven years for nothing [ch 067 / 067.02; ch 067 / moments]
- Gyrgum appealed to Tadric, invoking the party's deputization by Kalan Strongbranch, their record of service (defeating Moziqodo, saving Tadric's life), and his founding of a new philosophy, jokingly negotiating a jointly authored monograph; Tadric agreed and marched A'lai toward the null magic prison [ch 067 / 067.02]
- The party regrouped in the investigator's office with the cryptogram transcribed from A'lai's photographic memory; Sylvira Savikas arrived, grateful and weakened by illness, unaware the party killed her son Moziqodo [ch 067 / 067.03]
- Sylvira provided the true name of Miirym the Sentinel Wyrm — **Vydykyq** — as the answer to the first riddle, learned during a discussion about demonic possession and true names [ch 067 / 067.03; ch 067 / npcs]
- Sylvira identified the Eastern Light of Mystra's Mantle — **Limniz** — as an obscure star on the star charts in the Orrery of the Astronomicon, and noted with unease that three people who did not work there had been inside the Orrery [ch 067 / 067.03]
- Thorin led the decoding using a cryptogram-solving site; Gyrgum independently reached nearly the same solution; the full text revealed the instructions to access the vault [ch 067 / 067.03; ch 067 / moments]
- Kalan Strongbranch arrived out of breath, eager to contribute a clue, only to find the cipher already solved; he recovered ("Yes, well, I'm relieved to see that") and identified the statue of Alaundo in Founders Court as the first destination [ch 067 / 067.03; ch 067 / moments]
- At the House of Alaundo, the party debated whether to offer ink, blood, or the poisoned ink; Gyrgum chose fresh ink ("I'm not actually evil, so I'm going to start with ink") [ch 067 / 067.04; ch 067 / moments]
- Pouring ink into the statue's empty ink pot made the brass statue slide forward on its dais, revealing a long, unlit staircase descending into darkness [ch 067 / 067.04]
- Thorin observed that no one in all the statue's existence had ever put ink in its pot; the assembled scholars recoiled at the idea of defacing Alaundo's monument, explaining why centuries of reverence concealed the mechanism [ch 067 / 067.04; ch 067 / moments]
- The party needed Alaundo's age at death; a scholar suggested consulting his grave, and the party learned from the keepers' grove that Alaundo died at ninety-seven [ch 067 / 067.04; ch 067 / 067.05]
- Scholars provided the text of the original prophecy, known by heart by every Candlekeep scholar; the party's goodwill made obtaining it easy [ch 067 / 067.05]
- The party recruited a little-known local-history scholar who was thrilled to accompany them and asked for time to collect personal items [ch 067 / 067.05]
- At the House of Mechanus, Spanner the gnome librarian explained dust of Mechanus is "the most perfect of all lubricants"; killing a modron for it was absolutely unacceptable, so he lent the party modron-made tools instead, on condition that Zalthir and Glabbagool later submit to a study of their mechanical symmetry [ch 067 / 067.05; ch 067 / npcs]
- Zalthir consulted Glabbagool and accepted, provided Spanner offered "constructive feedback, such that we may better symbiotically serve our Zen cause"; the interview was deferred until after the expedition [ch 067 / 067.05; ch 067 / moments]
- Spanner addressed the modrons in a mechanistic language; they deliberated through an eerie, synchronized dance of moving arms and bodies [ch 067 / 067.05]
- The party descended the hidden staircase counting toward the ninety-seventh step; Gyrgum planned to stand on it and recite the prophecy, with Zalthir joining him, Daz positioned higher for ranged line of sight, and Thorin near the front [ch 067 / 067.06]
- At Zalthir's request, Gyrgum healed him before the descent — Zalthir needed twenty-seven hit points but Gyrgum restored forty-four [ch 067 / 067.06]
- A ten-foot section of the staircase around the designated step hinged open into a vast circular well; Zalthir used strength and wings to hold companions, Daz cast **Telekinesis** to seize the collapsing stairs, and Gyrgum and Zalthir scrambled clear — but Thorin lost his footing and plummeted into the well, still in free fall when the session ended [ch 067 / 067.06; ch 067 / moments]
- Thorin fell through the hinged-open staircase trap triggered earlier and plunged into darkness below; Zalthir dived after him, realized out-flying a falling creature would not work because a falling creature dropped roughly five hundred feet per round, and instead plummeted to catch him [ch 068 / 068.01].
- Zalthir caught Thorin mid-air about five hundred feet below, and the two clung together while Glabbagool accompanied the fall, initially screaming "Wee!" and later advising, "One should always be Zen." [ch 068 / 068.01; ch 068 / moment].
- Dawnbringer reacted with "Not this again!", and Glabbagool wondered "Is this too much hugging?", to which Zalthir answered "There are worse things, Glabbagool." [ch 068 / 068.01; ch 068 / moment].
- Zalthir confirmed his fifth-level dragonborn ability allows him to fly, with a potion of flying as fallback [ch 068 / 068.01].
- Thorin shouted "I'm still kicking"; Zalthir decided they were already halfway down and might as well scout the bottom [ch 068 / 068.01].
- After another drop, Thorin and Zalthir entered a feather-fall zone and shouted for the others to follow; the rest leaped in one by one [ch 068 / 068.01].
- Gyrgum was reluctant—"Oh, God, that's going to be hard for me"—said a quick prayer, and jumped; Daz followed because he did not want to be separated from the party since holding the staircase up had not worked [ch 068 / 068.01].
- Edvaldo Sedanur accompanied the descent, announcing "I intend to write a very long monograph," and the entire party landed safely in a single tunnel roughly one thousand feet beneath the surface [ch 068 / 068.01].
- Daz is still holding the broken stairs in place with telekinesis as Thorin falls and Zalthir dives after him [ch 068 / moment].
- The tunnel led into a massive cylindrical cavern two hundred feet high and a hundred feet across, with a steaming lake of molten lava churning a hundred feet below the party's ledge [ch 068 / 068.02].
- A three-story stone tower rose from the lava on a thin pedestal of glassy black obsidian, similar in style to some of the older spires in Candlekeep [ch 068 / 068.02].
- Edvaldo identified it as a classic example of a style invented by a Candlekeep Avowed architect, noting many examples were destroyed by "needless rehabilitation and expansion" of towers [ch 068 / 068.02].
- Daz investigated the cogs and mechanical components embedded in the stone ledge and determined they formed a system for extending a bridge, also noticing a warning that the nearby airspace was protected by an anti-magic field [ch 068 / 068.02].
- The GM clarified the anti-magic field covers only the first ten feet near the bridge, not the entire thirty-foot gap [ch 068 / 068.02].
- The party used the specialized tools borrowed from Spanner to activate the mechanism, and the bridge ground outward across the molten lake to the tower [ch 068 / 068.02].
- The party crossed into the tower, whose unadorned stone walls held sconces lit with *Continual Flame* and four mounted crossbows guarding the interior [ch 068 / 068.02].
- Inside the first chamber, a tiny iron winged owlbear statue opened glowing white eyes, unfolded its wings, hovered twelve feet above the floor, and declared, "Halt, seekers," demanding three questions be answered and threatening to tear failed petitioners to shreds [ch 068 / 068.03].
- Daz answered the first question, identifying the Hearth as the only refuge for flames in Candlekeep [ch 068 / 068.03].
- Gyrgum answered the second question, naming Oghma, Deneir, and Milil as the holy triumvirate, barely resisting explaining their history [ch 068 / 068.03].
- For the final question, Edvaldo explained every Avowed was once expected to memorize the High Tower of Exaltation's height, a practice fallen out of fashion, and narrowed the answer to "over 500 feet" [ch 068 / 068.03].
- Thorin and Daz together determined the required answer to the tower-height question, and the guardian declared, "You have answered all three questions correctly. You may pass." [ch 068 / 068.03].
- The party briefly wondered whether they could defeat the guardian, deciding to save "doing something stupid for later" [ch 068 / 068.03].
- A cough sounded from the lava chamber before the guardian could answer what happens after its duty is completed [ch 068 / 068.03].
- A spectral silver dragon—Miirym, the Sentinel Wyrm, whose true name *Vydykyq* the party carries as a riddle answer—appeared, her ethereal form so insubstantial that molten rock flowed straight through her [ch 068 / 068.04; ch 068 / npcs].
- The dragon revealed "Manshoon entered my keep," that he remains somewhere inside, and Daz concluded "The protections fell when he entered." [ch 068 / 068.04].
- The dragon demanded the party identify the Reader and the witnesses; Gyrgum identified himself as the Reader and proved his knowledge of Candlekeep by describing the books and lore the party had investigated [ch 068 / 068.04].
- When asked where he stood "on the question of the age," Gyrgum answered, "I founded a new school that holds that there are certain fixed points among which we may make our own paths," and the dragon replied, "Why, wisdom, wisdom truly flows from Bahamut into you, cleric of Bahamut." [ch 068 / 068.04; ch 068 / moment].
- The dragon offered a boon; options discussed included holding the door so nothing leaves (which would keep Manshoon from teleporting away), revealing what he had taken, determining whether everyone present was who they claimed to be, or widening the anti-magic field [ch 068 / 068.04].
- The party asked whether she could fight beside them, and she refused: "I was bound to be witness, not weapon. If I could have stopped him, I would have stopped him at dawn." [ch 068 / 068.04].
- The party ultimately chose to have her widen the bridge's anti-magic field to thirty feet for ten minutes, banked in reserve until the party calls for it, with Zalthir requesting delayed activation; the dragon approved with a repeated "Well chosen." [ch 068 / 068.04].
- The party dropped the teleport-denial and the "what he took" boon options; the GM named the cost as they settled: Manshoon can still escape [ch 068 / 068.04].
- Heading back into the tower, the party noticed a damaged iron owlbear guardian lying on the floor—clear evidence that Manshoon had destroyed the previous guardian before a replacement took its place [ch 068 / 068.04; ch 068 / npcs].
- The party ascended to the vault, a plain circular room lit with *Continual Flame*, its windows sealed shut, with a single bookcase on the eastern wall and a desk and chair in the center [ch 068 / 068.05].
- A note in archaic Common explained the vault contained Candlekeep's most dangerous secrets, allowed consultation of what could benefit civilization but forbade removing the tomes, and warned that the chamber above held the means to destroy the collection if it ever fell into foul hands [ch 068 / 068.05].
- Approximately one hundred of the most powerful and dangerous magical tomes imaginable lined the shelves, and Manshoon—in truth one of his simulacra, though the party has not been told—sat calmly at the desk reading one without initially looking up [ch 068 / 068.05].
- Manshoon greeted them: "By all means, browse. The trick is leaving with what you came for. Did you come for the book or did you come for me?" [ch 068 / 068.05].
- Gyrgum answered, "Indeed, we came for you. We did not know of the books," adding "The books are but a bonus," and Manshoon prepared for battle [ch 068 / 068.05].
- Gyrgum proposed moving the fight to a lower chamber to protect the books, rolled 22 with advantage exactly as required to persuade Manshoon, and Manshoon agreed: "Yes, you're correct. We should not damage these books. They are of priceless in origin." [ch 068 / 068.05; ch 068 / moment].
- Gyrgum suggested they leave the books in place and come back after killing Manshoon; Manshoon supplied the alternative "Or I killed you," and Gyrgum conceded it was a possibility [ch 068 / 068.05].
- Manshoon sent the party down the stairs ahead of him and followed last, casting *Mirror Image* on himself so shimmering mirror images surrounded his form [ch 068 / 068.05].
- Manshoon stopped menacingly near Daz and announced, "Well, now we have found a place to have the magical duel." [ch 068 / 068.05].
- The iron owlbear guardian remained neutral, its assigned test already completed, possibly providing color commentary [ch 068 / 068.05].
- Daz opened hostilities with *Phantasmal Killer* cast at fourth level; Manshoon considered burning *Counterspell* and declined, then rolled an 8 on his Wisdom save, adding +9 to exactly meet Daz's DC 17 [ch 068 / 068.05].
- Under the standing PHB 2024 ruling, Manshoon took 13 psychic damage (half of 26), avoided the disadvantage rider, and the spell ended immediately [ch 068 / 068.05; ch 068 / spells].
- Daz then moved onto the bridge, inside the first ten feet of the active anti-magic band, ending his turn there unable to cast next round [ch 068 / 068.05].
- The session ended with Thorin about to take his turn as the battle with Manshoon began in earnest [ch 068 / 068.05].
- Combat resumed beside the basalt tower above the magma; Manshoon was thirteen points down from Daz's Phantasmal Killer and protected by Mirror Image, Daz stood at the start of the bridge's standing anti-magic band, and Edvaldo was nearby [ch 069 / 069.01].
- Thorin charged forward with Dawnbringer, landing a blow, but his second attack was diverted by one of Manshoon's Mirror Image duplicates [ch 069 / 069.01].
- Zalthir moved his magical darkness over Manshoon, activated his magical tattoo, manifested tendrils of eldritch darkness that struck, grappled, and dealt seventeen damage, then dragged Manshoon into the anti-magic barrier and battered him with further strikes including Flurry of Blows [ch 069 / 069.01].
- Manshoon used a legendary action to free himself and move twenty feet toward the tower; after clarifying Zalthir's movement, the party established Manshoon still had to run another thirty feet to escape [ch 069 / 069.01].
- Daz slashed the retreating Manshoon with a short sword for six damage (attack 21) [ch 069 / 069.01].
- Manshoon unleashed Ward Eater, a wave of magical energy that damaged and immobilized Thorin and Edvaldo (twenty-two points each) and restored twenty-five hit points to Manshoon; Zalthir and Daz were protected inside the anti-magic field, while Gyrgum took damage but was not immobilized [ch 069 / 069.01].
- Edvaldo survived Ward Eater and the subsequent Fireball despite accumulated damage of fifty-three points, when simple calculations suggested he should be dead [ch 069 / 069.01; ch 069 / 069.06].
- Gyrgum cast Spiritual Weapon at fifth level, striking Manshoon for nineteen damage (attack 28) and leaving the spectral sword hovering nearby, and called to Edvaldo, “Don't worry, if you die, I can bring you back.” [ch 069 / 069.01].
- Daz stepped out of the anti-magic field and cast a third-level Fireball shaped to catch Manshoon and Edvaldo but spare Gyrgum and Thorin; Manshoon saved for fifteen damage, leaving him at two hit points, while immobilized Edvaldo took the full blast [ch 069 / 069.01].
- Thorin, immobilized, hurled a javelin that would have finished Manshoon, but a remaining duplicate intervened; after considering throwing Dawnbringer ("I'm a sword, not a spear."), he threw a dart instead and missed [ch 069 / 069.01; ch 069 / moment].
- Manshoon used another legendary movement to position himself behind Edvaldo, using the researcher as cover; Edvaldo was “not very happy at all” about being used as a body shield [ch 069 / 069.01].
- Zalthir closed thirty feet and attacked; Manshoon cast Shield, raising his AC by five and turning the first hit into a miss, but Zalthir's follow-up attack landed and killed him [ch 069 / 069.02].
- The defeated Manshoon instantly reverted to snow and melted in the magma chamber's heat; an Arcana check confirmed the party had been fighting a simulacrum, a construct of ice and snow with Manshoon's statistics but only half his maximum vitality, unable to learn or regain spent spell slots [ch 069 / 069.02; ch 069 / moment].
- Daz responded simply, “Snow,” and Gyrgum asked, “Jeez, do we have to kill the real one?”; the party recalled that Manshoon was killed approximately 170 years ago but keeps returning [ch 069 / 069.02; ch 069 / moment].
- The Flying Iron Owlbear commented, “This was much better than the last one. The last one didn't even stop to say hello,” referring to the Manshoon duplicate just destroyed [ch 069 / 069.02; ch 069 / 069.04].
- The DM placed a snow-covered flagstone token where Manshoon fell [ch 069 / 069.02].
- Zalthir attacked and grappled Edvaldo, dragging him toward the edge of the walkway over the lava, and demanded, “Tell me the history of Candlekeep.” [ch 069 / 069.03; ch 069 / moment].
- Edvaldo protested his innocence (“What are you doing? I am Edvaldo! I am this lowly Candlekeep!”) and spoke fluently and at length about Candlekeep, including the Endless Chant and the Word Lattice; Daz's active investigation found the account convincing, but the party remained troubled by Edvaldo's earlier inability to state the tower's precise height [ch 069 / 069.03].
- Glabbagool earnestly asked why Zalthir had not simply thrown Manshoon into the lava; Zalthir explained that killing the wizard before he cast a single spell would not have been “sporty,” then told Glabbagool, “if there's an instant death trap you see that I forget about, feel free to remind me.” [ch 069 / 069.03; ch 069 / moment].
- The party heard faint speech drifting through the stone floor from upstairs, in a part of the tower where no one was expected to be [ch 069 / 069.03; ch 069 / 069.04].
- Gyrgum cast Locate Creature, specifying a Manshoon simulacrum (since Manshoon has been dead 170 years but keeps appearing), and the spell confirmed another duplicate was upstairs; Gyrgum reported, “Hey guys, there's a simulacrum of Manshoon upstairs,” and observed, “There must be a whole factory of these things.” [ch 069 / 069.04; ch 069 / moment].
- Edvaldo begged to be left out of another confrontation; after Gyrgum suggested leaving Glabbagool to watch him, Edvaldo negotiated second authorship on the eventual paper about the combat, which Zalthir agreed to on condition Edvaldo not resist [ch 069 / 069.04; ch 069 / moment].
- Zalthir thoroughly bound Edvaldo and left him subdued in the anti-magic zone; Glabbagool remained with the party rather than guarding Edvaldo, and was impressed by how quickly Zalthir bound the prisoner [ch 069 / 069.04].
- A disembodied magical voice spoke in prophecy cadence: “A dwarf will return to the Hall of Stone with the names of demons in her mouth," also referencing the lords of the dwarves, an unlit forge, a giant-raised dwarf, and a Shadow Walker; the party interpreted the returning dwarf as Eldeth, the giant-raised dwarf as Thorin, and the shadow-walker as Zalthir [ch 069 / 069.04; ch 069 / moment].
- Daz recognized the voice as conjured rather than human because of its slight fluctuation in timbre and lack of breathing between enunciations [ch 069 / 069.04].
- The party ascended into a plain circular chamber with Dawnbringer as the principal light; shelves along the eastern wall held roughly one hundred of the most dangerous books in the world, each showing the faint distortion of a magical glyph, with a desk and note in the middle and an open velvet tray holding ten prismatic gemstones, four of them dark [ch 069 / 069.05].
- At the far end stood a seamless door of black stone with no handle, keyhole, or visible seam, bearing eleven scorch marks in a rough circle at chest height from repeated failed attempts to force it open [ch 069 / 069.05].
- A second Manshoon, dressed in noticeably cheaper gray robes, stood waiting with his hands clasped behind his back; he warned that hundreds of Glyphs of Warding covered the books and an explosion could trigger a catastrophic cascade, noting he had solved part of the door's mystery but could do nothing with it [ch 069 / 069.05].
- A recorded echo of Alaundo's voice declared, “To the last door I gave no lock, for a lock is a promise that a thing can be stolen. I gave it a question instead." The party recalled the decoded instructions: those well-versed in Candlekeep's lore may fearlessly pass the Obsidian Door; the answer concerns what is consumed to make light, must be spoken in the dark, offered as a gift rather than a demand, and said only once; Gyrgum, as the verified reader, would need to speak it [ch 069 / 069.05; ch 069 / moment].
- The second Manshoon proposed a bargain, stressing it was not a threat: “If you want the dwarf's prophecy, I want a door. These are not the same object. Your cleric says one word, the door opens. You take the tray, I take the book." He lifted one of the prophetic stones during the negotiation, implying he could destroy the prophecies if the party refused [ch 069 / 069.05; ch 069 / moment].
- Manshoon concluded, “I would rather not do this the expensive way. I've been polite for 6 hours, and it has become something of a habit." The party debated accepting the bargain, opening the door and stopping Manshoon, or refusing and risking the prophecies [ch 069 / 069.05].
- Gyrgum suggested Edvaldo might know the answer; Manshoon eagerly agreed the party should bring him up, but Zalthir rejected this, suspecting Edvaldo might be the real Manshoon, so the party instead returned downstairs to question Edvaldo [ch 069 / 069.05].
